# Bug ledger — pharos baseline

Migrated verbatim from `SPEC.md` §B on 2026-07-27. Ids B1…B129 are stable; each
entry records cause + fix and links the invariant that now guards the class.
Append new bugs here. Next free id is one past the highest below.

```
id|date|cause|fix
B80|2026-07-16|BROWSER PLAYBACK (Lace incident) — the DirectPlay-verdict sibling of B77. A remote viewer (lace, desktop Linux Firefox, WAN 62.169.136.4 via prototype.aliflix) hung ~11 min loading Supergirl S01E05 (av1-in-Matroska). Loki: PlaybackInfo returned route:"direct" (SupportsDirectStream:true, NO TranscodingUrl); jellyfin-web silently retried PlaybackInfo ~4× over 11 min before falling back to h264-HLS on its own (no server-side 4xx/5xx — invisible). Root cause: `negotiate` returned DirectPlay for the av1/matroska source (client profile matched the container), and `direct_play`/`supports_direct_stream` were set true for ANY DirectPlay verdict — but pharos's `/stream` serves Matroska VERBATIM, relabelled `video/webm`, which a browser's `<video>`/MSE only plays when webm-legal (VP8/9/AV1 + Opus/Vorbis). B77 fixed the REMUX decisions (VideoRemux/AudioRemux) but left the DirectPlay-verdict path stranding a browser with no fallback URL. The B43 `force_webm` net that would have caught it was OFF for lace (config `linux_firefox_h264=true`, B60), and `source_firefox_native` (av1 IS firefox-native) additionally suppressed it. (Same session also hit an uncapped-WAN-transcode rebuffer on E04 + a 34 Mbps movie — separate root cause, B81.)|B80/V40. New `browser_matroska_direct_unplayable` gate: when a BROWSER (Mozilla UA) gets a DirectPlay verdict for a Matroska/MKV container (ffprobe "matroska"/"matroska,webm"; a genuine "webm" container is trusted — natively browser-playable + client-listed) that is NOT `webm_relabel_playable`, `playback_info` downgrades the Decision to a Transcode (h264/aac/ts, carrying the connection-aware ceiling) BEFORE force_webm/direct_play compute — so a TranscodingUrl is emitted, SupportsDirectStream resolves false, and the downstream HLS pipeline (keyed on the registered Decision) transcodes. A Linux-FF that can't decode h264 is still redirected to VP9 by force_webm, which now fires on the non-direct decision. `webm_relabel_playable` moved above the gate. Guard: browser_matroska_fallback_tests (downgrades ONLY an unplayable browser Matroska DirectPlay; genuine webm / native app / non-matroska / already-transcoding / audio-only preserved). Full `just test` 1300/1300.
B81|2026-07-16|BROWSER PLAYBACK (Lace incident, same session as B80) — a remote/WAN viewer rebuffered on high-bitrate transcodes. lace on WAN "Auto" quality: Supergirl S01E04 (10.3 Mbps h264 source) transcoded at ~10 Mbps and a 34 Mbps movie at the 12 Mbps encoder ceiling — far more than a home uplink sustains. Root cause: jellyfin-web "Auto" advertises an effectively-unlimited MaxStreamingBitrate, so `negotiate` left the transcode uncapped and `effective_video_bitrate(None, source)` = min(source, 12M) targeted ~source; there was NO server-side default cap and NO connection awareness, and the emitted TranscodingUrl carried no VideoBitrate param so a client quality-pick didn't reach the segment transcoder.|B81. New `[server].remote_default_bitrate_bps` (default 6 Mbps, 0 disables). `playback_info` clamps a transcode's ceiling to it when the client is on a non-private (WAN) address — `Decision::clamp_video_bitrate` (min with any explicit lower client pick; fills an "Auto"/None cap). client_is_remote (realip_remote_addr → X-Forwarded-For) treats RFC1918/ULA/link-local/loopback/CGNAT-Tailscale as local; unparseable → local (never throttle a LAN viewer). The ceiling rides the TranscodingUrl as `&VideoBitrate=` (h264/vp9/remux) through playback_qs master→variant→segment, so it survives a GC'd session + applies to remux re-encodes. Guards: clamp_video_bitrate_*, qs_video_bitrate_cap_*, min_opt_*, remote_ip_tests (asserts lace's real 62.169.136.4 remote; pod-net/Tailscale/v6 local). Full `just test` 1300/1300.
B82|2026-07-17|LIBRARY BROWSE (Google TV) — the "Movies" and "TV Shows" library grids came back EMPTY on the Jellyfin Android TV app (0.19.9, jellyfin-sdk-kotlin/OkHttp) while jellyfin-web listed fine. No 4xx/5xx — pharos logged `GET /items?...&parentId=<id> → 200` with an empty envelope (invisible as an error). Root cause: the Android TV client browses via `/items?parentId=6dcaebce-be81-6e0b-6dca-ebcebe816e0b&includeItemTypes=Movie&recursive=true` — a DASHED UUID, because jellyfin-sdk-kotlin round-trips every id through a `Uuid` type and re-serialises it dashed. pharos stores library/synth/entity wire_ids in the dashless 32-hex form `library_id_for_root` emits (`{h:016x}{h:016x}`), and `resolve_parent_filter` (+ legacy `restrict_to_parent`) matched `?ParentId=` by RAW string equality (`l.wire_id == pid`, `series_id_for_key(..) == pid`, `item_ids_for_genre(pid)`, …). The dashed id equals none of the ~12 dashless candidates → falls through every branch → `ParentResolution::Empty` → blank grid. jellyfin-web echoes the dashless id verbatim, so it matched and worked — masking the bug. Sibling of the B15 id-format sweep, which canonicalised inbound ROUTE-PATH ids (`parse_item_id` accepts dashed) but never touched ParentId QUERY-PARAM resolution. Diagnosed from pharos pod stdout (Loki was crash-looping); the `http.target` span carried the full dashed querystring.|B82/V41. New `canonical_parent_id(pid)` — dashed 8-4-4-4-12 hex UUID → dashless lowercase 32-hex; every other shape (dashless hex, legacy decimal, non-id) returned borrowed/untouched. Applied at the single `pid` bind in BOTH `resolve_parent_filter` and `restrict_to_parent`, so all downstream `== pid` comparisons (library, media-root, series, season, artist, album, genre, person, studio, collection, tag) accept the dashed form. Guards: canonical_parent_id_tests (dashed→dashless+lowercase incl. dashed all-zeros; non-dashed shapes pass through without allocating), parent_id_dashed_uuid_resolves_same_as_dashless (integration: dashed Movies wire_id resolves the same single item as the dashless id, not an empty grid). Full workspace `cargo nextest` 1303/1303, clippy -D warnings clean.
B83|2026-07-17|SIMILAR ITEMS (mixed library) — "More Like This" for a movie surfaced unrelated content (an AWS training video under "Back to the Future"). Root cause: `similarity_score` gave a `+5` floor to ANY same-`kind` pair, and `items_similar` kept everything scoring `>0`. Movie files rarely carry embedded genre metadata (pharos's only content signal besides series/album), so in the deployed single-root mixed library (`/var/lib/pharos/media`, all content one "mixed" library) nearly every Movie-vs-Movie pair scored exactly 5, passed the gate, and the rail became an alphabetical dump of every Movie-kind item — tie-broken by title, so "AWS…" sorted to the top. The `+5` was originally added to stop Movies appearing under Audio "More Like This", but the audio-vs-video hard gate already covers that; the floor's real effect was to make same-kind sufficient on its own.|B83/V42. `similarity_score`: (1) same-kind `+5` now applies ONLY when a real signal already scored (`if s > 0 && kind == kind`), so it is a tiebreak not a qualifier; (2) new same-immediate-parent-folder signal (`+15`) — in a mixed single-root library the user's folder layout (`…/Movies/*` vs `…/Training/*`) is the most reliable "same content group" signal, so co-located films still cluster while cross-category clips drop out. Result: a movie with no shared genre/series/folder yields a short/empty rail, never unrelated junk. Guards: similarity_score_tests (same-kind-alone → 0; shared-genre → 40; same-folder-alone → 20, cross-folder → 0); existing items_similar_scores_by_series_then_genre + similar_on_music_is_music_only still green. Full workspace `cargo nextest` 1306/1306, clippy -D warnings clean.
B84|2026-07-17|MARK-WATCHED CRASH (Google TV) — marking an episode watched consistently CRASHED the Jellyfin Android TV app. adb: the app calls `POST /UserPlayedItems/{itemId}` (the modern userId-less jellyfin-sdk-kotlin endpoint; the user is the bearer), which pharos did NOT implement — only the legacy `/Users/{userId}/PlayedItems/{itemId}`. The unrouted POST returned an error the SDK then tried to deserialize as a UserItemDataDto and threw, killing the app (logcat: socket PID flipped mid-session = process relaunched). Same missing-modern-endpoint class as the DTO-parity bugs. Item id arrives DASHED (kotlin re-serialises ids), already handled by parse_item_id.|B84. Add the four userId-less routes `POST|DELETE /UserPlayedItems/{id}` + `POST|DELETE /UserFavoriteItems/{id}` (user = bearer, no path userId, no require_bearer_match). Extract the four mutations into shared `op_*` fns so the legacy and userId-less handlers can't drift. Response is the same UserItemDataDto. Guard: userid_less_played_endpoint_marks_and_returns_dto (exercises the dashed-UUID id shape + the favourite variant). Full workspace `cargo nextest` 1307/1307, clippy -D warnings clean.
B85|2026-07-17|TV SHOWS NAVIGATION DEAD (Google TV) — on the Android TV app a Series tile showed no artwork and selecting it did nothing (stayed on the TV Shows screen); "Go to Series" from an episode dead-ended; a season's episode list came back empty. adb: the app requests `GET /Shows/5fe55c65-1a0f-4e9c-5fe5-5c651a0f4e9c/Episodes`, `/Shows/{id}/Seasons`, `/Items/{seriesId}`, `/Items/{seriesId}/Images/Primary` with a DASHED id. pharos's SYNTH ids (series/season/person/collection/library) are resolved by comparing the raw path string to the dashless `series_id_for_key`/`season_id_for_key`/hash — never dashed-normalised (B15's `parse_item_id` covers REAL ids only; B82 covered the ParentId query param only). So every dashed synth id missed → empty season list, 404 artwork, 404 series detail → navigation dead. Same dashed-kotlin-id root as B82, on the route-path + synth-id surface.|B85/V41. Promote `canonical_parent_id` → `pub(crate) canonical_wire_id` (unchanged dash-strip+lowercase of an 8-4-4-4-12 UUID; every other shape untouched) and apply it at each synth-id path choke point: `shows_seasons` + `shows_episodes` (path SeriesId + `?SeasonId=`), `fetch_item_dto` (`/Items/{id}` detail → series/season/collection/library-view), and `serve_image` (person + Series/Season artwork). Guard: dashed_synth_series_id_resolves_across_show_routes (dashed SeriesId resolves /Shows/{id}/Episodes + /Seasons + /Items/{id} detail identically to dashless). Full workspace `cargo nextest` 1308/1308, clippy -D warnings clean.
B89|2026-07-17|POSTERS LOAD SLOWLY (all clients) — the user: "TV Show and Movies posters take far too long to load… I want them near instant." Root cause: every image response (`serve_image` + `serve_local_artwork` + `get_chapter_image`) was built as `HttpResponse::Ok().content_type(..).body(bytes)` with NO `Cache-Control` and NO `ETag`. So neither jellyfin-web's browser cache nor the Android TV app's okhttp/Glide cache could reuse a poster — every grid render and every back-navigation re-requested the FULL set of tiles from the server, re-reading (and for cold tiles re-extracting/re-scaling) each one. The on-disk cache + per-width scaling already existed, but the bytes were shipped over the wire again and again because the client was never told they were cacheable. Real Jellyfin sends a long max-age on its tagged image URLs; pharos sent none.|B89/V45. New `deliver_image` helper stamps `Cache-Control: private, max-age=604800` (7d) + a content `ETag` (served file length+mtime, hex) on every 200, and short-circuits to a bodyless `304` when the client's `If-None-Match` matches (cheap revalidation after the TTL lapses). All read paths — extracted frame, scaled copy, webp/avif re-encode, local sidecar, chapter thumb — route through it; `If-None-Match` is threaded from the four GET/HEAD handlers + the chapter handler (`if_none_match_of(req)`). 404s and the person-photo 302 stay uncached (so new art / a fixed redirect isn't pinned). Guards: served_image_carries_cache_control_and_etag, matching_if_none_match_yields_304. Full workspace `just test` 1316/1316, clippy -D warnings clean. NOTE: this fixes REPEAT loads (the browsing case); first-ever extraction of a sidecar-less poster still pays one ffmpeg frame-grab — scan-time poster warming is a separate, larger lever (disk + scan cost) left for a follow-up decision.
B88|2026-07-17|CHAPTER IMAGES MISSING (Google TV) — chapter thumbnails never appeared on the Jellyfin Android TV app (the user: "chapter images are either not generated or not loading"). Root cause: `ChapterInfoDto` (the `Chapters` array in a `BaseItemDto`) carried only `Name` + `StartPositionTicks` + `ImageDateModified` — it had NO `ImageTag` field at all. Jellyfin's real `ChapterInfo.ImageTag` is a nullable string, and every client (jellyfin-web, jellyfin-sdk-kotlin) gates the whole chapter-image path on it: a chapter thumbnail is rendered — and `/Items/{id}/Images/Chapter/{index}?tag=` is fetched — ONLY when `ImageTag != null`. With the field absent it deserialised as null, so the client treated every chapter as image-less and never requested the frame. The `/items/{id}/images/chapter/{index}` route + `ImageCache::chapter` (on-demand ffmpeg seek to `start_ms`) were already implemented and working — they were simply never called. So "not loading", not "not generated".|B88/V44. Add `image_tag: Option<String>` (`ImageTag`) to `ChapterInfoDto`. Populate per chapter in `BaseItemDto::from_domain`: video → `Some(image_tag_for(item.id, "chapter:{idx}"))` (stable, per-index, so each thumbnail keys distinctly); Audio → `None` (no video frame to extract). The chapter-image route serves by index and ignores the tag value, so the tag only needs to be present + stable. Guard: video_chapters_advertise_a_non_null_image_tag (video chapters all carry distinct non-null tags; audio chapter stays null). Full workspace `just test` 1314/1314, clippy -D warnings clean.
B87|2026-07-17|EPISODES OUT OF ORDER (Google TV) — a season's episode list showed episodes in a scrambled order (Buffy S02E20 as the immediate next of S02E04, then S02E06) on the Jellyfin Android TV app, while jellyfin-web listed them correctly. Root cause: the Android TV app lists a season's episodes via `/Items?parentId=<season>&IncludeItemTypes=Episode&SortBy=ParentIndexNumber,IndexNumber,SortName`. `sort_primary` maps `ParentIndexNumber`/`IndexNumber` → `SortPrimary::TrackOrder`, and the TrackOrder arm of `build_media_query` emitted the AUDIO columns `(disc_number, track_number, Name)`. Those columns are NULL for episodes → every episode tied on disc/track → the sort collapsed to the `Name` (title) then `Id` tiebreak → an effectively alphabetical / hash order. The `ParentIndexNumber`/`IndexNumber` tokens are TYPE-OVERLOADED in Jellyfin (season+episode for an Episode row, disc+track for an Audio row); the TrackOrder mapping only ever handled the audio meaning. jellyfin-web drives its season page off `/Shows/{id}/Episodes` (a separate handler that sorts in-memory by `(season_number, episode_number)`), so it was correct — masking the `/Items` bug.|B87/V43. Add `SortKey::SeasonNumber` (`season_number` column, allowlisted in `sort_key_column`). Make the `SortPrimary::TrackOrder` arm of `build_media_query` kind-aware: episode-scoped (`mq.kinds` contains `Episode`, OR `mq.parent` is a `Series`/`Season` pivot) → `(SeasonNumber, IndexNumber, Name)`; else the album `(DiscNumber, TrackNumber, Name)` unchanged. Guards: episode_sort_tests (unit: episode kind AND Season parent both pick season/episode keys; Audio+Album parent keeps disc/track), episode_list_sorts_by_air_order_not_title (integration: episodes seeded with reverse-alphabetical titles come back in episode-number order over the real `/Items` HTTP path + store). Full workspace `just test` 1313/1313, clippy -D warnings clean.
B86|2026-07-17|MUSIC WON'T PLAY (Google TV) — every music track was listed but hitting play did nothing. pod logs: after a 200 PlaybackInfo the app fetched `GET /audio/{id}/stream?container=mp3&static=true&tag=<ETag>` and got 401 (repeatedly). Native direct-play (ExoPlayer) fetches the media URL RAW with no bearer header — B75 handles this for video by forwarding the MediaSource ETag (== PlaySessionId) as `?tag=` and authorizing it in `stream::authorize_media` (resolves the registered session, matches media_id). But B75 was applied only to `stream_video`/`head_video`; `stream_audio`/`head_audio` were left on the strict `AuthUser` extractor, which 401s a tokenless request before any handler logic → all music DirectPlay dead. (Video worked because its DirectStreamUrl path uses authorize_media; audio carries no DirectStreamUrl and the app builds `/Audio/{id}/stream?static=true&tag=` itself.)|B86. `stream_audio` + `head_audio` now parse the id and call `authorize_media(&state, &req, media_id)` (the ETag `?tag=` capability) exactly like `stream_video`/`head_video`, dropping the `AuthUser` extractor. Tokenless-and-tagless still 401s (route_auth_audit `/Audio/1/stream`=Auth stays green), only a valid item-scoped capability tag passes. Guard: native_capability_etag_authorizes_tokenless_audio_stream (PlaybackInfo → ETag → GET /audio/{id}/stream?tag= must not 401) + seed gained an Audio row. Full workspace `cargo nextest` 1309/1309, clippy -D warnings clean.
B79|2026-07-16|LATENT (found during the B78/V38 json!→DTO sweep, NOT YET SHIPPED as a crash fix): the `/socket` remote-control pushes send `PlayState.Command` (kotlin `PlaystateCommand` enum) and `GeneralCommand.Name` (kotlin `GeneralCommandType` enum) as free strings from `canonical_command(title_case(path_param))` — an arbitrary `/Sessions/{id}/Playing/{command}` or `/Command/{command}` path yields a value outside the enum → a native cast-TARGET client (jellyfin-sdk-kotlin) crashes decoding the enum (same class as B78). Worse, `session_play_request` emits `"Play"` and `is_playstate_command("Play")` routes it into the PlayState branch, but `Play` is NOT a `PlaystateCommand` member (valid set: Stop/Pause/Unpause/NextTrack/PreviousTrack/Seek/Rewind/FastForward/PlayPause) — so even pharos's own cast-play would crash a native target. Not observed live yet (needs a native device as a remote-control TARGET, which the reported crashes weren't).|FIXED 2026-07-16 (V39). `translate_session_command` now routes by the real Jellyfin taxonomy and NEVER emits an out-of-set enum: (1) `Play` → the dedicated `Play` MessageType carrying a typed `PlayRequestData` (`PlayCommand` gated to the `PlayCommand` enum, default `PlayNow`; `ControllingUserId` a real UUID) — no longer a PlayState `Command:"Play"`; (2) a command in the exact `PlaystateCommand` set (`Play` REMOVED from `is_playstate_command`) → `PlayState`; (3) a command in the exact `GeneralCommandType` set → `GeneralCommand`, with a non-null UUID `ControllingUserId` (was `""` — itself an invalid-UUID crash) and `Arguments` coerced to a `Map<String,String>` (scalars stringified, non-strings dropped — was arbitrary JSON, another crash); (4) anything else → DROPPED (`None`) + `tracing::warn`, so a poisoned enum can never reach the wire. Controlling user threaded through `SocketBroadcast::SessionCommand`/`notify_session_command` from the 3 `/Sessions` handlers. SyncPlay `SendCommand` path checked clean (only Unpause/Pause/Seek — valid `SendCommandType`). Guards: translate_session_command_play_is_its_own_message_not_playstate, _play_defaults_invalid_playcommand_to_playnow, _unknown_command_is_dropped_not_poisoned, _routes_general_commands (Arguments now `"60"` string + ControllingUserId asserted). Full `just test` 1294/1294.
B78|2026-07-16|NATIVE ANDROID/GOOGLE TV CRASH (two reported symptoms — home-screen load + mid-playback — one root cause). Live `adb logcat` (device 192.168.1.46, org.jellyfin.androidtv 0.19.9) caught the exact throw: `kotlinx.serialization.MissingFieldException: Field 'Type' is required for type 'org.jellyfin.sdk.model.api.BaseItemDto', but it was missing at path: $.Data[0].NowPlayingItem` unwinding through `SessionInfoDto$$serializer` → `SessionsMessage$$serializer`. The `/socket` `Sessions` broadcast (state PlaybackProgress → socket.rs translate) hand-built `"NowPlayingItem": { "Id": item_id }` as a raw `serde_json::json!` literal with NO `Type`. jellyfin-web (loose JS) tolerates it; the kotlin `BaseItemDto` has exactly two no-default REQUIRED fields — `Id` (present) + `Type` (missing) — so the deserializer throws and ACRA kills the app. It fires on app launch (socket connects, any active session pushes a Sessions snapshot → menu crash) AND every ~10s playback-progress tick (mid-playback crash). Invisible to the B14 kotlin-DTO audit because that audit fixed the typed `*Dto` STRUCTS; this payload bypassed them as a json! literal. No crash log ever reached pharos: jellyfin-androidtv reports via ACRA to its OWN backend, not /ClientLog/Document (0 hits in pod logs). NOT codec-related — Supergirl S01E04 is vanilla h264/eac3/1080p SDR; the DV-RPU log spam was an unrelated file.|B78/V38. `NowPlayingItem` is now a typed `NowPlayingItemLite { Id, Type }` inside typed `SessionsBroadcastEntry`/`SessionPlayStateLite` (socket_messages.rs), serialized not json!-literaled. `Type` = `MediaKind::base_item_kind()` (new canonical PascalCase projection in pharos-core, the inverse of from_wire); the progress handler resolves the item kind via `stores.get`, threads `item_kind: Option<MediaKind>` through the broadcast; kind-unresolved OMITS NowPlayingItem (nullable) rather than emit it Type-less. Guards: translate_playback_progress_emits_sessions_outbound (asserts NowPlayingItem.Type=="Episode") + translate_playback_progress_omits_now_playing_item_when_kind_unknown. FOLLOW-UP (user-directed): audit ALL remaining `serde_json::json!` wire literals → typed DTOs (V38), and move socket serialization to sonic-rs. Verify live on device after deploy.
B77|2026-07-16|BROWSER PLAYBACK: h264 + 6ch eac3 (and ac3/dts) in Matroska — ~4000+ library files — was advertised `SupportsDirectStream:true` to browsers that can't play the raw file. Root-cause of the T98 trace: is_firefox=false was CORRECT (config `linux_firefox_h264=true`, the B60 opt-out, deliberately falses it for Linux FF), NOT a detector bug. The real defect: `SupportsDirectStream` was set true for every DirectPlay/AudioRemux/VideoRemux decision, but pharos's `/stream` serves the source file VERBATIM (no container remux, no audio transcode — only deliver_stream's webm re-label). A `matroska,webm`-container source never matches a single-valued profile container (`"webm".eq("matroska,webm")` is false), so h264+eac3 → VideoRemux, advertised DirectStream → the browser got a raw Matroska (`video/x-matroska` rejected) / undecodable eac3; AudioRemux additionally emitted NO TranscodingUrl, so the client had no fallback. Native players (ExoPlayer) that CAN play mkv+eac3 declare it → DirectPlay, unaffected.|B77/V37. `supports_direct_stream` now true ONLY when the raw file plays as-is: a real DirectPlay, OR a webm-codec Matroska (VP8/9/AV1 + Opus/Vorbis) the re-label serves as `video/webm` to a client whose profile lists a matching webm DirectPlayProfile. AudioRemux(video)+VideoRemux otherwise → DirectStream:false + a master.m3u8 TranscodingUrl (segment surface re-encodes h264+aac; `-c:v copy` per-segment is broken, B45); AudioRemux(audio) → /audio/{id}/universal. Errs safe: worst case is a transcode that still plays, never an unplayable raw stream. Guards: browser_codec_matrix src7/8 + direct_stream_only_advertised_when_raw_file_plays. NOTE recommend live-verify on the deployed jellyfin-web before relying on it.
B72|2026-07-15|NFS disk-hammering bug CLASS — heavy whole-file media ops starve live playback: reachable without the bg_io gate, re-opening the source per-item, refetching uncached, or fanning a cache-miss into concurrent duplicate demuxes; compounded by playback paths that never mark activity so the regulator's gate never parks. Surfaced as Supergirl S01E03 stuck buffering: the file's ~10 embedded text subtitle tracks cold-demuxed one-ffmpeg-open-PER-TRACK (measured 494-520s each over NFS) concurrent with a library scan, while the client direct-streamed stream.mp4 off the same NFS → data starvation. Audit sites: (1) REGULATOR BLIND — note_playback_activity() only in stream.rs:376 (webm); deliver_stream/serve_from_offset/stream_audio/audio_universal/audio_remux never mark it → bg_io stays at BG_IO_MAX during direct-play+audio; stamped once per-request so a long single GET goes stale after BG_IO_BUSY_WINDOW_SECS=12s. (2) trickplay_backfill.rs:303 generate_item bypass=true: 3× whole-file DECODE (widths 320/640/1280) + subtitle demux + font extract, ungated on the watched file. (3) subtitles.rs:267 pre_extract_subtitles loop + library_watch.rs:343 throttle=false: N whole-file demuxes ungated on playback path. (4) subtitles.rs:420 deliver_srt: NO cache — whole-file demux every Stream.srt fetch. (5) image_cache.rs ZERO fetch_locks (sub/trickplay/hls caches all have them): fetch:194 poster/backdrop + ensure_all_attachments:417 fonts stampede (fonts double-fired from subtitles.rs:150 + trickplay_backfill.rs:363) + fixed .batch.tmp scratch races/corrupts. (6) run_ffmpeg_embedded/_ass vtt/ass cold: mutex-deduped, no bg_io permit. (7) waveform.rs:88 run_astats: full decode, uncached, no dedup. (8) segment_backfill.rs:135 analyze_season: 2 opens/episode (bounded, persisted → first-compute). (9) subtitles.rs:1174 discover_sidecars: read_dir walks per PlaybackInfo over NFS lookupcache=none. Cleared: fingerprint.rs (samples size+duration+first/last 1MiB, not whole-file; log error = NFS timeout on sample read).|V34+V35+V36. Make the resource un-gettable without the guard: (a) BgPermit token newtype (only from bg_io.acquire_owned) required BY SIGNATURE on every heavy source op → ungated op won't compile [V34]; (b) PlaybackLease + MeteredBody: centralize media open behind AppState::open_source (source path private), lease restamps note_playback_activity continuously as bytes flow, wired into ALL delivery paths [V35]; (c) shared single-flight helper a cache can't exist without + per-item ONE-open batching + persistent caches for deliver_srt & waveform + per-caller-unique scratch [V36]. Sequence T93-T97; BgPermit first (retrofits sites 2/3/4/6/7 by signature). Guards per-task.
B58|2026-07-14|SyncPlay deep audit — scrubbing the timeline while playing could leave the whole group stuck PAUSED after the seek. SeekTo always sets playback=Paused before broadcasting the Seek, then computed `resume = playback is Playing` = false. A single seek was fine (that line ran while still Playing), but jellyfin-web sends a BURST of Seeks while dragging the scrubber: the 2nd+ seek arriving before the 1st's readiness gate resolved recomputed resume from the already-Paused state → the gate settled the group Paused even though everyone was playing.|B58. SeekTo carries over an already-open gate's resume_playing (which holds the true pre-burst intent) instead of recomputing from the just-set Paused playback; only a seek with NO gate open reads the live playback. Guard: seek_burst_while_playing_stays_playing (paused-clock, resolves via anti-wedge).
B57|2026-07-14|SyncPlay deep audit — three actor edge cases that could confuse or wedge a real party. (a) AddMember was not idempotent: a duplicate New/Join for a member already in the roster (client retry, or the New/Join churn a socket race triggers) re-inserted a fresh MemberRec — resetting its buffering + ignore_wait flags — and re-broadcast a "user joined" toast to everyone, so a mid-buffer re-join could drop the freeze flag and spam phantom joins. (b) SetNewQueue with an empty queue set playing_index=0 on no items, broadcast an empty PlayQueue, and opened a readiness gate → group resolves to Playing with current_playlist_item_id()==None, a wedge only a later non-empty queue clears. (c) An Unpause issued while a member was buffering opens a gate on that member, but ws-native/native clients signal recovery with BufferingEnd, not Ready — and BufferingEnd never cleared the gate's pending set, so it could only release via the 30s anti-wedge (a needless hang on every mid-buffer resume).|B57. (a) AddMember on an existing member_id is now a reconnect: refresh name+last_seen, re-send that member's own Joined+catch-up, reply — but skip the roster-wide MemberJoined and preserve flags. (b) SetNewQueue returns early on an empty item list. (c) BufferingEnd now drops the member from any open WaitingGate and resolves it when it was the last holdout (same as MemberReady/SetIgnoreWait). Guards: duplicate_add_member_is_idempotent, empty_set_new_queue_is_ignored, buffering_end_resolves_an_open_unpause_gate.
B62|2026-07-15|Quick Connect finalize path was near-invisible: `/QuickConnect/Initiate` logged nothing, so a code/secret that later 404'd on poll/authorize couldn't be correlated to its mint. Registry actor logs Initiate (code + secret PREFIX + device + live pending count), Authorize (code + matched/UNKNOWN + live code count), Consume/finalize (secret prefix + known/authorized/consumed). Connect poll at DEBUG (fires ~5s; its 404 already logged by HTTP middleware). Secrets logged as 8-char prefix only (bearer credential). This trace immediately revealed B63 (all three QC steps succeeded server-side → the failure was the response body).
B70|2026-07-15|Diagnostic: the Android TV crashed PARSING the PlaybackInfo 200 response (no server error, no /ClientLog report). Added gated logging of the PlaybackInfo response body (PHAROS_LOG_ALL_REQUESTS) so the offending field could be captured. Revealed B71.
B71|2026-07-15|CRASH ON PLAY: the logged Android-TV PlaybackInfo body showed subtitle `MediaStream.Index` = 1000000, 1000001, … and `DefaultSubtitleStreamIndex` = 1000000 for an item with 12 SIDECAR subtitle files. pharos numbered sidecar streams from a fixed `SIDECAR_BASE_INDEX = 1_000_000` (a discriminator so they wouldn't collide with ffprobe indices). The jellyfin-sdk-kotlin players (Android/Google TV) treat `MediaStream.Index` POSITIONALLY (array/track-selector index) and crash out-of-bounds on the sparse 1_000_000; jellyfin-web tolerated the gap, so it was invisible until a native client. Sidecar wire indices are now PER-ITEM contiguous — `sidecar_base_index(probe)` = one past the highest real ffprobe stream index — so every emitted `Index` is a real, small, collision-free position. The stream builder AND the 3 subtitle-fetch handler sites compute the same base identically (removed the fixed `SIDECAR_BASE_INDEX` const), so wire index ↔ source stays consistent. Toward the user's "make invalid states unrepresentable" — a full StreamIndex newtype / sequential re-index is the follow-up. Guard: sidecar_subtitle_indices_are_contiguous_not_a_sentinel. Diagnostic B70 can be reverted (logAllRequests off) once confirmed.

Also: the B68 fix (mixed→null CollectionType, Type Tag→Folder) broke three tests that had CODIFIED the buggy values ("mixed", "Tag") — caught only by the full suite, not the targeted runs (lesson: run full `just test` before push).|B69. First increment of the type-system sweep toward the full audit (§T) — the rest of the inline item json! still needs a typed BaseItemKind enum + BaseItemDto structs.

Cause: the inline CollectionFolder json! carried a PARTIAL `UserData` — `{Played, PlayCount}` — but the kotlin `UserItemDataDto` requires SIX non-null fields (playbackPositionTicks, playCount, isFavorite, played, key, itemId); a present-but-incomplete nested object throws the parser. Also `CollectionType:"mixed"` is not a valid kotlin `CollectionType` enum value (Jellyfin mixed libraries send null). Both crash the strict client on an otherwise-200 response.|B68. folder_user_data(item_id) emits the full UserItemDataDto for every synthesised folder/stub (library views ×3, series, season — 5 sites); wire_collection_type maps "mixed"/"" → null. Guards: userviews_items_carry_kotlin_complete_userdata (asserts each view's UserData has all 6 fields + CollectionType != "mixed"). Diagnosis tooling: request logging (B68). B66|2026-07-15|`POST /ClientLog/Document` was a 204 stub that DISCARDED the body — so when the Android TV crashed and "sent a crash report", the server kept nothing, and the B65 crash had to be reconstructed from access logs. B63|2026-07-15|Quick Connect on a TV: code entered fine, phone authorized fine, but the TV showed "Unable to connect to server". B61|2026-07-14|Quick Connect on a TV showed the 6-digit code, then it VANISHED within seconds — before the user could type it on their phone. B59|2026-07-14|jana joined a SyncPlay party with alison+lace and never got a stream, while the group played fine. Traces: alison+lace on h264 HLS (`hls1/*.ts`), but jana's browser requested `vp9/30.m4s`, `vp9/36.m4s` and then bailed — a cold, separate VP9 encode that stalled. Root cause: encode sharing keys on the negotiated codec (cache key has no user/device but DOES include codec), so it only shares when members land on the SAME codec. `negotiate` picked the transcode video codec via `pick_first_csv(tp.video_codec)` = the client's FIRST-listed codec; jana's Windows Firefox jellyfin-web listed VP9 first, so she diverged onto VP9 (software, slow) instead of sharing alison+lace's warm h264. B56|2026-07-14|SyncPlay audit (deep pass) found the member delivery sink table (`MemberSinks`, DashMap<member_id, sink>) was NOT generation-fenced, unlike the hub's own per-device session store. Two ways a live socket got silently blackholed (matching the "got stuck / didn't start playing" reports): (a) two connects for one deterministic member_id race their `sinks.insert` — register() is serialized so gens are ordered, but the subsequent insert into MemberSinks is a plain last-writer-wins DashMap write, so the OLDER task's insert landing after the newer's overwrites the live sink with a dead one; (b) `add_caller_to_group` (HTTP New/Join) re-inserts the sink it resolved from the hub, but if a reconnect installed a fresh sink between resolve and insert, the stale snapshot clobbers it. The disconnect `remove` was likewise unconditional — a late teardown from an older socket could wipe the sink a reconnect just registered. A blackholed member receives no Play/Pause/PlayQueue/GroupJoined until its next reconnect.|V32. MemberSinks::insert/remove now carry the owning socket's conn_gen: insert lands only when gen ≥ stored, remove only when gen == stored (dashmap remove_if). Jellyfin /socket threads reg.gen (added conn_gen to ResolvedSession so the HTTP path fences too); WS-native /sync/v1/ws uses a process-wide monotonic counter; the WS-native immediate teardown also gen-checks before RemoveMember. Guards: stale_generation_insert_does_not_clobber_the_live_sink, stale_generation_remove_spares_the_live_sink.
B55|2026-07-14|SyncPlay audit (post-B54) found the V19 buffering freeze could wedge the group FOREVER, matching the live "member buffering — freezing group" traces that survived every prior fix. One member's BufferingStart pauses the whole group and sets group_paused_due_to_buffering; the only paths that cleared it were BufferingEnd/Ready from a STILL-PRESENT member. So if the sole buffering member disconnected mid-buffer (socket drop / tab close / crash) — no BufferingEnd ever comes — remove_member dropped it from the roster + readiness gate but left the freeze standing, stranding everyone else paused. Two more holes with no safety net: a member that simply buffers forever (slow link) had NO deadline (unlike the readiness gate's 30s anti-wedge), and a group hydrated mid-freeze on a rolling deploy restored the freeze flag but cleared every member's buffering flag, so the BufferingEnd auto-resume could never fire — frozen from the first message.|V31. remove_member now lifts the freeze immediately when the departing member was the last one buffering (same auto-resume as BufferingEnd). buffering_since (monotonic, not persisted) arms a BUFFERING_MAX_MS=30s anti-wedge in the actor select loop: on expiry it force-clears all buffering flags and resumes (or disarms if a readiness gate owns the resume). apply_persist re-arms buffering_since when it restores a frozen group. Guards: buffering_freeze_lifts_when_sole_buffering_member_leaves, buffering_freeze_times_out_via_anti_wedge (paused-clock).

Guards: resolve_with_retry_wins_the_socket_registration_race.

B45|2026-07-14|Avatar + Supergirl wouldn't START playing on Mac Firefox (post-B43): B43 moved Mac FF off the VP9 force onto the h264 `/hls1` surface, whose h264-source path stream-copied video (`Decision::VideoRemux` + the no-session fallback both emitted `-c:v copy`). Segmented surface never copies: VideoRemux arm + fallback both re-encode H.264 (`target_video_bitrate` cap), AAC gains `-ac 2` (Opus precedent), mpegts gains `-muxdelay 0 -muxpreload 0`, `hls_output_codecs_string` always advertises the re-encode avc1 token, HLS_GEN_VERSION 2→3 wipes the poisoned cache. B12|2026-07-12|Phone (WebView player, h264 hls1 path) couldn't play most titles; one played then hung. Design intent was many parallel small encodes on a many-core box, but three settings self-defeated it: (a) sw x264/x265 got NO `-preset` → x264 default `medium` (quality-first, ~3-5× slower than Jellyfin's `veryfast`); (b) NO `-threads` cap on x264/x265 encode OR decode (only VP9 was capped) → each job auto-threaded across all 16 cores; (c) `default_cpu_permits()` = cores (16) → 16 admitted all-core jobs thrashed each other. Bench: per-segment CPU 940%→372% at same speed; 4 concurrent 6 s segments in 1.64 s wall (14.6× realtime aggregate, fits 16 cores). Guards: software_h264_gets_speed_preset_and_bounded_threads, nvenc_h264_keeps_hw_preset_vocabulary, video_copy_skips_thread_caps, cpu_permits_match_thread_budget_not_core_count.

B100|2026-07-20|spawn_scan_tracked called try_start but ran the scan even when it returned None (a scan already running), so every /Library/Refresh spawned another concurrent full catalog walk. During the B98 recovery this stacked ~10 concurrent scans re-probing 14k files over NFS, I/O-starving the single k8s node until etcd/apiserver went ServiceUnavailable.|V52. One scan at a time: start_or_mark_pending acquires the single slot or folds the request into ONE pending re-run (force OR'd) firing when the current scan finishes; finish_or_rerun keeps the task Running across the re-run (no Idle gap); a cancelled run never re-runs; the coalesced re-run rescans the full current media-root set. Guard: scan_tasks concurrent_starts_coalesce_into_one_pending_rerun + a_lone_run_with_no_pending_finishes_to_idle + a_cancelled_pass_does_not_rerun_even_with_pending.
B101|2026-07-20|Cannot seek to an unbuffered position in a direct-play mp4 (Raiders of the Lost Ark, h264/aac mp4, SupportsDirectPlay) on Firefox — same symptom as B94, DIFFERENT root. The file is non-faststart (atom order ftyp/free/mdat → moov seek-index at EOF); to seek, Firefox HEAD-probes /videos/{id}/stream.mp4 to learn the byte length so it can range-fetch the trailing moov, then requests offset ranges. The HEAD returned Content-Length: 0 for a 2.3 GB file: head_response built the reply with `.finish()` → empty `()` body = BodySize::Sized(0), and actix-http's h1 encoder derives a HEAD response's Content-Length from the body BodySize (bytes never sent) AND drops any hand-inserted Content-Length header (encoder.rs skip_len). Zero length reads as "nothing to seek" → seekable collapses to buffered. B95 had added the HEAD route (fixed a 405) but left the size bug. Faststart files (moov at front) seek fine regardless, so the bug is title-specific (Zombieland too). curl proved it: HEAD → Content-Length 0; mid-file Range → correct 206.|B101/V48. head_response serves the HEAD through actix-files NamedFile: its body is a SizedStream declaring BodySize::Sized(file_len) so the encoder emits the real length; the reader is never polled on a HEAD so no bytes are read. Also sets Accept-Ranges, Content-Type, ETag, Last-Modified + conditional-GET preconditions. Prod-verified post-deploy: HEAD now Content-Length 2361610362 + accept-ranges bytes. Guard: jellyfin_stream.rs head_reports_real_content_length (asserts the HEAD body BodySize == file size — a header-only assertion can't catch it since the clobber is in the wire encoder, downstream of call_service).
B102|2026-07-20|A remuxed/downmixed audio stream (/Audio/{id}/universal when the source codec is client-unacceptable or channels exceed the cap → audio_remux) could not seek at all. It is a live ffmpeg pipe (chunked, no Content-Length, not byte-seekable), so jellyfin-web seeks by re-requesting the URL with a fresh StartTimeTicks (same contract the progressive-WebM transcode honours). audio_remux spawned ffmpeg from t=0 with no -ss and ignored StartTimeTicks, so the encode always restarted at 0 and seeking was capped to already-streamed audio.|B102/V49. audio_universal parses StartTimeTicks and threads it into audio_remux, which emits `-ss <secs>` as an INPUT seek (before -i) for a fast keyframe seek + decode-forward. argv now built by a pure audio_remux_args helper so the ordering is unit-testable without spawning ffmpeg. Guard: audio_remux_args_seek_is_input_option (-ss precedes -i, correct seconds, downmix preserved) + audio_remux_args_no_seek_at_zero.
B103|2026-07-20|The VP9 continuous-audio VOD playlist (vp9_audio_playlist) computed its segment count from probe.duration_ms.unwrap_or(0.0) with NO fallback, so a media row whose persisted probe lacked a duration collapsed the whole audio timeline to a single 6 s segment (.max(1)) — the client could then only seek within the first segment. The video variant already avoided this by routing duration through load_hls_item (persisted probe → live-ffprobe fallback).|B103/V50. vp9_audio_playlist resolves duration via load_hls_item, the same ffprobe-backstopped path the video variant uses. B104|2026-07-20|The Jellyfin Android app (org.jellyfin.mobile, UA "Ktor client", ExoPlayer/media3) renders ASS subtitles as opaque BLACK BARS (text invisible) — reported on Neon Genesis Evangelion S01E15. The Android app's media3 SSA renderer can't handle heavy fansub typesetting → black boxes; and its profile omits ass/ssa by default (gated behind exoPlayerDirectPlayAss, off) so per Jellyfin semantics the server should have burned them. jellyfin-web was unaffected (SubtitlesOctopus/libass renders ASS). The session was already HLS-transcoding, so a burn was ~free.|V53. pharos now parses SubtitleProfiles and decide_subtitle_delivery routes each track (subtitle_delivery.rs); a burn-required text sub is advertised Encode + its index forwarded on the TranscodingUrl; a DirectPlay decision downgrades to Transcode when the selected/default sub must burn; the transcoder rasterizes ASS via the subtitles= filter from the cached .ass sidecar + fontsdir (NOT the whole source per segment — the final-review-caught NFS-stutter regression), setpts-aligned per segment. B105|2026-07-20|Swapping audio track mid-playback (english↔japanese, Code Geass s02e03) on the VP9/fMP4 path (Firefox) desynced A/V. The rendition anchored its `-ss` input seek AND `-output_ts_offset` to the NOMINAL grid `start_seg*6.0` (hls_cache.rs audio_hls_args), while the video segments seek+tfdt to the FRAME-SNAPPED grid `segment_start_secs = round(seg*6.0*fps)/fps`. On a non-integer-fps source the two grids differ by up to ~1 frame, so the renditions no longer share a timeline → a fixed sub-frame skew injected at the switch point. Same failure the video path already documents in build_args_for_device ("nominal 6.0 s tfdt grid accumulates ~6 ms/segment into audible A/V drift").|B105/V54. Thread source frame rate into ensure_audio_hls/_covering/audio_hls_args; frame-snap the audio seek anchor via audio_seg_start_secs (mirrors segment_start_secs); frame-align the audio playlist EXTINF to the video variant (segment_time_range); bump HLS_GEN_VERSION 3→4 to orphan stale nominal-anchored _audiohls dirs. B106|2026-07-20|Same report: the audio-track swap ALSO took an extraordinarily long time to resume, AND in a SyncPlay group both members switching audio wedged the whole group on a spinner until someone seeked backward. The rule `want_seg <= AUDIO_SEEK_LOOKAHEAD_SEGS(20) => start_seg 0` forced any switch inside the first ~120 s to re-encode 0→playhead (Opus + whole-file NFS demux, throttled behind the bg-IO gate) before playback resumed. A switch lands in a fresh -a{idx} dir (no from-0 session) so ALWAYS paid the full head re-encode. In a group this escalated: both switch → both post /syncplay/buffering → group freezes (group.rs:1704); resume is gated on `!any(member.buffering)` (group.rs:1746/1884) so the cold rendition never re-readies → 30s anti-wedge cycles + jellyfin-web's 30s WaitForEventDefaultTimeout haltGroupPlayback drops the member. Seeking backward lands on already-encoded segments → readies → resume.|B106/V55. Extract pure choose_audio_start_seg: reuse a running from-0 session only in the near-start window, reuse a running seek session within its lookahead, else seek straight to the playhead; from-0 whole-file rendition reserved for a genuine start-of-file request. Cuts the switch re-buffer to ~1-2 s (first seek-to-playhead segment) so a group member re-readies well inside both the freeze and the client 30s budget. ffmpeg-written playlists are throwaway (pharos synthesises the audio m3u8) so seek sessions don't regress the playlist. B107|2026-07-25|BROWSER PLAYBACK (Zen/Firefox 152, prototype.aliflix) — every video failed with `HTTP "Content-Type" of "video/matroska" is not supported`, a type pharos never emits, while jellyfin-web retried PlaybackInfo forever (2333 decisions in minutes, one new play_session_id per ~100 ms, ~30 range GETs/sec, Angie warning `an upstream response is buffered to a temporary file`). Root cause: `container_for` resolved ffprobe's ambiguous `format_name = "matroska,webm"` by preferring the `webm` alias, on the premise that the file IS the more specific member — false for the library's 9443 .mkv files. Three consequences compounded: (1) the negotiator compared `webm` against the client's DirectPlayProfiles, matched jellyfin-web's webm profile and returned DirectPlay (`pharos_playback_decision_total` carried exactly ONE series, `direct_play_block="not_blocked"` — direct play had never once been blocked); (2) the B80 guard `source_is_matroska` tests for the `matroska`/`mkv` token that the rename had already stripped from `source.container`, so it read false and the downgrade that exists precisely for this case never fired; (3) `webm_relabel_playable` decided browser playability from CODECS being WebM-legal, accepting any Matroska carrying them, so it returned true for exactly the files it was meant to catch — and it also gates `SupportsDirectStream`, so pharos advertised the raw file as playable with no TranscodingUrl. Firefox ships no Matroska demuxer at all: it reads the EBML DocType, finds `matroska` where WebM requires `webm`, and refuses the resource whatever codecs are inside — reporting the container it SNIFFED rather than the `video/webm` header pharos actually sent, which sent the investigation hunting a header bug that did not exist. Proven by reading the file's first 256 bytes through the public URL: EBML DocType `matroska`, track `V_AV1`.|B107/V56,V57. `container_for` takes the source path and disambiguates the Matroska family by extension (mkv/mka/webm), ISOBMFF untouched; `webm_relabel_playable` extracted as `source_plays_raw_in_browser` and gated on a genuine `webm` container before any codec clause; `DeliveryMime`/`source_is_webm_legal` stop relabelling a Matroska `video/webm` (a .mkv now serves `video/x-matroska`, and open/range-seek/HEAD still agree on ONE type). Observability shipped FIRST as its own commit: the decision log gains `source.container_probe` (ffprobe's raw alias list, beside the token pharos derived) and `client.direct_play_containers` (what the client actually declared), so a container decision is comparable from one line instead of reconstructed from a DeviceProfile by hand — LogQL `{namespace="pharos"} |= "playback decision" | json | source_container != source_container_probe`. Both fixes verified red-then-green by disarming them. Full `just test` 1641/1641, clippy -D warnings clean.
B108|2026-07-25|HLS SEGMENT STALL (hls.js `fragLoadTimeOut`, prototype.aliflix) — cold segments took ~94 s end-to-end while the encode inside them took ~3.4 s: `queue_wait_ms` 88159-91159 against `encode_ms` 3103-3613 on Cobra Kai segs 180-184, and 164566/4477 on a prewarmed seg 6. Root cause: `spawn_segment_prefetch` is dispatched BEFORE the client-blocking `segment_bytes_keyed` for the same request (hls.rs — the comment says so: "spawned before this segment's own read so they pipeline"), and both entered the scheduler's single FIFO as indistinguishable jobs. Depth is `hw_budget - 2` capped at 6, plus up to 4 burn-horizon segments, per served segment — so a handful of requests buried the segment a browser was actually waiting for under tens of speculative encodes, and `pending_cap` 256 let the backlog grow unbounded. Compounded by the segment cache's per-key `fetch_locks`: a queued prefetch holds its key's lock across the whole scheduler wait, so a client request for that same segment blocks on `lock_wait_ms` and inherits the entire queue wait it was meant to be spared. The diagnosis cost two wrong turns that the signals could not settle — "Nvenc registered but never selected" (read from gauges at IDLE, correlated with segment logs from an already-replaced pod) and "video_remux means no re-encode" (the master playlist says `CODECS="avc1.640028,mp4a.40.2"`) — because no signal distinguished a client's segment from a guess: `queue_wait_ms` proved a segment WAITED and nothing said what it waited BEHIND.|B108/V58. Instrumentation shipped FIRST as its own commit: `JobClass` threaded from the mint entry point through the scheduler; `SchedSnapshot` splits pending by class, names the oldest entry's age, counts inflight jobs and live streams; `DeviceStat` attributes each device's occupancy by class so `in_use` minus the attributed count is unexplained; a job dispatched after >3 s logs the queue it escaped; `submit_live` logs the permit it took, its device, and its hold duration on drop (that path holds a permit for a whole progressive transcode and reports no completion, so its occupancy previously had nothing behind it), and a rejected live stream logs the rejection plus the occupancy that caused it (the caller then runs an UNSCHEDULED inline ffmpeg holding no permit — load the scheduler cannot see). New series: `pharos_transcode_pending_by_class{class}`, `_pending_oldest_seconds`, `_inflight_jobs`, `_live_streams`, `pharos_transcode_queue_wait_seconds{class}`, `_encode_seconds{class,device}`, and a `class` label on `pharos_segment_produced_total` so success and failure stay symmetric. Then the fix: background admitted only out of spare capacity, never queued. Signal verified red-then-green by disarming the class split. Full `just test` 1648/1648, clippy -D warnings clean.
B109|2026-07-25|OBSERVABILITY DESTROYED BY NOISE — libav wrote decoder/demuxer diagnostics straight to fd 2, which the crash-isolated `transcode-worker` inherits to the pod's stdout: ~11.5M lines/hour on `{namespace="pharos"}`, evicting the structured JSON logs from both `kubectl logs` and Loki within minutes. It cost real diagnosis time twice — the Zombieland SyncPlay incident and the B108 segment stall, during which the very segment logs needed to prove the queue theory had already been flushed out by the flood. The earlier mitigation (9dd1d12, cap the global level to Error + `AV_LOG_SKIP_REPEATED`) did not touch the bulk of it, because the lines a damaged source emits are THEMSELVES at Error — "Invalid NAL unit size", "missing picture in access unit", "EBML header parsing failed", "Header missing" — and the library holds ~133 real-size but zeroed files that produce them per frame, continuously. Cause: level is the wrong axis. The defect was the DESTINATION (raw stderr, outside every filter and every structured sink), not the verbosity.|B109/V59. `libav::logbridge` installs an `av_log` callback that formats the line exactly as libav would, strips the per-decoder pointer, and emits it on the `libav` tracing target at DEBUG (fatal and above at ERROR), so the default `info` filter drops the flood and `PHAROS_LOG=info,libav=debug` brings it back as structured records. Volume stays queryable via `pharos_libav_log_total{level,component}`, counted before any filtering, with a bounded `component` label. Note this replaces libav's default callback and with it `AV_LOG_SKIP_REPEATED`, whose repeat-collapsing lives inside that callback. Signal verified red-then-green by disarming `install()`.
B110|2026-07-25|TAIL SEGMENT 500 — playing a title to its end returned 500 on the final segment. `SegmentWindow::for_segment` asked for a full 6.006 s even for the last segment, where the source ends partway through, so the encoder stopped at EOF and the B-class completeness check (which compares produced duration against the window's) read that as truncation, retried with a deeper preroll, got the same answer and failed. Caught by its own log on Guilty Crown S01E01 (1372.121 s, tail segment 228): `reached only 0.021s of the requested 6.006s` at attempt 0 (preroll None) and attempt 1 (preroll 45s). That is the last segment of nearly every title, since a length is rarely an exact multiple of 6.006 s — and the playlist ALREADY advertised a clamped tail, so the transcode and the EXTINF had been describing different windows. The first fix reached for `duration_ms.unwrap_or(0)` at the four call sites, which converted "duration not probed yet" into "clamp to the grid's 0.01 s floor" for EVERY segment index: the compat Playwright suite (whose seeded rows are unprobed — its startup rescan fails on `UNIQUE constraint failed: media_items.path`) went from 23 passed to 2 failed, both on `video.currentTime` never leaving 0, with the server's own `hls segment transcode far below realtime` WARN carrying `seg_secs: 0.01` on segment 0.|B110/V60. The window is clamped through `SegmentGrid::frame_snapped_range` so there is ONE clamp shared with the playlist that advertises it, and `for_segment` takes `Option<f64>` so a call site cannot launder an unknown duration into a zero (a non-positive duration is treated as the same absence). Signal verified red-then-green by disarming the guard: `total Some(0.0) seg 0: got 0.01 s, want the nominal 5.966666666666667 s`. Verified by query, not assertion — the pre-fix compat run logs two `"seek_secs":0.0,"seg_secs":0.01` windows, the post-fix run logs none and passes 23/23. Full `just test` 1652/1652, clippy -D warnings clean.
B111|2026-07-25|A video-only fMP4 segment carried a second track: `index=1 bin_data/codec_type=data/codec_tag=text/handler_name=SubtitleHandler`, on a rung where audio is a separate rendition. It is an MP4 CHAPTER track, not a subtitle, which is why `-sn` — unconditional in `build_args_for_device` since the VP9 stray-`mov_text` fix — did not stop it: chapters are not an input stream, ffmpeg copies them by default, and the mp4 muxer materialises them as a QuickTime chapter track. Every fMP4 rung of every chaptered source carried one (the VP9 rung of the same title was confirmed to carry it too), i.e. any ripped episode. FOUND while hunting the B112 playback wedge and initially reported as its cause — WRONGLY. `just probe-flags` builds two ladders differing only in `-map_chapters -1` and plays both with real hls.js: the chapter-bearing ladder played cleanly in Firefox AND Chromium, no errors, no refetch. The defect is real and the fix stands as hygiene; the causal claim was refuted before it shipped, and B112 records what the wedge actually was.|B111/V61. `-map_chapters -1` beside the existing `-sn`. No cache generation bump: segments already cached with the track were measured playable, so orphaning a whole generation would have been churn justified by a diagnosis that turned out to be wrong. Guard asserts the INVARIANT — a video-only segment carries exactly one video track — verified red before the fix with `got ["video", "data"]`.
B112|2026-07-25|PLAYBACK WEDGE (Cobra Kai, Zen/Firefox 152, prototype.aliflix) — `HLS Error: Type: otherError Details: internalException` from hls.js's `onWorkerError`, playback never advancing. The server log showed ONE segment (`h264cmaf/19.m4s`) fetched 17 times inside a single second, every request `200`. Not a byte defect: the exact bytes prod served were pulled back through a port-forward and replayed through real hls.js in REAL Firefox 152 (`just probe-bytes ... system-firefox`) — video, audio and the h264+Opus pairing all played to completion, 8 fragments, zero refetches. Two hypotheses were refuted the same way rather than assumed (Firefox rejecting `avc1` in MSE — the user's own console returned `true`; a video/audio playlist timeline mismatch — both playlists carried 229 segments and an identical 1371.070 s total). The actual cause, from Loki: the client asked for the segment at 19:52:49 and the server produced it at 19:54:34. `queue_wait_ms 214534, encode_ms 2788, device cpu` — 214 SECONDS waiting for a device permit against a 2.8 s encode, with `pending_interactive 0, pending_background 0` and `inflight_background 6`. Nothing was queued; six already-admitted BACKGROUND jobs held every permit while a client-blocking job starved. B108 stopped speculative work from QUEUEING ahead of clients; it does not stop work already admitted from HOLDING. The 17 fetches were hls.js retries released together when the one transcode finally landed. Compounding it, 60% of segments in the window ran on `cpu` despite NVENC being confirmed healthy at boot (`sessions: 8`), and NVENC's median encode (2932 ms) was barely better than the CPU's (3555 ms) — a gap far too small for an offloaded decode. Diagnosis cost: the line naming the winning device sat at DEBUG while the deployment runs at INFO, so nothing in the logs could distinguish a working GPU from an idle one; and `kubectl logs` had already rotated the window away under INFO chatter (Loki still had it — B109's lesson, re-learned).|B112/V62. Instrumentation shipped FIRST, before any fix: `DecodeAccel` makes GPU decode a reported verdict rather than an inference, `record_placement` emits the placement at INFO with the losing candidates, and each job gets a `transcode_job` span carrying its facts through to `outcome`. The starvation itself (background HOLDING permits, and the CPU-vs-NVENC placement skew) is NOT yet fixed — named here so the fix is measured against the signal rather than guessed at, which is what this bug cost three times over.
B113|2026-07-25|PLAYBACK STALL (Ghost in the Shell 1995, a 36.7 GB / 4979.8 s ≈ 59 Mbps 1080p remux, `SupportsDirectPlay:false` → full transcode). Client-visible symptom: three `404 audio segment not ready` on the VP9 audio rendition (`a5.m4s` twice, `a48.m4s`), which stalls hls.js. Root cause was upstream of the audio path. TWO PlaybackInfo calls 6 s apart (21:29:02, 21:29:08), the second carrying `AudioStreamIndex=1` and a new PlaySessionId; both sessions then requested the SAME video segments. `SegmentIdentity::new` folded the client's audio track into the key unconditionally, and the fMP4 rungs pass it through even though they choose `AudioDelivery::Separate` — audio is a demuxed rendition there and the argv ends in `-an`, so the segment carries no audio at all. Two keys for identical bytes, so the single-flight lock (keyed by exactly that identity) could not collapse them and BOTH transcoded: 6 of 47 distinct (container, seek) keys encoded twice, each pair byte-for-byte equal (8972316, 10498618, 10781180, 9964804 — proof of two independent deterministic encodes, not one file counted twice). The doubled GPU + NFS load dragged 6.006 s segments from 2087 ms to 13383/15659/16240/16990 ms — BELOW REALTIME — which starved the continuous audio session reading the same NFS stream. It fell below one segment per `AUDIO_POLL_STALL` (3 s), so a live session was declared wedged and 404'd. The 12 s cold grace could not save it: `audio_session_progress` was a GLOBAL high-water mark across every session under the rendition root, so once any session had produced anything, a freshly-spawned seek session was judged on the stall budget while still opening a 36.7 GB source. Diagnosis cost: the handler collapsed the cache's error to the literal string "audio segment not ready" and logged nothing on that path, so which of three budgets expired took a code read; and two `out_bytes: 0, outcome=done` jobs sent the investigation down a false empty-encode lead — they were live-stream sinks, whose bytes the worker never sees.|B113/V63,V64. Four commits, instrumentation FIRST per ODD: `AudioWaitGiveUp` + `HlsCacheError::AudioNotReady` name the expired budget through to the HTTP body with `pharos_audio_wait_total{outcome}` on both outcomes; then the key fix (audio track keys a segment only when the segment carries audio); then the stall budget raised to 10 s and asserted against `SEGMENT_SECONDS`; then read-wait progress scoped to the sessions that could serve the request. `out_bytes` is now recorded only for sinks that can measure it, so absent means unmeasured and 0 means empty.
B114|2026-07-25|SYNCPLAY EVICTION — member "lace" was silently removed from a live watch party mid-episode; their next SyncPlay command answered `NotInGroup`. Not a socket fault: a deploy hydrated the group at 21:15:58, `apply_persist` stamped every restored member seen-now, and each therefore got exactly `MEMBER_TTL_MS` (150 s) to reconnect. The LAN member reconnected in 28 s. lace is on WAN and jellyfin-web's socket backoff took 162 s — `21:18:28 pruning unresponsive member (ghost)` → removed; `21:18:40 /socket connected + registered in hub`; `21:18:53 POST /syncplay/pause → session is in no group`. Twelve seconds. lace's socket did not genuinely drop until 21:30:42, twelve MINUTES after the prune, so the member was never unresponsive. The prune judged every member on one TTL, but a member silent WITH a live socket (a dead client holding a place in every readiness gate) and one whose socket is GONE (holding nothing, since `follower_ids` filters on `connected`) are opposite situations. Compounding it, `apply_persist` restored members `connected: true`, asserting a socket this replica had never seen — which both made gates wait on absent members and put every restored member on the short TTL.|B114/V65. `DISCONNECTED_TTL_MS` (10 min, sized for a WAN client's exponential backoff after a deploy) for members with no socket, ghost TTL retained for silent-but-connected ones; hydrated members restored `connected: false`, which is what makes the longer grace safe. The prune log now carries `connected` so the two classes are distinguishable in Loki.
B115|2026-07-25|CHAPTER THUMBNAILS 400 — `GET /Items/{id}/Images/chapter/{n}` answered `400 unknown image type`, seven in one burst while jellyfin-web rendered the chapter strip for a playing title, each carrying a distinct `tag` (so the item genuinely advertised chapter images the server then refused). The chapter route EXISTED; it was unreachable. It was registered after `/items/{id}/images/{image_type}/{image_index}`, and actix matches in REGISTRATION order — a literal segment does not outrank a parameter — so every request went to the generic handler, which parses the segment as an `ImageRole`, fails, and 400s. A stale comment in `register` asserted the opposite ("actix matches the fixed segment count, so ordering is not load-bearing here"), which is true only between routes of DIFFERENT arity and was the reasoning that allowed the ordering. Found by query, from the `WARN http request completed with error status` line that exists for exactly this purpose. NOT a defect, checked and dismissed during the same investigation: `otel.status_code: "OK"` beside `http.status_code: 400` is deliberate in `tracing-actix-web` (`root_span_builder.rs:77`) and follows the OTel HTTP convention that a SERVER span is not an error for a 4xx — forcing ERROR there would inflate Tempo's error rate with every client probe.|B115/V66. Chapter route moved above the generic one; the stale comment corrected in place so the next route added here does not inherit it. Guard drives the registered app, since a shadowed route is invisible to a handler-level test.
B119|2026-07-26|AUDIO AHEAD OF PICTURE FOR A WHOLE TITLE (Rogue One; not reproducible on titles that DirectPlay, which is why it first looked media-type specific). The segment slices that file with `rel = input_seek - start_seconds`, and `-ss` on an input is measured from that input's own start time, so `rel` landed 1.7 s too deep and every frame received audio from 1.7 s later. It affected the ENTIRE film rather than just the opening, because once a from-0 session exists `ensure_continuous_audio_covering` tries candidate `0.0` first and every later segment reuses it — which also explains why resuming mid-film looked better (seek sessions were near-exact). B126|2026-07-26|PLAYBACK DEAD ON A LAPTOP — jellyfin-web stuck perpetually buffering on a black screen (user report), while the server answered `200` to every request and logged a complete encode for every segment. Cause: the mp4 muxer inherits the track timescale from the encoder, and the two device routes set it differently ON PURPOSE — software carries `-enc_time_base 1:90000` (B118's exact-timestamp route) while hardware rejects that flag and is pinned with `-r 24000/1001` instead. Each is correct read on its own clock, but the scheduler load-balances ONE session's segments across every device and they all decode under the single init segment 0 produced (`seg 69/70/71 device cpu` beside `seg 64-68 device Nvenc:0`, same media, same session). Burning subtitles is what made it reliably visible: the burn pushed encode time past realtime (6.2 s per 6.0 s segment), NVENC saturated, and segments spilled to CPU mid-session — which is why Men In Black International played while Fringe S01E05 did not. B127|2026-07-26|TWO REAL SHOWS NEVER MATCH A PROVIDER, so they carry no poster, no backdrop and no episode stills (found auditing the metadata backfill after B125's TMDB fallback: of 178 shows, 166 gained an overview and the fallback rescued 31 that TVDB alone had frozen, but Code Geass and Forever (US) stayed `none` — Code Geass 154 episodes, 2 with a Primary, both self-generated video frames). B128|2026-07-26|H264 CMAF PLAYBACK STALLS MID-STREAM IN THE BROWSER (Attack on Titan S01E01, Firefox, ~41 s): `HLS Error: otherError / internalException` and `FFmpegVideoDecoder … avcodec_send_packet: Invalid data found when processing input`. Source is HEVC → transcoded to h264 and served as a shared-init fMP4 (CMAF) rendition. Proven by pulling a live session's bytes and decoding each media segment under its init: seg0/seg1 decode CLEAN, but seg6/seg7 are corrupt (`number of reference frames (1+4) exceeds max (4)`, `deblocking_filter_idc 5 out of range`, `illegal reordering_of_pic_nums_idc`). Per-segment device logs: seg0-5,8 on `Nvenc:0`, seg6/seg7 on `cpu`. The init's `avcC` is NVENC's SPS; the CPU segments are libx264 slices encoded against libx264's SPS, and CMAF strips inband parameter sets, so the decoder parses libx264 slice headers with NVENC's SPS → garbage. The scheduler load-balances segments across devices BY DESIGN, and burning subtitles (this title has PGS) pushed NVENC past realtime so segments spilled to CPU — which is why it reproduced under load and not always. The slice-header misparse errors are SPS fields (POC type, frame_num bits, ref structure) that no `-profile`/`-level`/`-refs` pin can align, so unlike B126's timescale this cannot be forced identical across encoders — the rendition must be single-encoder. V67 frame-completeness and V78 timescale both passed on the corrupt segments (neither reads the parameter set).|B128/V80. `device_supports` makes hardware ineligible for `H264 + Container::Fmp4`, pinning the whole CMAF rendition (init + every segment) to libx264 — realtime at `-preset veryfast`, self-consistent, GPU freed. Scoped to Fmp4: the self-describing mpegts h264 variants keep hardware. HLS_GEN_VERSION 11 -> 12 orphans the mixed-encoder cache. H265 fMP4 carries the identical latent hazard but no hevc CMAF rung exists today.
B129|2026-07-26|THE SELECTED AUDIO TRACK PLAYS THE WRONG LANGUAGE (user: Aliens shows English auto-selected yet plays Ukrainian; Campfire Cooking S1E1 shows Japanese yet plays English). The pick never reached the render path: the demuxed audio rendition (`/vp9/audio.m3u8`) is a separate HLS session whose track comes from the `AudioStreamIndex` on its request, forwarded from the `TranscodingUrl`'s query string. `stream_selection` put an `AudioStreamIndex` on that URL ONLY when the CLIENT named one on the request, and on first play jellyfin-web names none (it trusts the server's default and appends the index only after a manual switch), so `resolve_audio_rel` saw `None` and ffmpeg default-selected the container's first/default-disposition track — Ukrainian for Aliens (track 1 is default), English for Campfire (English default). Confirmed from the deployed pod: the AoT/Aliens `TranscodingUrl` was `master.m3u8?PlaySessionId=…&renditions=…` with NO `AudioStreamIndex` while the MediaSource set `DefaultAudioStreamIndex`.|B129/V81. `ensure_audio_index_param` appends the server-selected absolute index to the transcode URL when it carries none (mirrors the `StartTimeTicks` append), forwarded through master → audio group → `resolve_audio_rel`. Idempotent with a manual switch (skipped once the URL names a track). No cache/schema change. Live verification: user plays Aliens and hears English; Campfire hears Japanese.
B130|2026-07-27|SKIP INTRO INVISIBLE IN EVERY BROWSER — a viewer reported that Skip Intro never appears while Skip Outro does. `/MediaSegments/{id}` was correct the whole time (verified body carries both an Intro and an Outro entry), and detection had 6113 intro rows across 481 seasons. The client never asked: jellyfin-web's `MediaSegmentManager.onPlayerPlaybackStart` reads `MediaSource.HasSegments` and returns BEFORE calling `getItemSegments` when it is false — and pharos hardcoded `has_segments: false`, having added the field only to satisfy the strict kotlin decoder (B13). So the entire media-segment surface was unreachable from every browser, for every item, always, with no error anywhere: the request that would have failed was never made. Found by the ABSENCE of a request in Loki — a browser played an episode whose intro is stored at 322.3-342.8s and issued no `/MediaSegments` request in the whole session — then confirmed by grepping the deployed `main.jellyfin.bundle.js` for the call site. Fix: answer `has_segments` from the same two sources `build_media_segments` serves (a detected row, or a chapter whose title classifies), so the advertisement cannot disagree with the response, and log the verdict. B131|2026-07-27|INTRO DETECTION SEEDED NO SHIFTS — `align::compare` discovered candidate alignment shifts from an inverted index probed at `point ± InvertedIndexShift` (±2 as an INTEGER addend), while point acceptance is fuzzy to `max_bit_diff` (6 differing bits of 32). On a bit-packed chromaprint word those are different notions of similarity, so two episodes could share an entire opening — every point of it inside the 6-bit tolerance — and seed ZERO candidate shifts: the opening was never compared, not compared and rejected. Measured by replaying a real season's stored fingerprints: 9 of 10 intro pairs produced no shift, against 6 of 10 credits pairs matching at 91s on the SAME five files. Fix: bounded exhaustive search over every shift whose overlap could still hold a `min_duration` span (~2800 shifts x <=1400 points, milliseconds per pair); `SEGMENT_DETECT_VERSION` bumped so seasons re-run the cheap half over cached fingerprints, `SEGMENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` deliberately untouched. The synthetic suite had passed throughout because its filler was an arithmetic ramp, which DOES align under some shift — fixed by avalanching the filler, not by relaxing the assertion. Guarded by V83.
```
B132|2026-07-27|SERVER UNUSABLE FOR PLAYBACK DURING THE SEGMENT RE-DETECTION SWEEP — reported as "slowing the server down to the point of it being unusable for watching things" while the `SEGMENT_DETECT_VERSION` 2→3 sweep ran. Not an I/O problem: fingerprints were already cached, so the sweep was doing almost no disk work. `analyze_season` called `detect_season_verbose` twice DIRECTLY on the async runtime, after the `bg_io` permit covering fingerprinting had been dropped. That was tolerable while shift discovery was an indexed lookup; B131 replaced it with a bounded exhaustive search (~2800 shifts x <=1400 points per pair, every episode against every other), so each season pinned a tokio worker for a long stretch — starving the same executor that serves playback and HTTP — and, having no permit, ignored the regulator that parks background work while a client streams. The adjacent stage ducked correctly and the expensive one did not.|B132/V84. `detect_gated` holds a `bg_io` permit across both windows and runs them on `spawn_blocking`. Test `detection_stands_down_while_playback_holds_the_gate` drains the semaphore, asserts the task stays pending, then releases and asserts completion; verified to FAIL with the gate disarmed ("consensus ran while playback held every bg_io permit"). Mitigated live before the fix shipped by stamping the 1845 un-analysed episodes current so the sweep idled — reversible, no segments deleted.
B133|2026-07-28|A SHARED-INIT RENDITION'S URLs CARRIED NO IDENTITY FOR WHAT PRODUCED THEM, SO THE BROWSER CACHE OUTLIVED THE ENCODER CHANGE. `init.mp4` and every `.m4s` are served `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` under a URL naming only the item and segment index. When spec 003 moved CMAF H264 from libx264 to NVENC, clients kept serving THEMSELVES the previously fetched libx264 init while fetching NVENC segments, whose SPS that init does not describe — issue #114, but resident in the browser rather than on the server. Diagnosis was delayed by exactly that: the fault survived a full server-side cache wipe (`HLS_GEN_VERSION` 12->13) AND a playback restart, which was read as evidence that NVENC's output was malformed, and the feature was reverted on that reading. An offline box-by-box diff of NVENC vs libx264 output built with pharos's own argv then showed the muxes are STRUCTURALLY IDENTICAL — same box tree, `mdhd timescale=90000`, `trun sample_count=143`, `tfhd`/`trun` flags, one `moof`; the only differences are the expected `avcC` (52 vs 61 bytes, the longer NVENC SPS) and a 514-tick (5.7 ms) `tfdt` offset, and both decode cleanly. Nothing was wrong with the hardware output. Fix: embed `HLS_GEN_VERSION` in the init + segment URIs of every shared-init (fMP4) rendition (`rendition_qs`), so a generation change is a URL change and the client fetches a matching init instead of reusing an incompatible one. Guarded by V90.
B134|2026-07-28|CACHE HITS WERE UNCOUNTABLE, AND ONE OF THE TWO HIT PATHS WAS ENTIRELY SILENT. Found while investigating a viewer's repeated playback failures on "Disclosure Day" (HEVC/E-AC3 → h264 CMAF, remote Firefox 153). The server record was rich on the miss path — 490 `hls segment transcoded (cache miss)` lines carrying device, queue wait, frame deficit — and empty on the hit path, so the 647 segment requests the client actually made could not be split into hits and misses at all. Two defects: the fast hit branch incremented `pharos_segment_cache_total{result="hit"}` and logged nothing, and the post-lock re-check branch (a request that queued on the single-flight lock and then found the file populated by the winner) returned the bytes having touched NO counter and NO log — invisible in both the hit rate and the miss rate, while having paid the full lock wait. This mattered beyond tidiness: with V89/V90 fresh (cached artefacts outliving the rule that produced them), the load-bearing question was whether that session had been served pre-deploy bytes, and nothing in the record could answer it — the investigation had to fall back on fetching live segments and parsing their boxes by hand. Not a regression; the hit path had never been instrumented.|B134/V91. `record_cache_hit` on BOTH branches: counter `pharos_segment_cache_total{result,class,hit_path}` (`hit_path` bounded to `fast`/`post_lock`, `none` on a miss so the ratio keeps its series), histogram `pharos_segment_cache_read_seconds` (a slow hit off a contended PVC stalls a client exactly like a slow encode, while every transcode metric stays clean), and an `hls segment served (cache hit)` line carrying the miss line's identity fields plus `age_secs` and `read_ms`. The existence check moved from `try_exists` to `metadata`, so the mtime behind `age_secs` costs no extra stat. The miss counter gained the same `class`/`hit_path` labels for symmetry. Test `a_cache_hit_is_counted_and_labelled` installs a thread-local `DebuggingRecorder`, serves a pre-populated segment whose SOURCE PATH DOES NOT EXIST (so it cannot pass by accidentally encoding), and asserts the counter and all three labels; verified to FAIL with the increment disarmed ("a cache hit must emit pharos_segment_cache_total — it is the signal").
B135|2026-07-28|RE-LANDING THE CMAF HARDWARE AFFINITY WITHOUT A GENERATION BUMP WOULD HAVE REPRODUCED #114 FROM DISK. Found while investigating whether #139 was safe to re-land now that B133/#142 embeds `HLS_GEN_VERSION` in shared-init rendition URIs. #139 touches device.rs, options.rs and scheduler.rs and NOT pharos-cache — it carries no generation bump, and did not need one when first written because the bump lived in its companion #140 (12->13). The revert then bumped 13->14 (correctly: reverting an assignment rule is itself an assignment-rule change, V89), and #142 kept 14 while making it `pub`. So a straight revert-of-the-revert resolves the constant back to 13 and re-lands the GPU rule under a generation that already names libx264 output: measured 5042 CPU-produced `codec=26` (H264+fMP4) segments cached in the preceding 36 h, every one of which would then be served beneath an NVENC-produced init. That is exactly B128/#114 — a shared-init CMAF rendition whose segments and init come from different encoders — reached through the cache rather than through the scheduler.
