Safe where the first attempt was not, because #142 makes the bump visible to the CLIENT cache too — the first #139 changed the rule while browsers kept serving themselves a year-`immutable` init from the previous generation, which is what made its failure look like malformed NVENC output and cost a revert of working code. Evidence for the re-land itself: NVENC CMAF ran at 4.42x realtime (p50 1330 ms) against libx264 CMAF at 1.71x (p50 3463 ms) on the same rung, and the 215 NVENC CMAF segments produced while #139 was briefly live decode correctly under their own init (research R4: three independent NVENC encodes byte-identical `avcC`).
B136|2026-07-28|THE COLD-START PREWARM WARMED THE OPENING OF EVERY TITLE, INCLUDING ON RESUME — so on a resume it warmed three segments the client would never fetch and left the one gating playback cold. Not a partial miss but an INVERSION: the prewarm exists to keep a cold ffmpeg spawn (and the large NFS-backed source open) off the critical path of the first segment request, and on a resume it moved that spawn onto exactly the request it was meant to cover, while spending three background encodes on bytes nobody would ask for. `prewarm_cold_start` looped `0..COLD_START_PREWARM_SEGS` with no reference to the start position, though `resume_ticks` was already in scope at the call site and already rode the response as `start_position_ticks` (and, for native players, `StartTimeTicks` on the transcoding URL — B74). Measured live while investigating a viewer's repeated failures on "Disclosure Day": the session resumed at 91.9 min, playback began at segment 918, and the prewarm produced segments 0, 1 and 2. Episodic viewing is mostly resumes, so this was the common case rather than the edge one. Invisible because the prewarm logged only a debug line on FAILURE — a prewarm warming the wrong end of a film was indistinguishable from one warming the right end.|B136/V92. `prewarm_base_segment` resolves the resume position through `pharos_core::SegmentGrid` — the same grid the playlist enumerates and `serve_segment` keys on — so the warmed index cannot drift from the one the client requests; the prewarm then runs `base..base+COLD_START_PREWARM_SEGS`. A position at or past the end resolves to `None` and warms nothing rather than clamping to the last segment (clamping would warm bytes no client will request), and a missing duration also declines rather than falling back to 0, which would be actively wrong for a resume. An INFO line now records the decision (`resume_ticks`, `prewarm.base`, `prewarm.count`, `resumed`) so the choice is queryable. Test `prewarm_starts_at_the_resume_segment_not_at_zero` pins the measured case (5514 s -> segment 919) plus both sides of a segment boundary and the two decline paths; verified to FAIL with the old hardcoded 0 restored ("left: Some(0), right: Some(919)"). NOT fixed here: the prewarm still builds mpegts opts while browsers are served only h264cmaf, so browser prewarm remains unreachable regardless of position — tracked separately.

B137|2026-07-28|A GROUP WATCH FROZE ON A MEMBER THAT HAD ALREADY RECOVERED, AND PRESSING PLAY MADE THE HANG LONGER. Reported live: "the video stopped seemingly needing to buffer, however it was stuck until I seeked back". At 19:50:31 one member POSTed `/SyncPlay/Buffering`, so V19 froze the group and broadcast a bare `Pause`. It was not starved: every segment request in the window was a cache HIT served instantly (segments 123-130, including 124-130 at 19:50:36, five seconds AFTER the buffering report), i.e. the member already held ~42 s of buffer. It never sent `Ready`/`BufferingEnd` because it had nothing to send — the freeze paused its player, and jellyfin-web posts `Ready` only on a player TRANSITION, so a paused player awaiting a withheld `Play` can never signal recovery. This is the Buffy join-wedge (PR#64) one member over: that fix clears the SENDER's stale `buffering` flag, and here the stuck flag was on a non-sender, so `GroupMsg::Unpause` opened a readiness gate on precisely the member that could not ack (`pending=1` proves it — had the sender been the buffering one, the set would have emptied and playback would have started). The second press hit `if state.waiting.is_some() { return; }` and vanished. Worse, the press EXTENDED the hang: the B55 buffering anti-wedge armed at 19:50:31 would have force-resumed at 19:51:01, but opening a gate disarms that path (`if state.waiting.is_none()`) and starts a fresh 30 s `READY_TIMEOUT_MS` from 19:50:39 — healing moved to 19:51:09, ~37 s of dead air. A manual seek at 19:50:43 cleared the freeze wholesale and forced real transitions in all three clients (nine `Ready` posts), which is why seeking back was the only thing that worked. Second, latent: when the buffering anti-wedge does fire it force-clears every `buffering` flag but does NOT ack the gate those flags created, clearing the cause and leaving the effect.|B137/V93. FIXED in three parts, after the instrumentation shipped first. Diagnosis needed four Loki queries and hand-correlation of three streams because nothing was countable: `readiness gate opened pending=1 reason=Unpause` named neither the member held nor which of five situations opened the gate, a gate rescued by the anti-wedge logged the same line as one every member acked, the freeze broadcast a `Pause` indistinguishable from a user pause, and the dropped press left no trace at all. Shipped: `GateReason` (wire string kept as jellyfin protocol, `label()` split out so the five situations that all broadcast "Unpause" separate — `seek`, `buffering_unpause`, `queue_change`, `playlist_item`, `next_item`, `previous_item`, `restored`), persisted with the gate so a pod takeover keeps it; `pharos_syncplay_gate_total{reason,outcome}` + `pharos_syncplay_gate_wait_seconds` with every gate terminating in exactly one of `resolved`/`anti_wedge`/`superseded`/`replaced` (they sum to gates opened, so a dropped gate shows as drift not silence); `pharos_syncplay_buffering_freeze_total{outcome}` + `_seconds` with `recovered`/`superseded`/`anti_wedge`, where `superseded` counts freezes a human had to break out of; `pharos_syncplay_unpause_ignored_total{gate}` for the swallowed press. Gate-open, cancel and timeout lines now name the holdout member ids and whether a V19 freeze is in force. Tests assert the `anti_wedge` counter from a gate driven to its deadline, `superseded` from a freeze ended by a manual seek, and the dropped-unpause counter; each verified RED when disarmed, and both label sets asserted distinct (a collision between `anti_wedge` and `resolved` would sum a hanging group into the healthy bucket and silently disarm the alert). The named query for the next occurrence: `sum by (outcome) (pharos_syncplay_gate_total)` and `sum(pharos_syncplay_unpause_ignored_total{gate="buffering_unpause"})`. FIX: (1) an explicit `Unpause` supersedes the freeze outright, as `PauseShared` already does — the sender-clear's own reasoning ("the sender undergoes no transition, so a gate opened on it self-deadlocks") applies to every captured member, since the freeze pauses them all; V19 is preserved by RE-REPORT rather than by holding, and the test asserts the stalled member re-freezes the group immediately afterwards, so the fix cannot have traded a deadlock for a loss of buffer isolation. (2) `enter_waiting` caps a new gate's deadline at the earlier of `READY_TIMEOUT_MS` and any armed `buffering_since + BUFFERING_MAX_MS` — a gate may bring the group's release forward, never push it back. (3) the buffering anti-wedge resolves the `buffering_unpause` gate its own flags created instead of clearing the cause and leaving the effect (defence-in-depth: after (1) that pairing is reachable only via a snapshot restored across a deploy, and it carries no test because the state can no longer be built through the actor's message API — a fabricated fixture would assert itself rather than the behaviour). Two existing tests asserted the old gating and were moved onto the path where an Unpause still gates (a `BufferingStart` on an already-PAUSED group flags the member without engaging a freeze), so B57's BufferingEnd-resolves-promptly and the never-gate-the-author guarantee both keep coverage. (1) and (2) each verified RED when disarmed.
B138|2026-07-28|B137's FIX COVERED ONE OF THREE HANGS — the wedge class is any gate deadlocking on a member with no player transition to make, not just the freeze-plus-Unpause instance. Found by checking the previous night's film after the viewer said "we did get several": 7 freezes on 2026-07-27, most correctly self-recovering, but THREE 30 s anti-wedge hangs. (1) 22:10:34 — the B137 shape, and worse than the reported one: freeze at 22:10:34, press at 22:10:44 opens a gate, second press at 22:10:59 swallowed, the freeze anti-wedge clears the flags at 22:11:04 but cannot resume because a gate is open, and that gate then runs to its OWN deadline at 22:11:14. 40 s, two presses ignored. Confirms B137's fix (3) was a live 10 s contributor, not the theoretical guard it became after fix (1). (2) 22:12:14 — a SEEK gate on 3 members, two acked, `e08fb57b` never did; a press at 22:12:29 was discarded because `waiting.is_some()`; 30 s. Not a freeze at all, so B137's supersede does not apply. (3) 22:12:56 — an Unpause gate on one flagged member with NO freeze active (the 22:12:48 BufferingStart did not freeze, the group not being Playing), so `group_paused_due_to_buffering` was false and B137's supersede was skipped; 30 s. The common factor in all three is not the freeze — it is that a gate's only recovery is a 30 s timeout while the member it waits on may be structurally unable to answer, and a viewer pressing play during that window changes nothing.|B138/V93. Two changes, both evidence-led. Sizing first: 4 days of production gate open->resolve deltas show acks mostly under 3 s but tailing to 15.5 s and 18.1 s, so halving `READY_TIMEOUT_MS` globally — the obvious move — would truncate legitimate waits and start groups without members about to arrive; it was rejected on that measurement. Instead `GateReason::timeout_ms()` splits the bound by KIND of wait: `BufferingUnpause` gets `UNPAUSE_GATE_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 s, above the observed bulk) because a human is watching from t=0 and its holdouts are the members B137 showed cannot ack, while loading-client gates keep 30 s. Second, an Unpause landing on an open gate is no longer discarded: it acks the sender (which has just asserted readiness and will never signal any other way) and caps the REMAINING wait, one-directionally so the old spam-play-extends-the-hang bug cannot return and without replacing the gate. Covers (2) and (3); (1) was already covered. Three tests, each verified RED when disarmed — note the one-way test initially passed WHILE disarmed because it advanced a full grace window at the end, letting a re-armed deadline expire too; it now steps just past the first press's cap, the only interval that separates the two behaviours. A test that cannot fail is not evidence.
B139|2026-07-28|THE COLD-START PREWARM BUILT MPEGTS OPTIONS WHILE BROWSERS ARE SERVED ONLY CMAF, so browser prewarm was unreachable for every session regardless of position. A web client on h264 gets the demuxed all-fMP4 master (the `h264cmaf` rung, PR#70) and never requests an `/hls1/*.ts` segment; `prewarm_cold_start` called `build_segment_opts`, which hard-forces `SegmentContainer::Mpegts` + muxed audio for any video item because that is correct for the .ts route it serves. So each browser session spent three BACKGROUND encodes on segments no client would fetch AND left the cold ffmpeg spawn (plus the large NFS-backed source open) on the very first request the prewarm exists to absorb — both halves of the waste, on the commonest playback path. Identical in shape to B136 and it SURVIVED B136: that fix corrected WHERE the prewarm aimed (segment index on a resume) without touching WHAT it built (container), and the two failures compose — a resumed browser session warmed the wrong segments of the wrong rendition. Invisible for the same reason as B136: the prewarm's only signal is that segments get produced, which is equally true when they are unreachable.|B139/V92 (third application). `fmp4_segment_opts` split into `fmp4_segment_opts_resolved`, taking the bitrate cap and session burn set as parameters rather than reading them off an `HttpRequest`, so the prewarm — which already holds both from PlaybackInfo — derives the key through the SAME function the CMAF route uses instead of reconstructing it. The cap handling is the trap and had to differ: the mpegts arm post-clamps a computed bitrate while the CMAF path folds the cap through `effective_video_bitrate`, so reusing the post-clamp would have produced a valid-LOOKING fMP4 key that missed on every fetch. The `demuxed_cmaf` predicate at the call site mirrors `master_playlist`'s `demuxed_h264` exactly (web client, non-audio, first ranked rendition h264) and reads `renditions` — the same value `select_master_video` parses back off the URL that very response returns, so the prewarm and the master cannot disagree without the response contradicting itself. The chosen rendition is logged. Test `a_browser_prewarm_builds_the_key_the_cmaf_route_requests` pins container, audio-free delivery, the cap fold and the grid window, asserts the mpegts rung unchanged AND asserts the two containers DISTINCT so it cannot pass by the renditions collapsing; verified RED with the prewarm pointed back at `build_segment_opts`. Deliberately NOT asserted: equality of the prewarm and route keys — they now call one function with one set of inputs, so that comparison would be tautology rather than evidence.

B140|2026-07-29|ANDROID TV PLAYS THE FIRST 8 MiB OF A DIRECTPLAY TITLE AND THEN GIVES UP — reported as "the Android TV app cannot play Central Intelligence" while Batman played fine. Every server signal said the negotiation was healthy: `decision: direct_play`, `direct_play_block: not_blocked`, `downgrade: none`, and three 2xx `/videos/{id}/stream` responses (one 200, two 206). The client then made no further request for that item and the viewer moved on to another title. Cause: the 8 MiB `DIRECTPLAY_RANGE_CAP_BYTES` window added for the Deadpool report (a browser `<video>` stalling a reverse proxy on a multi-GB `bytes=X-` body) was applied to EVERY client. A browser MSE source buffer treats a short response as "this window is done" and issues the next `bytes=X-`, which is what makes the cap work; a native progressive player does not — ExoPlayer's `DefaultHttpDataSource` sizes its read from the response's `Content-Length` and returns `RESULT_END_OF_INPUT` to the extractor once that many bytes are consumed, so the film simply *ends* at 8 MiB. The delivery path was silent, which is why this looked like a codec problem: PlaybackInfo logged fourteen fields and the code choosing the actual bytes logged one `debug!` on one branch.|B140/V94. Instrumentation first, as its own commit: `pharos_directplay_delivery_total{delivery,client}` + a `directplay: delivery decision` line carrying the requested Range, the file size, the served window and whether that window stops short of EOF, recorded on every branch. Then the fix: `DirectPlayClient::caps_ranges()` restricts the cap to `Browser`, so a native player's open-ended range is answered with the whole tail. Guard test `native_open_ended_range_is_served_whole` was verified red by disarming the gate (it returned `bytes 0-8388607/8388708` instead of the full `bytes 0-8388707/8388708`).

B141|2026-07-29|MUSIC GENRES VIEW SPINS FOREVER — selecting any genre under Music showed a spinner that never resolved. jellyfin-web's music library calls `ApiClient.getMusicGenres` → `GET /MusicGenres`, an endpoint pharos never registered; the 404 rejected a promise with no `.catch` on it, so nothing ever cleared the loading state. Same class as the Edit Images spinner and the missing `DELETE /Videos/ActiveEncodings`: a jellyfin-web flow dies silently on a missing endpoint rather than reporting an error, so the symptom names the view and not the route. Serving `/Genres` under the alias would have been wrong — it lists every film and television genre, which is not what the tab means.|B141/V96. New `list_music_genres` handler: ids and names come from `genres_with_counts` (so a music genre's `Id` is byte-identical to the `/Genres` one and the `/Items?ParentId=` pivot resolves unchanged), while membership and counts are recomputed over `MediaKind::Audio` items, splitting `probe.genre` on the `split_genre_field` convention. `Type` is `MusicGenre`. Guarded by a test seeding a mixed library and asserting a video-only genre never appears and the count is audio items rather than every item carrying the genre.

B142|2026-07-29|MUSICBRAINZ MATCHED FEWER THAN HALF THE ALBUMS IT SHOULD HAVE — found by running the verification query on the first live pass of T108 rather than by a report: 25 `hit` against 22 `no_candidates`, 3 `no_cover_art`, 6 `unavailable`, 1 `no_album_tag`. The misses were not obscure music, they were ordinary albums whose tags carry pressing detail: `A Rush Of Blood To The Head (Japan)`, `Invaders Must Die [Bonus Tracks]`, `Prospekts March (CD EP UK & Europe)`, `Talk (Remixes) (CDr Promo) US`. A MusicBrainz release GROUP is the abstract album — those qualifiers belong to an individual release, so a title wearing one matches no release-group at all. Second cause: every `Ministry of Sound` compilation missed because the query narrowed on `artist:"Various Artists"`, and MusicBrainz credits a compilation to its actual contributors, so naming the placeholder EXCLUDES the release being searched for. Third, the 6 `unavailable` lines said only `error sending request for url (...)` — reqwest's `Display` names the request and stops, so none of them said whether it was DNS, TLS, a timeout or a refusal.|B142/V95. `search_attempts` builds an ordered ladder — exact tag, then the title with trailing bracketed qualifiers stripped, then title-only — walked ONLY while an attempt returns zero candidates, so a clean tag that matches still costs exactly one rate-limited request and the later rungs are paid for solely by albums that would otherwise have stayed blank. Compilation placeholders (`Various Artists`/`VA`/`Soundtrack`/...) drop the artist clause. Scoring uses the stripped title so removed qualifiers don't count against similarity. `error_chain` walks `source()` so the underlying cause reaches the log. Stripping refuses to eat a wholly-bracketed title or an interior bracket. Every stripping test uses a title that genuinely missed in production.

B143|2026-07-29|B142'S BETTER QUERIES WOULD HAVE DONE NOTHING FOR 30 DAYS. Caught by reasoning about the deploy rather than by a symptom: the album-art pass stamps a miss so the album's other eleven tracks don't each re-run a rate-limited search, and the TTL then holds that verdict for `refresh_ttl_days` (30). Correct for a stable query, wrong the moment the query improves — the 22 albums B142 fixes had ALREADY been stamped by the first live pass, so shipping the fix would have changed nothing visible until late August, and the natural conclusion would have been "the fix didn't work". A stamped verdict is only as good as the question that produced it.|B143/V97. `ALBUM_ART_QUERY_VERSION` (1 -> 2 for B142) is stamped into `match_external_id` as `miss-v{N}` beside every miss, and `audio_items_needing_art` re-admits any `none` row whose marker differs from the current one REGARDLESS of the TTL. An unversioned stamp (what the first release wrote) counts as stale, which is what reaches the existing rows. A miss reached by the CURRENT version is still held for the full TTL, so an album that genuinely has no artwork is not re-searched every pass — asserted by a test in each direction, plus a backend-conformance arm covering both engines.

B144|2026-07-29|A TAG CLIPPED MID-WORD MATCHES NOTHING, HOWEVER CLOSE IT IS. Seen in the second live pass after B142/B143 (hits 25 -> 65): `The Prodigy - Always Outnumbered Never Outgu` missed every rung. The album is `Always Outnumbered, Never Outgunned`; the tag was truncated by whatever wrote it. Every rung of the B142 ladder is a QUOTED Lucene phrase, which matches exactly, so a clipped title finds zero candidates and the confidence floor never gets a chance — `title_similarity` scores the pair at 0.88, comfortably above the 0.85 floor, if only the search returned it. Also confirmed NOT fixed by this and left honestly open: `Ministry of Sound: Anthems II: 1991-2009` still misses, because MusicBrainz indexes the album without its label prefix and the resulting similarity (~0.53) is correctly below the floor — accepting it would need a rule that guesses which leading words are a label, which is how a wrong cover gets shipped.|B144/V95. A final UNQUOTED rung, `releasegroup:(words...)`, appended to the ladder: Lucene scores partial matches where a phrase match cannot. Lucene operators (`-` = NOT, `:` = field, unbalanced brackets = parse error) are DROPPED rather than escaped, since there is no phrase to preserve; a title with nothing searchable left yields no rung at all rather than a query matching everything. Safe only because acceptance stays gated on `MIN_ALBUM_CONFIDENCE` — a loose query returns loose candidates and the similarity floor is what stops one becoming a wrong cover. `ALBUM_ART_QUERY_VERSION` 2 -> 3 so B143 re-admits every existing miss.

B145|2026-07-29|B144'S UNQUOTED RUNG WAS INERT FOR THE CASE IT WAS WRITTEN FOR, AND MUSICBRAINZ WAS THROTTLING US. Caught by verifying B144 in production instead of trusting its reasoning. `The Prodigy - Always Outnumbered Never Outgu` still recorded `no_candidates` with the ladder visibly ending in `releasegroup:(Always Outnumbered Never Outgu)`. Cause: MusicBrainz's search ANDs bare terms, so the unquoted rung STILL required the clipped token `Outgu` to match a real word — dropping the quotes fixes phrase-vs-token matching, which was never the problem. B144's premise (`title_similarity` scores 0.88, so the match is acceptable) was correct and its remedy did not deliver a candidate to score. Two more defects in the same evidence: `Coldplay - Prospekt's March` produced `releasegroup:(Prospekt s March)` because the tokeniser split on the apostrophe, ANDing against a stray `s`; and six `unavailable` lines read `release-group search returned 503`, i.e. MusicBrainz asking us to slow down — their limiter is a burst budget, and B142's ladder had quietly turned one request per album into up to four.|B145/V95. The unquoted rung wildcards its FINAL token (`Outgu*` matches `Outgunned`), gated on `MIN_WILDCARD_STEM` = 4 so a short token can't wildcard half the database into the similarity floor. Apostrophes stay inside a word. A 503 backs off — honouring `Retry-After` (delta-seconds only, clamped 1..60) or 5s — retries ONCE, and pushes the shared `RateGate` out via `delay()` so every other album waits too rather than walking into the same throttle; counted as `pharos_album_art_throttled_total`. `ALBUM_ART_QUERY_VERSION` 3 -> 4. LESSON, and the reason V97 exists: the first verification (25 -> 65 hits) proved the pass worked and was then used to assume the NEXT change worked too. It did not, and only a second query showed it.
