Whoever learned a playlist's wire id could read its contents, rewrite it or delete it.|V198. `services::access::check_playlist` admits the owner, and for a server-owned playlist (or one whose owner was deleted) any reader and an admin writer. Every playlist route goes through `playlists::playlist_for`, and `/Items/{id}` checks it on the playlist branch; a refusal answers as a missing playlist does (404 on `/Playlists`, 400 "invalid id" on `/Items/{id}`, the same as any unknown id), logs a WARN with the playlist, owner, user and action, and counts under `pharos_playlist_access_denied_total{action=read|write}`. `ChildCount` on the header, `/Items/{id}` and the listing counts only entries the reader may open (`playlists::visible_entry_count`). Guarded by `it_feature::playlists::{a_playlist_answers_only_its_owner, an_admin_manages_only_an_orphaned_playlist, items_by_id_answers_another_users_playlist_as_nothing, a_playlists_child_count_is_what_its_reader_may_open}`.
B260|2026-11-02|ANY SIGNED-IN USER COULD MAKE THE SERVER FETCH ANY URL, INSIDE THE NETWORK INCLUDED. With `[remote].enabled` (off by default), `POST /Pharos/Remote/Items` ran yt-dlp on the posted URL for any bearer, admin or not, and checked nothing about where it pointed: `http://169.254.169.254/…`, the Loki endpoint or any LAN service was requested from the server, and yt-dlp's error text came back in the 502.|V188, V199. The route is admin-only (403, `pharos_remote_ingest_total{outcome="not_admin"}`). `remote::check_target` runs inside `ResolverCache::describe`, before yt-dlp: only http and https, no user info or backslashes, and every address the host resolves to must be public (`remote::is_public`: not private, loopback, link-local, CGNAT, multicast, broadcast or reserved, a v4 address embedded in v6 judged as v4), unless `[remote].allow_private_hosts` names the host. A refusal answers 400 naming the host and address, logs a WARN and counts under `pharos_remote_ingest_total{outcome=refused_scheme|refused_malformed|refused_private|refused_unresolvable}`. Guarded by `it_books::remote_end_to_end::{only_an_admin_catalogues_a_url, a_private_url_is_never_catalogued}`. Open: yt-dlp resolves the host again and follows redirects itself, so DNS rebinding or a public page redirecting inward still reaches a private address; closing that needs yt-dlp pinned to the checked address or an egress proxy.
B261|2026-11-02|ANY SIGNED-IN USER COULD CREATE BOX SETS AND CHANGE THEIR MEMBERS. `POST /Collections` and `POST|DELETE /Collections/{id}/Items` took `AuthUser` with no admin check, though a box set is shared by every user: anyone could create one, add items to an existing one or empty it. Jellyfin gates collection management behind a permission.|V188. Each route asks `require_admin` first. `require_admin` now logs a WARN naming the user and counts under `pharos_admin_required_total`, so a refused admin-only request is visible by query. Guarded by `it_feature::jellyfin_feature_user_policy::collection_editing_is_admin_only`.
B262|2026-11-02|`/Genres`, `/Studios`, `/Tags` AND `/Persons` LISTED EVERY LIBRARY'S NAMES, AND `/Persons/{id}` ANSWERED ANY PERSON. The four listings read the store-wide `*_with_counts` rows and ignored the bearer, and `/Persons/{id}` resolved any person wire id, so a user restricted to one library (or by a parental cap) saw the genres, studios, tags and cast of every other library, with their item counts.|V200. Each listing goes through `services::library::visible_entity_rows`: for a user whose policy hides nothing the store's rows and counts stand; otherwise each row's count is over its items narrowed to the ones `apply_policy_scope` lets the user see, and a row with none is left out. `/Persons` with `ParentId` or `PersonTypes` already counted over the user's items. `/Persons/{id}` answers 404 for a person with no visible item and logs a WARN. Each withheld entity counts under `pharos_entity_hidden_total{kind=genre|studio|tag|person}`. Guarded by `it_feature::item_access::entity_lists_answer_only_through_visible_items` and `services::library::tests::entity_kind_labels_are_distinct`. A restricted user's listing costs one store query per entity row.
B263|2026-11-02|`/Items/{id}` ON A GENRE ID AND A PERSON'S PORTRAIT ANSWERED FOR ENTITIES THE USER COULD NOT SEE. After B262 scoped the listings, `/Items/{genre wire id}` (`synth_genre`) still returned any genre's name and store-wide item count, and `/Items/{person wire id}/Images/Primary` still redirected to any person's portrait URL. A genre or person wire id is a hash of the name, so a user restricted to one library could confirm a guessed name in another library, read the genre's count, and read the person's portrait URL.|V200. Both go through `services::library::visible_entity` (which `/Persons/{id}` now uses too): no query for a user whose policy hides nothing, otherwise one `apply_policy_scope` query over the entity's items. A genre or person with no item the user may see answers as an unknown id does (400 "invalid id" on `/Items/{id}`, 404 on the image; a failed check withholds the image too); a genre's count and its Genre/MusicGenre `Type` are computed over the visible items only, so hidden items do not show through either; each refusal counts under `pharos_entity_hidden_total{kind}` and logs a WARN. Guarded by `it_feature::item_access::an_entity_by_id_answers_only_through_visible_items`.
B264|2026-11-02|BOX SETS LISTED AND COUNTED MEMBERS FROM LIBRARIES THE USER COULD NOT SEE. `/Collections`, `/Items?IncludeItemTypes=BoxSet` and `/Items/{id}/Collections` read the store-wide `collections_with_counts` rows, and `/Items/{collection id}` answered any box set with its full member count. A box set is shared by every user, so a user restricted to one library saw the names of box sets holding only other libraries' items, and every box set's count included hidden members.|V200. The three listings go through `visible_collections` (`services::library::visible_entity_rows` with `EntityKind::Collection` over `collection_items`), and `/Items/{collection id}` through `visible_entity` (a `collection_wire_id` query under `apply_policy_scope`): a box set lists only with a visible member, its count is over those members, and one with none answers as an unknown id. Each withheld box set counts under `pharos_entity_hidden_total{kind="collection"}`. `/Items?ParentId=<collection id>` was already scoped. Guarded by `it_feature::item_access::a_box_set_answers_only_through_visible_members`.
B265|2026-11-02|`/SyncPlay/List` SHOWED, AND `/SyncPlay/Join` ADMITTED, ANY GROUP TO ANY USER. The list answered every live and persisted group to any signed-in user, and Join added the caller to any group id, with no look at what the group was playing; the `/socket` `SyncPlayJoinGroup` frame and a `Join` on the native `/sync/v1/ws` (registered on every server) did the same. A user restricted to one library saw the names, members and state of groups watching another library, and joining one sent them its queue.|V201. As in Jellyfin, a group is listed to and joined by only a user who may see every item in its queue: `GroupSnapshot` and `snapshot_summary` now carry the queue's wire item ids, and `syncplay::may_see_queue` checks them through `services::access::retain_visible` (an id naming no stored item hides the group). Every join path asks `syncplay::may_join`, which reads the queue from the group's actor or, when the actor answers nothing (it lives on another replica), from its persisted snapshot, and refuses a restricted user when neither is readable. A refused HTTP or `/socket` join answers the caller `NotInGroup`, which makes jellyfin-web leave group mode; a refused native `Join` (asked through `TokenResolver::may_join`) closes as an unknown group. Each refusal counts under `pharos_syncplay_group_hidden_total{route=list|join|socket|native}` and logs a WARN. Guarded by `it_feature::item_access::{a_syncplay_group_answers_only_a_user_who_may_see_its_queue, a_native_sync_join_needs_a_visible_queue}`, `syncplay::tests::{join_reads_a_remote_groups_queue_from_its_snapshot, group_hidden_route_labels_are_distinct}` and `socket::tests::a_socket_join_frame_needs_a_visible_queue`. Open: `retain_visible` applies `EnabledFolders` but not the parental cap (fixed with the other policy fields); `sync_play_access` is not enforced; a member may queue an item other members cannot see.
B266|2026-11-02|THE PARENTAL CAP AND TAG LISTS WERE NOT ENFORCED OUTSIDE `/Items`, TAG LISTS NOT AT ALL, AND `SyncPlayAccess` NEVER. `services::access` judged an item by `EnabledFolders` alone and skipped every check for a user with all folders enabled, so an item over a user's `MaxParentalRating` stayed reachable by id (details, streams, HLS, downloads, marking played) and in every list built from the item cache (resume, latest, counts, similar, playlists, artists, albums). `BlockedTags` and `AllowedTags` were stored and reported but no query read them. `SyncPlayAccess` was stored and reported but never read, so a user set to `None` or `JoinGroups` could still list, join and create groups.|V202, V187, V201. `MediaQuery` gains `tag_scope`, applied in SQL as `NOT EXISTS` / `EXISTS` over the item's tags matched by lowercased name; `apply_policy_scope` fills it from both lists, and `hides_nothing` counts either list. `check_item`, `retain_visible` and `visible_items` ask the store through the same scoped query (by id for a short list, the whole visible library for a long one; a folder-only policy keeps the per-library id lists). An item the full policy hides is always withheld, and probes with the tag lists, then the cap, lifted pick the reason label (`parental_rating`, `tag`, `not_found`). SyncPlay's queue checks skip only when `hides_nothing`. `syncplay::sync_play_allows` enforces `SyncPlayAccess` on `/SyncPlay/List`, `Join` and `New` (403), the `/socket` create and join frames (`NotInGroup`), and the native `/sync/v1/ws` through `TokenResolver::may_create` and `may_join`; an empty value reads as Jellyfin's default. Guarded by `backend_conformance` (tag scope, sqlite and postgres), `it_feature::item_access::{tag_lists_narrow_every_query_scoped_list, the_parental_cap_and_tags_hide_an_item_everywhere, sync_play_access_gates_listing_joining_and_creating}`, `socket::tests::a_socket_create_frame_needs_create_access` and the label tests. Open: a member may queue an item other members cannot see, and a member already in a group is not re-checked when its queue changes or their policy narrows (separate fix: the group must record each member's user); SQLite's `LOWER` folds ASCII only, so a non-ASCII tag name matches only in its stored case on SQLite. Only an item's own tags are matched: an episode does not inherit tags from its series, as it does in Jellyfin. A SyncPlay socket keeps the user it signed in with, so a policy change reaches its join and create checks only after it reconnects.
B267|2026-11-02|LIVE TV HANDED IPTV PROVIDER CREDENTIALS TO USERS AND TO ANONYMOUS CALLERS, AND IGNORED `EnableLiveTvAccess`. `GET /LiveTv/Channels/{id}/Stream` answered 302 with the playlist's stream URL in `Location`; IPTV providers put the account's username and password in that URL (userinfo, query or path), so any signed-in user could read them. `GET /System/Configuration/{key}` took no bearer and overlaid the stored section an admin saved, which for `livetv` holds tuner and guide provider URLs with their credentials. A malformed playlist error quoted the stream URL into the startup log. No live TV route read `EnableLiveTvAccess`.|V203. The stream route relays the upstream's bytes and `Content-Type` only (`live_tv::stream`); an upstream error answers 502 without the upstream body, and reqwest errors are logged `without_url()`. Each request counts under `pharos_live_tv_stream_total{outcome}`. Every data route asks `live_tv::backend_for`, which answers a user with `EnableLiveTvAccess` off as if no backend were configured (`IsEnabled` false, empty lists, 404 by id) and counts under `pharos_live_tv_access_denied_total{route}`. `/System/Configuration/{key}` requires a signed-in user and overlays stored values for admins only; others get the defaults, counted under `pharos_named_config_withheld_total`. The playlist parser names the line, not the URL. Guarded by `it_entities::jellyfin_live_tv::{channel_stream_relays_without_leaking_provider_credentials, live_tv_access_off_reads_as_not_configured}`, `it_feature::jellyfin_feature_inventory::a_named_sections_stored_values_are_admin_only`, `redaction::live_tv_stream_never_logs_the_provider_url`, `route_auth_audit` and `live_tv::tests::a_malformed_playlist_error_does_not_quote_the_url`. Open: an HLS playlist upstream is now refused (502, `outcome="playlist_unsupported"`) where the 302 played it, since its segment URLs would resolve against pharos or name the provider; serving it needs playlist rewriting. The public channel logo route still 302s to the playlist's `tvg-logo` URL, which can carry a provider token; it has no user to check. The relay follows the provider's redirects (reqwest's default of 10, Authorization dropped across hosts), so a malicious or compromised provider could point it at an internal host whose bytes reach live TV users; the provider is admin configuration. A relayed stream's bytes pass through pharos, so each viewer costs the server the stream's bandwidth.
B268|2026-11-02|A SYNCPLAY MEMBER COULD QUEUE AN ITEM OTHER MEMBERS MAY NOT SEE. `/SyncPlay/SetNewQueue`, `/SyncPlay/Queue` and the `/SyncPlay/New` seed applied whatever item ids the caller sent. V201 checked the queue only at join, so once a group was formed any member could put another library's item into it, and every member's player then fetched its name, artwork and stream. Group members did not record their user, so nothing could say what each member may see.|V201. As in Jellyfin (`AllUsersHaveAccessToQueue`), a queue change applies only when the caller and every member's user may see every new item (`syncplay::may_queue`), on the live, retry and recovery dispatch paths. Members record their user across the bus and the persisted snapshot (ADR 0032). A refusal answers 204, applies nothing, logs a WARN and counts under `pharos_syncplay_queue_refused_total{via=set_new_queue|queue|seed, reason=queuer|member|unknown_member|unreadable}`. Guarded by `it_feature::item_access::{a_syncplay_queue_change_needs_every_member_to_see_its_items, a_member_with_no_recorded_user_blocks_a_queue_change, a_new_group_is_seeded_only_with_an_item_its_creator_may_see}`, `syncplay::tests::queue_refusal_labels_are_distinct` and `group::tests::a_member_records_its_user_and_old_state_loads_without_one`. Open: a member that joined before members recorded their user blocks every queue change until it reconnects (its socket's resync fills the user in). A member whose policy is narrowed after joining blocks changes it may not see but stays in the group and keeps the queue it already has. The check reads the members before the change reaches the group actor, not inside it: a member who joins between a change's check and its application (or after the persisted snapshot that another replica's check read) was never checked against that change and receives it. Checking inside the actor needs the store there.
B269|2026-11-02|THE WEB UI SHIPPED UNOPTIMISED WASM. `dx build --release` keeps DWARF by default (`--debug-symbols true`), and binaryen 132 aborts re-emitting it (`llvm_unreachable` in `DWARFYAML::onEndCompileUnit`, SIGABRT on a plain read and write). dx logs `wasm-opt failed` and copies the unoptimised file, so `pharosUiBundle` built green and shipped a 3.34 MB wasm, 1.35 MB of it `.debug_*` sections.|V206. `pharosUiBundle` builds with `--debug-symbols false`, so wasm-opt runs (1.66 MB, no DWARF), and its installPhase fails the build if any shipped wasm still holds `.debug_info`.
B257|2026-11-02|A RESCAN FAILED WHOLE ON A PATH ALREADY HELD UNDER ANOTHER ID: `put_batch: transaction failed; retrying item by item`, `UNIQUE constraint failed: media_items.path` (postgres: `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "media_items_path_key"`), then `library rescan failed`. `scan_into` and `update_path` looked the existing row up by `stable_id(path)` only, so a row holding the path under a different id was missed and the file was inserted again under the path-derived id. Rows like that are the Playwright seed's (ids 1–9, written by `admin seed-playwright-user`; every e2e startup and periodic rescan failed, leaving the fixtures unprobed) and, by inference, a moved file rebound to its old id whose bytes have since changed, so the fingerprint no longer finds it. Live hit once in 30 days: 2026-10-12T01:31:41Z, a periodic rescan of `/var/lib/pharos/media` on an `Ebooks/` path while the nested `/var/lib/pharos/media/Ebooks` root was scanning too.|V207. On a miss by `stable_id(path)` both paths ask `MediaStore::id_at_path` and write the probe over the row that holds the path, under its id, counted as updated. Guarded by `it_books::rescan_held_path` (red on main with the UNIQUE error) and the `id_at_path` checks in `backend_conformance` on both backends. Not closed: two overlapping roots scanning the same file at once can still race a rebind against an insert between the lookup and the write.
B270|2026-11-02|`pharos-transcode probe::rate_tests::two_runs_on_one_device_agree_within_a_wide_margin` FAILED THE FULL SUITE INTERMITTENTLY (`just test-changed` on the 010 phase 1 branch: 3132/3133, passing alone). The test compares two back-to-back wall-clock `probe_encode_rate` runs of libx264 and wants them within 3x. T127 put the probes in a `max-threads = 1` nextest group, which stops them overlapping each other but not the rest of the suite. libx264 encodes on every core, so each probe still shared the CPU with however many tests nextest was running at that moment, and two probes a moment apart saw different load. On an idle machine the 120-frame encode takes about 270 ms and the 1-frame baseline about 16 ms, so the baseline subtraction is not what amplifies the noise; the load difference is.|V208. The probe override in `.config/nextest.toml` (default, ci and nix profiles) adds `threads-required = "num-test-threads"`, so nextest runs each probe with no other test beside it. Three full workspace runs passed 3133 of 3133 at the same ~19 s wall time. Still not proof: other processes on the machine can load it too. If it recurs, make the assertion load-independent, as T127 says.
B271|2026-11-02|ANYONE COULD SIGN IN TO AN ACCOUNT WHOSE PASSWORD IS EMPTY, THROUGH THE NATIVE SIGN-IN (`POST /pharos/session` and `Bootstrap.login`). Jellyfin `POST /Users/New` without a `Password`, and the admin ResetPassword, store the hash of "", and `BuiltinAuth::authenticate` accepts "" against that hash. Nothing reported such accounts to the operator.|V209. `password_login` refuses an empty password before any hash, with the wrong-password answer `PasswordLoginError::Refused(LoginRefusal::Invalid)`, logged as `native login refused` with `reason="empty_password"`. Startup runs `services::auth::report_empty_password_accounts`, which sets `pharos_users_empty_password` and warns with the names. Jellyfin API behaviour is unchanged (compat decision pending with the owner). Tests: `it_rpc::session::an_empty_password_never_signs_in_and_answers_like_a_wrong_one` (red without the refusal: 204 instead of 401), and `it_auth::empty_password_report` (three cases: names exactly the empty-password accounts, none, no users).
B272|2026-11-02|UNAUTHENTICATED LOGIN FLOOD STARVES ACTIX WORKERS AND HOLDS 19 MIB PER ATTEMPT, cause: BuiltinAuth::authenticate and dummy_verify ran argon2id inline in an async fn on the worker thread, and the per-user login throttle admits a fresh key for every unique username, so the flood is never throttled|V210. Password checks run via spawn_blocking, bounded by one process-wide Semaphore (MAX_CONCURRENT_VERIFIES = 4, VERIFY_QUEUE_TIMEOUT = 5 s, with a comment on the memory reason); a busy refusal is AuthError::Backend, which the throttle abandons, so it is not counted as a failed guess. Signals: pharos_password_verify_in_flight (gauge), pharos_password_verify_busy_total (counter), warn "password check busy: refused" with waited_ms. Tests: busy_verify_is_a_backend_refusal_not_invalid_credentials (fails with the bound removed: known user gets Ok), one_slot_serves_logins_in_turn_and_is_released.
B274|2026-11-02|ONE HOST'S IDLE UPGRADES MADE EVERY OTHER NEW CLIENT GET `429 too_many_unauthenticated` (login, quick connect), cause: the anonymous-connection cap (64) was global only, so one address's sockets held all 64 for the 30 s `AUTH_DEADLINE`|V212. An address may hold 8 unauthenticated connections (`MAX_UNAUTHENTICATED_PER_IP`), keyed by `ip_key` as the login throttle keys it, enforced only when `trusted_proxies` is set. Refusal is `too_many_unauthenticated_from_address` in `pharos_rpc_refused_total` and `limit="unauthenticated_connections_per_address"`. Tests: `limits::tests` (per-address cap with another address still admitted, release, sign-in, sign-out re-count, disabled mode); the cap test fails with the check disarmed.
B275|2026-11-02|A CLIENT SENT UNLIMITED INBOUND MESSAGES ON ONE SOCKET, and capnp-rpc's question and answer tables grew with each, cause: no per-connection bound on inbound messages|V213. Each connection has a token bucket over its binary messages (burst `INBOUND_MESSAGE_BURST` 200, refill `INBOUND_MESSAGES_PER_SECOND` 50). Past it the socket closes with `Policy` and `rate_limited`, the message is not forwarded, and the close counts under `pharos_rpc_closed_total{cause="rate_limited"}` and `limit="inbound_messages"`. Tests: `socket::tests::message_bucket_*` (burst then refuse, refill at the rate and no further) and `it_rpc::limits::a_flood_of_messages_closes_with_rate_limited`.
B276|2026-11-02|ANY AUTHENTICATED USER GREW THE TRANSCODE SESSION REGISTRY AND THE transcode_sessions TABLE WITHOUT LIMIT: each native Playback.negotiate and PlaybackInfo call minted a session row kept 6 h, cause: `register` inserted every negotiated session and nothing counted or bounded a user's live sessions|V214. `TranscodeSessionRegistry::insert` ends the user's oldest sessions beyond `MAX_LIVE_PLAY_SESSIONS_PER_USER` (32) inside the actor, in the same step that inserts the new one, so count, evict and insert cannot interleave; the durable rows are deleted and `register` cancels the evicted sessions' prefetch and Now Playing record. Observable as counter `pharos_play_sessions_evicted_total` and log `play session cap: ended oldest`. Tests: `transcode_sessions::tests::minting_past_the_cap_ends_the_users_oldest_session`, `concurrent_mints_never_leave_more_than_the_cap_live`, `eviction_is_counted_and_logged`; the first one went red with the cap disarmed (3 of 11 registry tests failed).
B277|2026-11-02|A USER WITHOUT ACCESS TO AN ITEM'S LIBRARY COULD WRITE ITS RESUME POSITION, PLAYED FLAG AND SESSIONS ENTRY VIA POST /Sessions/Playing, /Progress AND /Stopped, cause: THE PLAYBACK REPORTS USED THE CLIENT'S ITEM ID WITHOUT AN ACCESS CHECK|V215. Fix: `services::play_state` report and stop call `check_item` before any write, and the start route calls `require_item_access` in its handler; a withheld item answers 404 (native RPC: NotFound), nothing is written. Tests: `it_feature/playback_access.rs` (hidden item refused on all three routes with no user data written; visible item still recorded).
B278|2026-11-02|THE JELLYFIN-WEB ANGIE WROTE LIVE SESSION TOKENS TO LOKI. Its `access_log /dev/stdout;` used angie's default combined format, which logs the full request line and the Referer. jellyfin-web puts the session token in HLS segment, image and stream URLs as `api_key=`, so every playback logged a live token, and Loki held them for at least 7 days (seen 2026-11-02 in `{namespace="pharos", container=~".*jellyfin-web.*"} \|~ "api_key="`). pharos's own request log already redacted `api_key`; the proxy in front of it did not.|V216. The angie config logs through `log_format pharos_noquery`: path (`$uri`) only, with no query string and no Referer. The flake check `checks.angie-access-log` fails if the log format names `$request`, `$request_uri`, `$args`, `$query_string`, `$arg_*` or `$http_referer`; it was red with `$request_uri` swapped in. A request with `?api_key=SECRET` and a Referer carrying it logged neither. Tokens already in Loki stay valid until they expire (90 days) or are revoked.
B280|2026-11-02|ADMIN PASSWORD CREATES AND CHANGES HASHED ARGON2 ON THE ASYNC WORKERS, cause: `hash_password` was sync and the async handlers and CLI-driven async helpers called it inline, so only verify had been moved off the workers and hashing drew memory outside the verify bound|V218. `BuiltinAuth::hash_password_off_worker` takes a slot from the same `VERIFY_SLOTS` semaphore as verify, with the same timeout and busy refusal (`password check busy`), and runs the hash on `spawn_blocking`; every async caller uses it, and the sync `hash_password` remains for the CLI sync paths and test fixtures. The in-flight gauge `pharos_password_verify_in_flight` now counts hashes too. Tests: `auth::tests::busy_hash_is_a_backend_refusal_and_is_counted` (red with the slot bound bypassed) and `auth::tests::hash_off_worker_returns_a_hash_that_verifies_and_releases_its_slot`.
B279|2026-11-02|A USER COULD HOLD AN UNBOUNDED NUMBER OF AUTHORIZING PLAY SESSIONS ACROSS REPLICAS, cause: THE B276 CAP COUNTED ONLY ONE REPLICA'S MEMORY WHILE EACH DURABLE ROW KEPT AUTHORIZING FOR 6 H AFTER ITS MEMORY ENTRY EXPIRED|V217. `TranscodeSessionStore::trim_transcode_sessions_for_holder` deletes the holder's live rows beyond the newest 32 in one statement per backend (sqlite and postgres), excluding the session being minted. The mint runs it in `insert()` on the caller's task, after the actor reply and the evicted-row deletes, then removes each trimmed id from memory with the actor's `Remove` message and returns evicted plus trimmed (deduplicated) so `register`'s prefetch and Now Playing cleanup covers them. The registry actor does no store I/O, since it also serves every `get`. Racing mints may each trim; the trim is idempotent and keeps the newest `keep` rows plus the caller's own, so the end state stays within the cap. It orders by `updated_at` (unix seconds) then id, so a user minting more than 32 sessions within one second can lose a different session durably than the in-memory eviction (mint order) ends; no schema change. Observable as counter `pharos_play_sessions_trimmed_total` and log `durable play session cap: trimmed oldest`. Tests: `backend_conformance` section 10b on both backends (red on sqlite with the statement disarmed), `transcode_sessions::tests::durable_rows_past_the_cap_are_trimmed_on_mint`, `concurrent_mints_never_leave_more_than_the_cap_live` (bounds only), and `a_hung_store_during_mint_does_not_block_get` (red when a store call was put back inside the actor's Mint arm: `get stalled behind a hung store: the actor is doing store I/O`).
B281|2026-11-02|THE NATIVE UI COULD NOT OPEN ANY LIBRARY, SERIES OR SEASON, cause: `BrowseScreen` fetched `Library.detail` for the title before the listing, and `Library.detail` answered every synthesised id (library view, series, season, album) with `notFound`, so the screen showed "notFound: item not found" for all of them and the listing never rendered. The refusal logged at debug only, so nothing in Loki or Prometheus showed it. FIX: `Library.detail` resolves a synthesised id through `jellyfin::items::synth_item_json`, the function `/Items/{id}` uses, so both APIs agree and apply the same library and parental checks; an unknown or withheld id still answers `notFound`. Every refused native item call logs at INFO (`native item call: refused`, with `call`, `id_kind`, `reason`, `item.id`) and counts under `pharos_rpc_item_refused_total{call,id_kind,reason}`. `BrowseScreen` shows the listing under a fallback title when the title fails and logs the error to the console. Not fixed: the native `Library.query` lists a TV library flat (episodes, no series tiles) because `Kind::Series` maps to no stored kind, so the series and season screens are reached by URL or an episode's parents only|V220. Tests: `it_rpc::library::browsing_a_library_a_series_and_a_season_answers_detail_then_listing`, `synthesised_detail_follows_library_access_and_the_parental_cap`, `refused_item_calls_are_logged_and_counted`, the `browse opens a library from home, then a series and its season` Playwright case, and `pharos-ui` `a_browse_screen_without_a_title_still_shows_its_listing`.
B282|2026-11-02|A PHONE USER COULD NOT SIGN IN BECAUSE THE KEYBOARD CAPITALISED THEIR NAME. The login lookup was an exact, case-sensitive `WHERE name = ?` on sqlite and postgres, so `Ali` found no account `ali` and every attempt was refused as an unknown user, while the native sign-in form set no `autocapitalize`, `autocorrect` or `spellcheck` on `#signin-name`. The refusal logged only the lowercased throttle key, so nothing showed what had been typed. Jellyfin matches the name without regard to case and clients pad it with spaces. CORRECTION (2026-11-02T19:45Z): the capitalisation cause above is a hypothesis the live data disproved. `pharos_auth_dummy_verify_total`, which counts only lookups that found no account, never moved for the phone's three attempts (18:36:54Z, 18:37:14Z, 18:37:57Z); its first sample appeared at 18:39:30Z from two probe logins with a made-up name. The account was found every time and the password was refused. The case-sensitive lookup, the missing input attributes and the uninformative log were real defects, and the fix below stands, but they were not why that phone failed.|V221. The login trims the typed name and asks the store for every case-insensitive match (`UserStore::lookup_by_name_ignore_case`); accounts differing only by case are refused as `ambiguous_name` unless one is typed exactly; `#signin-name` carries `autocapitalize=none`, `autocorrect=off`, `spellcheck=false`. The "login failed" log gains `reason`, `name_len` and `name_mismatch`, and `pharos_login_failed_total{reason}` counts every refusal. Not fixed: user creation (`UNIQUE(name)`) still accepts names that differ only by case.
B283|2026-11-02|A MERGE NEVER REACHED THE CLUSTER BECAUSE THE NEXT PUSH CANCELLED ITS IMAGE PUBLISH. `ci.yml` set `cancel-in-progress: true` on `${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}` for every ref, main included. The 22b0093a run (#557) passed every job; its `publish images (ghcr)` step was cancelled at 19:30:24Z when d91a350a was pushed, so no image was published and Flux image automation had nothing to roll out. The d91a350a run itself was cancelled two seconds after it started, with no jobs run and no newer main run in the group (the next, ea4e9dee, came at 19:34:35Z); the cause of that second cancellation is not recorded — it was not a supersede by a newer run. Two merges sat undeployed until the next push. Signal: a `ci` run on `push` to `main` with conclusion `cancelled` (`gh run list --workflow ci --branch main --json conclusion,headSha`); the live pod's image tag lagging main's head.|V223. Main runs are never cancelled by a newer push: `cancel-in-progress: ${{ github.ref != 'refs/heads/main' }}`, so a newer main push queues behind the running one (GitHub keeps one pending run per group; a third push replaces the pending one, which the third push's commits already contain). Pull requests still cancel superseded runs.
B284|2026-11-02|THE DIOXUS UI CI JOB FAILED BEFORE ANY TEST: PHAROS COULD NOT READ ITS UI BUNDLE. `dioxus UI` jobs (#566 job 125502910568, #569 job 125512810162) failed at startup with `Error: Ui(Read { dir: "/nix/store/…-pharos-ui-bundle-0.0.0", NotFound })`; the path was also gone from the build node's store afterwards. Cause: the ARC runner pods talk to the host's nix daemon through its socket and mount the host store read-only, while `$TMP` is the pod's own `/tmp`. `just compat-dioxus-full` kept only the printed path of `nix build --no-link .#pharosUiBundle`, so nothing rooted it, and a `--out-link "$TMP/…"` (tried in #569) does not help: the daemon resolves the auto root in the host filesystem, finds it dangling and drops it. The node runs with `min-free` 50 GiB and sat at 56 GiB free, so any concurrent build triggered auto-GC, which deleted the bundle during the job's cargo build. The media tree survived because it is copied out of the store right after its build. Signal: a `dioxus UI` job log containing `Ui(Read` with `NotFound`, now preceded by the recipe's `ui bundle … MISSING` diagnostic.|The recipe copies the bundle into `$TMP/ui` right after `nix build` and serves the copy; a GC between the build returning and the copy is still possible but the window is the length of one `cp`. Before serve it prints the store path's presence, `nix path-info`, its roots, free space and the `/nix` mounts. Main's publish job for ea4e9dee (run 41511203098) failed the same way: it built both image stream scripts up front and the second, `stream-pharos-jellyfin-web`, was gone by the time pharos had been pushed (`No such file or directory`, then skopeo `loading tar component manifest.json: file does not exist`). It now builds each script immediately before streaming it and rebuilds and re-streams once on failure, logging `publish: stream of <attr> failed, attempt <n>, script <path> present=<yes|no>`. `nix shell`/`nix run` were rejected: they exec the command, the daemon socket does not survive the exec, so no temporary root is held. `compat-playwright-full` reads no store path after building it, so it needed no change.
B286|2026-11-02|THE ANDROID APP SHOWED NO ARTWORK. B249's fix (ADR-0031) made every item image GET and HEAD require a bearer, and the Jellyfin Android app's image loader (okhttp) sends none: no header, no `api_key`, no cookie. Every poster, backdrop and thumb it asked for answered 401 "missing token" (186 in the 24h before the fix, all okhttp; browser image requests were all 200 through the sign-in cookie). Each URL carried `?tag=`, but tags are an unkeyed hash of id, art version and role, so they could not stand in for a credential.|V225. Image routes resolve their caller through `optional_bearer`: no credential, or one that does not resolve, is served anonymously; a resolving one still gets the library policy (`require_item_access_if_signed_in`, person visibility). Subtitles, attachments and fonts keep V189. Not fixed: anyone can fetch artwork by sequential id again; keyed image tags (ADR-0040's chosen follow-up) would close that for clients that echo the tag.
B282|2026-11-02|A PHONE USER COULD NOT SIGN IN BECAUSE THE KEYBOARD CAPITALISED THEIR NAME. The login lookup was an exact, case-sensitive `WHERE name = ?` on sqlite and postgres, so `Ali` found no account `ali` and every attempt was refused as an unknown user, while the native sign-in form set no `autocapitalize`, `autocorrect` or `spellcheck` on `#signin-name`. The refusal logged only the lowercased throttle key, so nothing showed what had been typed. Jellyfin matches the name without regard to case and clients pad it with spaces.|V221. The login trims the typed name and asks the store for every case-insensitive match (`UserStore::lookup_by_name_ignore_case`); accounts differing only by case are refused as `ambiguous_name` unless one is typed exactly; `#signin-name` carries `autocapitalize=none`, `autocorrect=off`, `spellcheck=false`. The "login failed" log gains `reason`, `name_len` and `name_mismatch`, and `pharos_login_failed_total{reason}` counts every refusal. Not fixed: user creation (`UNIQUE(name)`) still accepts names that differ only by case.
B287|2026-11-02|THE NATIVE UI COULD NOT WALK A SHOW OR AN ARTIST: A TV LIBRARY LISTED EVERY EPISODE FLAT AND A SERIES LISTED ITS EPISODES, NOT ITS SEASONS (left open by B281), cause: native `Library.query` reached only stored rows, and `kind_filter` maps the kinds pharos synthesises (series, season, album, artist) to "matches nothing" (B153), so there was no way to ask for the groups `/Items?IncludeItemTypes=Series` and `/Artists` fold out of stored episodes and tracks|V230. A query naming exactly one synthesised kind is answered by `services::library::grouped`, which calls the folding the Jellyfin handlers now share (`items::virtual_shows_page`, `items::music_groups_page`), scoped by the stored query's parent, library and parental rules; artists and albums are scoped to the parent library (the Jellyfin `/Artists` and album-only paths still read every visible track). Seasons under anything but a series are refused (`notPermitted`), since season tiles key on number alone. Every native query counts under `pharos_rpc_query_total{shape,outcome}` (`shape` = stored, series, season, album, artist). The UI picks a layout from the parent's detail: series grid, show page with season tabs and an episode list, music library tabs, artist album grid, album track list by disc. Tests: `it_rpc::library::a_tv_library_lists_its_series_then_seasons_then_episodes`, `grouped_listings_follow_the_parental_cap`, `a_music_library_lists_its_artists_then_albums_then_tracks`, `native_queries_are_counted_by_shape_and_outcome`, `pharos-ui` `native::browse::tests`, and the Playwright cases `browse opens a library from home, then a series and its season` and `browse walks an artist to an album and its tracks`.
B288|2026-11-03|THE DIOXUS UI CI JOB FAILED AFTER EVERY TEST PASSED: ITS CLEANUP COULD NOT REMOVE THE RUN'S TMP DIR. #582's `dioxus UI (native on)` job 125820286168 passed 16 tests, then `rm: cannot remove '/tmp/…/tmp.…': Directory not empty` failed the recipe. `just compat-dioxus-full`'s cleanup sent SIGTERM and polled 10 s for pharos to exit, but pharos's SIGTERM path waits `drain_grace_secs` (default 10) for a load balancer before actix's shutdown, so pharos (and its transcode workers) were still writing into `$TMP` when `rm -rf` ran; locally the exit took 10.07 s. Signal: a passing Playwright summary followed by `Directory not empty` in a `dioxus UI` job log.|The run's config sets `drain_grace_secs = 0`; the cleanup waits up to 35 s for pharos (actix's `shutdown_timeout` is 30 s), SIGKILLs only past that with a log line, stops and waits for the transcode-worker children and the serve job, and retries `rm` once. Verified by two back-to-back local runs, both passing with the tmp dir removed.
B289|2026-11-02|A RESCAN KEPT A DELETED 3D EDITION. Grouped editions (`Film (2010) - 3D.mkv` beside `Film (2010).mkv`) are stored on the primary's `alternate_sources`, and `has_3d` is computed from them when the row is put. The incremental scan skipped a primary whose own mtime, size and probe schema version were unchanged without looking at its editions, so deleting, adding or replacing an edition file left `alternate_sources` and `has_3d` stale: the item still listed a source that no longer existed and still matched `Is3D`. Nothing recorded the skip.|V232. `FsScanner::scan_into` compares the stored editions (`MediaStore::alternate_sources`, one single-column select) with the grouped files on disk before it skips a primary and re-puts it when a path was removed or added or a recorded size differs. Signal: `sum by (reason) (increase(pharos_scan_reprobe_total[1h]))` (`edition_removed|edition_added|edition_changed`, from `ReprobeReason::label`, asserted distinct) and the info line "edition set changed since the stored row was written" with `media.id`, `path`, `reason`, `edition` and `outcome`. Guarded by `fs::tests::a_rescan_drops_an_edition_whose_file_was_deleted`, `a_rescan_picks_up_an_edition_added_beside_a_scanned_primary`, `a_rescan_refreshes_an_edition_whose_file_changed_size`, `an_unchanged_rescan_of_a_grouped_primary_still_skips` and `it_items::rescan_3d_edition::a_deleted_3d_edition_stops_matching_is_3d_after_a_plain_rescan`. Not fixed: `AlternateMediaSource` records no mtime, so an edition replaced by a file of the same size is only caught by `--force`; an edition whose probe fails is never stored and re-probes its primary on every scan; the watcher (`update_path`) never groups editions at all.
B290|2026-11-02|A TRANSCODE OF A BLU-RAY 3D RIP WAS ADVERTISED AS 3D BUT CAME OUT 2D. ffmpeg cannot decode the H.264 MVC second view (it decodes only the base view), so a transcode of a `Video3DFormat::Mvc` source is 2D by construction whatever `StereoOutput` says; a packed source under `StereoOutput::Mono2D` is likewise a left-eye crop scaled back up. PlaybackInfo copied `video_3d_format` from the probe onto the MediaSource and its video MediaStream regardless of the playback decision, so a client was told MVC (or HalfSideBySide) and handed 2D through the TranscodingUrl, and a 3D-aware player could split a 2D frame or badge a 2D stream. An MVC transcode with `Preserve` also left `stereo_mode=block_lr` on the output. The 3D decision counter recorded only the request, so nothing showed how often this happened.|V233. `pharos_transcode::Output3D::of(format, effective_stereo_output)` answers what a transcode does to a 3D source (`preserved|flattened_mvc|flattened_mono`), with `TranscodeOptions::output_video_3d_format`. PlaybackInfo advertises `Output3D::format` on the MediaSource and its video MediaStream (alternates are always transcoded and follow the same rule); direct play and direct stream keep the source format. `build_args` clears `stereo_mode` for every flattened output. Signal: `sum by (format, output) (increase(pharos_playback_3d_decision_total{decision="transcode"}[1d]))`, where `output="flattened_mvc"` counts clients told MVC and given 2D before this fix; the "resolved 3D playback decision" log (`target=pharos::playback::3d`) carries `output` and `advertised_video_3d_format`. Guard: it_items `jellyfin_3d::{mvc_transcode_advertises_no_3d_format,mvc_direct_play_keeps_mvc,packed_transcode_keeps_its_format_unless_flattened_to_mono,the_3d_decision_counter_says_what_the_output_is}`, the `Output3D` unit tests and `mvc_preserve_transcode_clears_stereo_mode_but_packed_preserve_keeps_it`. Not fixed: Blu-ray 3D transcodes stay 2D (ADR-0043).
B291|2026-11-02|TURNING ON A SUBTITLE ON A PACKED 3D FILM DROPPED THE 3D. The viewer lost 3D for the whole session just for reading subtitles. It was visible only as `pharos_playback_3d_decision_total{reason="subtitle_burn",stereo_output="mono2d"}`. The comment on `effective_stereo_output` cited B239 for the rule; B239 is the `Is4K` misclassification, the rule is V183.|V234. The burn graph splits the frame, crops each eye, scales a half-layout eye to full aspect, burns the subtitle onto each, scales back and stacks them (`hstack` for side by side, `vstack` for top and bottom); an image burn splits the bitmap track once per eye. Only `StereoMode=mono` or a profile that rejects 3D gives `Mono2D`. The plan is reported as `reason="burn_per_eye"` in `pharos_playback_3d_decision_total` and the `pharos::playback::3d` log, with `format` and `stereo_output="preserve"`. Signal: `sum by (format) (rate(pharos_playback_3d_decision_total{reason="burn_per_eye"}[5m]))` for the new plan; the defect read `sum(rate(pharos_playback_3d_decision_total{reason="subtitle_burn",stereo_output="mono2d"}[5m]))` and must now be zero. Guard: the V234 tests, with the forcing disarmed once in `services/playback.rs`, `hls.rs`, `hls_cache.rs` and `per_eye_burn_format` in `options.rs`, each going red. Segments older builds cached under `_mono2d` are never served to a `preserve` request. Not covered by the real-ffmpeg run: an image (PGS) burn, whose graph shape is asserted but never executed; a bitmap canvas sized to the whole packed frame rather than to one eye would be misplaced.
B292|2026-11-03|THE NATIVE PLAYER WAS AN ISLAND IN THE PAGE, NOT THE PAGE. 013 US2 asks for a full-screen-first player whose chrome auto-hides, with next/previous; the #563 watch page drew the shell header, a heading above the picture, page padding and an `80vh` cap around a rounded `<video>`, so a 16:9 film on a desktop filled about two thirds of the window with empty page below it. Its chrome hid only when the pointer left the player and the player was not focused, so a click (which focuses the player) kept the controls up for the whole film, and there was no way to the next or previous episode but back through the series page. Cause: the player was styled as page content (`.pharos-watch { width: 100% }` inside `.pharos-main`), not as the screen. Fix: `.pharos-watch` is `position: fixed; inset: 0` above the shell, the `<video>` fills it with `object-fit: contain`, and the title and a Back control sit in a top overlay drawn over the picture like the control bar; the chrome and cursor fade after one to two `IDLE_TICK_MS` ticks without pointer or key activity while playing (a tap that only wakes it does not also pause); an episode shows previous/next buttons (and Shift+P / Shift+N) found by `browse::neighbours` across season boundaries, reading a season page after page and looked up once per item in `WatchScreen`; moving to one replaces the history entry, and the watch route goes through `remount_on` so the next episode is a new playback. Back calls `history.back()` only from an entry the app pushed (marked in `history.state`), else goes home. Signal: none server-side; the layout is client-only. Guard: V235.
B293|2026-11-03|TURNING ON AN IMAGE SUBTITLE IN THE NATIVE WATCH PAGE DID NOTHING, AND NEITHER DID PICKING ANOTHER AUDIO TRACK. Both need a new playback (a DVD/PGS subtitle is burned in; the `<video>` cannot switch audio), so `Player` asks `WatchScreen` to renegotiate. The server negotiated it (Loki: `{namespace="pharos"} |= "native playback negotiated" |= "subtitle_burn"`, three times for Fringe S01E01 at 17:45Z, once per click), but no request for the new session's stream followed: `WatchScreen` rendered the player as a lone `Player { key: session }`, and Dioxus compares keys only among list siblings, so the new props re-rendered the old `Player`, whose hooks (engine, subtitle signal, report clock) kept the first session and never attached the new URL. Fix: `remount_on` puts the player in a one-item keyed list, so a new play session unmounts the old player and mounts a new one. Guard: V236; `a_new_play_session_mounts_a_new_player` (red with the lone keyed node) and the Dioxus e2e `picking another audio track loads the new playback's stream` on the new `08-test-dual-audio.webm` fixture.
B294|2026-11-03|A BROWSE GRID STOPPED AT ITS FIRST 100 TITLES. `browse::load` lists a parent's children with `start_index=0, limit=BROWSE_PAGE` and the grid rendered `MoreNote`, "Showing 100 of N.", with no way to ask for the rest: a movie library of 101 or more titles had titles the native UI could not reach, short of searching for them by name. Fix: a `Browse::Grid` backed by one paged listing carries its `ListSource`; `PagedGrid` (`native/browse.rs`) keeps a `Pager` (`native/paging.rs`) and asks for the next page when an `IntersectionObserver` sees the tile 30 from the end, or when "Show more" is pressed. A mixed library (two listings merged by `series_first`) and the music, show and album layouts still stop at one page. Signal: a page past the first is `ui_action=more` on the RPC span, `{ui_action="more"}` in the server's call logs; a failed one is a `rpcFailed` client event with `route=browse` and the console warning `pharos: browse <id> more failed`. Guard: V237 (`paging::tests`, `browse::tests::a_further_page_is_asked_for_under_the_more_action` with the action string disarmed once and seen red, `a_failed_page_warns_and_reports_an_rpc_failed_event`, and the Playwright case `browse scrolls a library past one page and every title loads`).
B295|2026-11-03|SCROLLING DOWN THE NATIVE HOME PAGE MOVED THE SHELVES SIDEWAYS INSTEAD. `depth::rail_wheel`, written for 011 US5 ("mouse wheel scrolls rails horizontally on hover"), cancelled every purely vertical wheel turn over a rail and scrolled the rail by it until the rail reached an end. Continue watching, Next up, Books and Movies fill most of `/ui/`, so a wheel turn nearly always landed on a rail and the page barely moved. Fix: drop `rail_wheel`, `wheel_step` and the rail's `onwheel`, and amend 011 US5. The browser already scrolls an `overflow-x` rail on a sideways swipe or Shift+wheel, and touch, keyboard focus and the controller are unchanged. Guard: V238; the Dioxus e2e `a vertical wheel over a rail scrolls the page` (part-watches videos so an overflowing Continue watching shelf is always there; red against the old handler).
B296|2026-11-03|THE NATIVE PLAYERS IGNORED THE MOUSE BEYOND CLICKS. The watch and listen screens took a plain click (play/pause) and keys, but the wheel scrolled nothing and there was no mouse way to the previous or next episode or track, which desktop players commonly bind (wheel volume, Shift+wheel seek). Fix: over either player one wheel notch steps the volume 5% (`WHEEL_VOLUME_STEP`), Shift+wheel seeks 5 s (`WHEEL_SEEK_MS`); travel accumulates to `WHEEL_NOTCH_PX` so a trackpad's small deltas do not each count, and the larger axis counts because Shift turns some wheels sideways. Shift+click plays the previous episode or track and Shift+right click the next (a press that started on a control is that control's own, and Shift+right counts only when pressed and released on the player), with its context menu suppressed (Firefox shows its own regardless, the skip still happens); Shift+P / Shift+N do the same on the music screen as on the watch page. A track's neighbours come from `browse::neighbours` on its album. V238 now exempts the two player screens. Signal: none server-side; the input handling is client-only, as for B295. Guard: V239.
B385|2026-11-04|NATIVE PLAYER SUBTITLES WERE A SQUARE, NEARLY OPAQUE BOX AND ANIME TYPESETTING WAS LOST. The watch page never styled `::cue`, so a WebVTT cue took the browser default: grey-white text on a near-black rectangle over the picture. And every text subtitle reached the player as WebVTT, which the server converts an embedded ASS/SSA track to keeping only the words, so a release's signs, positions, fonts, colours and karaoke were dropped. Fix: `.pharos-video::cue` draws white text with a one-pixel dark outline and a soft shadow and no background (`::cue` takes no border-radius, so any box stays square). A picked track whose codec is `ass` or `ssa` is drawn from its original script at `stream.ass` (`native::playback::styled_subtitle_url`) by JASSUB 2.5.23, bundled and vendored per ADR-0057 and loaded only then, over the `<video>` inside `.pharos-video-frame`; its WebVTT track stays on screen until JASSUB is ready and comes back if it fails or is not ready within 45 s. Not fixed: fonts embedded in the file (MKV attachments) are not passed to the renderer, since the native API does not list them, so every style draws in Liberation Sans; sidecar `.ass` files are listed as `webvtt` and still play as plain text. Signal: the console warning `pharos: styled subtitles failed, showing plain text`. Guard: V325.
B314|2026-11-03|AN EPISODE IN A SCENE RELEASE FOLDER BECAME A SHOW NAMED AFTER THE RELEASE. `parse_series_info` names the show after the nearest folder that is not a season folder, so a download dropped straight into the TV root as `Person.of.Interest.S01E03.720p.HDTV.x264-IMMERSE/` made a one-episode show called `Person.of.Interest.S01E03.720p.HDTV.x264-IMMERSE`, and the home page showed that on its tile; two episodes of the live library were filed this way. Fix: a folder whose name holds a season and episode token (`S01E03`, `1x03`) or a season-pack token (`.S02.`) is a release folder (`parse_release_folder` in `pharos-scanner` `fs.rs`): the show is the cleaned name before the token (the filename cleaner drops separators and reads any year), the season comes from the token, and the folder above it is kept as the show folder only when its name matches; otherwise the show is keyed on a folder of the show's name beside the release, so release folders of one show group together. Rows already in the library keep the old name and series key until their file is re-probed (an item refresh, or a full-refresh scan), so until then an old row and a newly scanned episode of the same show list as two shows. Signal: none; the series name is in the stored row. Guard: V254.
B315|2026-11-03|A HOME PAGE RAIL TOOK A DOZEN WHEEL NOTCHES TO CROSS AND COULD NOT BE DRAGGED. After B295 a rail scrolled sideways only on the browser's own horizontal input, so a Shift+wheel or tilt-wheel notch moved it about 100 px, under one poster, and a mouse user without a horizontal wheel had no way to move it but the keyboard: the rail shows no scrollbar, and pressing on a poster and dragging started the browser's drag of the link. Fix: `rails::wheel_step` takes a notch-sized sideways turn (Shift held and at least 40 px, purely horizontal and at least 100 px, or any turn the browser reports in lines or pages) over a rail and glides the rail three times as far, adding quick notches up; a plain vertical wheel and a trackpad's small or diagonal turns stay with the browser. A primary mouse or pen press that moves more than 6 px drags the rail, and the click its release makes is dropped so no poster opens; a shorter press is still a click. Touch keeps the browser's own panning. Guard: V257.
B317|2026-11-04|A LIBRARY IN RELEASE DATE OR DATE ADDED ORDER HAD NO WAY TO JUMP. V252 showed the letter rail only in name order, since letters mean nothing in a date order, so a grid sorted by release date or date added could only be scrolled, and Shift+wheel did nothing. Seen in prod: "Release date doesn't appear".|V256. `Library.index` sections a date order by year: release year (premiere date, else production year) for Release date, the year `created_at` falls in for Date added, `#` for neither; a synthesised series takes its newest episode's `created_at` as its `DateCreated` so its year matches where Date added puts it. The UI shows those years on the rail (every few, first and last kept, when there are more than 27) and Shift+wheel steps every year. Signal: none server-side; the sections ride on the index reply. Guard: V256.
B316|2026-11-04|DATE ADDED "OLDEST FIRST" LISTED NEWEST FIRST. Native `Library.query` sent one `SortOrder` token for a multi-key `SortBy`, and the Jellyfin listing reads a lone `DateCreated` with one order token by its legacy rule, where Ascending means newest first; the native grid's "DateCreated,SortName" took that route, so both directions came back inverted. Release date was right: its first key, `PremiereDate`, always takes the per-key chain.|V255. When the first `SortBy` key is `DateCreated` or `DateAdded`, `read_query` sends one order token per key, so the listing takes the per-key chain and Descending is newest first. Other orders send one token as before, keeping the legacy tie-breaks of a name or index order. The Jellyfin route is unchanged. Signal: none; the order is in the reply. Guard: it_rpc `library::date_orders_list_newest_first_when_descending` (failed before the fix on DateCreated ascending and descending).
B335|2026-11-04|EVERY EPISODE IN A SEASON SHOWED THE SERIES POSTER INSTEAD OF ITS OWN STILL. The sidecar provider probes the show folder for an episode and records its `folder.jpg` as the episode's Primary artwork (synth series and season tiles resolve through that row), while the episode's own `<stem>-thumb.jpg` is recorded only as Thumb. The native season list and jellyfin-web ask for an episode's Primary, so all of Red Dwarf series 1 drew the show's logo. Fix: `serve_image` answers a real episode's Primary with its own still first, a per-file sidecar beside the media (Primary before Thumb) and then a provider episode image; show-level art (`folder.jpg`, a season folder's `landscape.jpg`) never counts, and an episode with no still of its own keeps the series poster. The episode Primary tag hashes a new role key so browsers drop the poster cached under the old tag for a week. No rescan: the stored rows were right, only the choice among them was wrong. Live library at the time: 8620 of 12106 episodes have a `-thumb` sidecar and 1061 a TVDB still. Signal: none. Guard: it_entities `jellyfin_episode_images::episode_primary_is_its_own_still_and_the_series_keeps_its_poster`, pharos-jellyfin-api `episode_primary_tag_is_retired_and_matches_its_image_info`.
B313|2026-11-03|FILES STILL ON DISK DROPPED OUT OF THE LIBRARY AFTER A START. The server starts one scan task per root, all at once, and a typed library inside a mixed root (the B174 shape, which the deployment and the Playwright seed both use) is two roots over the same files. Each scan stamps the rows it sees with its own scan id and ends by sweeping the rows under its root that it did not stamp. The outer scan re-stamped the inner library's files while the inner scan ran, so the inner scan swept them as removed. The count lost varied from run to run: CI kept 58, 73 and 93 of the 120 Bulk titles on #598, and a run on main pinned to one CPU logged `swept rows for files removed from disk` with `removed=90`. The files came back on the next periodic scan, but a swept row's user data goes with it. Fix: `FsScanner::scan_into` and a watcher's create/modify batch take one process-wide gate, so scans run one at a time. SQLite has one writer anyway, so little parallelism is lost. Guard: V253.
B312|2026-11-03|A LIBRARY COULD ONLY BE READ TOP TO BOTTOM BY NAME. The native library grid (B294) listed everything A to Z with no way to reach "S" but scrolling through every page before it, and no way to narrow it to a genre or to what is still unwatched, or to order it by release date or by when it reached the library. Fix: `Library.query` takes a `genre` (the genre entity for films, the show metadata's genres for series) and its `IsPlayed` / `IsUnplayed` filters now reach series tiles (played once every episode is), which `virtual_shows_page` had dropped because its episode query is unpaged; a new `Library.index` (schema v5) answers where each first letter starts in a listing and the genres there are. The UI's `LibraryGrid` (`native/browse.rs`) puts a sort and filter bar over a library's grid (Sort: Name, Release date, Date added, with a direction; Genre; Watched) and, in name order, an A to Z rail on the right; Shift+wheel steps to the next or previous letter. A jump past what is loaded reads ahead through whole pages (`Pager::begin_through`, at most 1000 a request) so the grid stays contiguous. Mixed and music libraries keep their own layouts. Signal: `ui_action=index` on the RPC span for each listing's index, a failure is the console warning `pharos: browse <id> index failed`. Guard: V252.
B311|2026-11-03|HOME PAGE CARDS CUT OFF THE NAME AND THE EPISODE NUMBERS. A poster tile in a rail (Next up, Continue watching, a library's latest) is 9rem wide; its name was one `nowrap` line with an ellipsis, and an episode's series name and numbers shared a second line, "American Horror Story · S1 · E1", so a long series name hid the season and episode and a long episode title showed only its first words. Fix: the name wraps to two lines before the ellipsis (`-webkit-line-clamp: 2`); an episode's series and its "S1 · E1" are separate lines (`sub_lines` in `native/views.rs`), so the numbers are never behind a long series name; every line carries its full text as a `title` tooltip. Tile width is unchanged. Signal: none server-side; the layout is client-only. Guard: V251.
B310|2026-11-03|A PART-PLAYED SONG SHOWED UP IN THE NATIVE HOME PAGE'S "CONTINUE WATCHING" ROW. `services::library::home` filled `Home.resume` from `resume(.., KindFilter::Unset, ..)`, so every resumable kind came back in one list and the UI drew it under one heading; a song also took one of the row's 12 slots, so music could push films and episodes out of it.|V250. The watching row asks `resume` for `WATCHING_KINDS` (movie, episode); music comes back in the new `Home.listening` (`listening @3`, sized by `resumeLimit`) and the UI draws it as "Continue listening", just before the music libraries' rails. A book resumes in neither row. Signal: none server-side; the row split is in the reply's shape. Guard: it_rpc `library::home_resumes_music_apart_from_what_is_watched`, pharos-ui `music_resumes_under_continue_listening_not_watching` and `home_rails_run_shows_films_listening_music_books_then_mixed`. Not fixed: each home call reads the user's resumable ids twice, once per row.
B340|2026-11-03|THE NATIVE UI SHOWED NO RATINGS FOR FILMS OR TV SHOWS. Alison: "We are also missing IMDB and Rotten tomatoes ratings in TV Shows and Movies". The server stores a Jellyfin `CriticRating` (0–100; the Rotten Tomatoes Tomatometer by Jellyfin's convention, from NFO `<criticrating>`: 520 films and 330 episodes on the live library) and a `CommunityRating` whose source is mixed (TMDB vote average, TVDB score, or an NFO `<rating>`), but the native `ItemSummary` carried neither, so no title page or poster showed a score. Fix: schema v6 appends `ItemSummary.imdbRating @12` (IMDb score in tenths) and `criticRating @13` (Tomatometer percentage), both `OptU16`; `write_item` and `write_synth_summary` send the stored `CriticRating`, rounded, and drop a value outside 0–100. `CommunityRating` is not sent as IMDb because it is not one. The UI draws "IMDb 7.8" and "RT 92%" chips (`Ratings` in `native/views.rs`) for films and shows only, under a title's facts, on the show page's hero, and small in a poster's bottom-left corner (hidden from assistive tech there, so the link's name stays the title); RT 60% and up is fresh (red label, Rotten Tomatoes' brand colours), below is rotten (green). An out-of-range stored value is logged at debug and not sent. Not fixed here: a show's own scores, because `SeriesMetadata` comes from TVDB, which has no critic score, and a `tvshow.nfo` `<criticrating>` only reaches its episodes; and `imdbRating`, which stays empty until an IMDb source is stored. Both come from the OMDb adapter (B341), which needs an API key; the wire field ships now so that change needs no schema bump. Signal: none server-side. Guard: V280.
B375|2026-11-03|THE NATIVE MUSIC SCREEN BOXED ITS VISUALISER INSIDE THE PAGE AND COVERED THE MIDDLE OF IT WITH THE PLAYER CARD. The #590 listen screen drew the visualiser in a rounded `75vh` panel under the shell header, and a 56rem glass card holding a 15rem cover, the title, seek bar, transport, volume and style picker sat centred on it, so a Halo or Spectrum frame was hidden exactly where it is drawn. The watch page (B292) already fills the window. Fix: `.pharos-listen` is `position: fixed; inset: 0` above the shell like `.pharos-watch`, with the shell bar hidden and the page unscrollable under it; a top strip holds a Back control and the style picker, and the card is a compact bar along the bottom (5.5rem cover, one-line title, seek bar under it, transport and volume to the right). On a narrow screen the bar stacks the transport under the seek bar and keeps only the mute button. Signal: none server-side; the layout is client-only. Guard: V315.
B420|2026-11-04|THE TOP BAR'S ACCOUNT ACTIONS WERE LOOSE LINKS, AND MANAGE SHOWED FOR EVERY USER. Alison: "We should collapse the Appearance, Manage and Sign out into a button that can be pressed to show a menu". The native UI's top bar listed Appearance, Manage and Sign out side by side, and offered Manage to users who are not admins. Fix: one round account button at the right of the bar (`AccountMenu` in `native/account.rs`) shows the user's initials and opens a menu of Appearance, Manage (admins only, from `Session.me`) and Sign out; it closes on a pick, on Escape and on a click outside. `Shell` takes it as its `account` element. Until `me` answers, the button shows `?` and Manage stays hidden. Signal: a failed `me` logs the console warning `account: me failed`. Guard: V360.
B360|2026-11-03|THE NATIVE MUSIC PLAYER HAD NO LYRICS. The native API had no lyrics method, so the now-playing screen could not show any, and the server's only lyrics source was an `.lrc` sidecar read inside the Jellyfin handler (`GET /Audio/{id}/Lyrics`); words in the file's own tags (ID3 `USLT`/`SYLT`, Vorbis `LYRICS`, MP4 `©lyr`) and `.txt` sidecars were never read, and a plain-text line went out with `Start: 0`, so a Jellyfin client treated it as synced. Fix: one service, `services::lyrics`, tries an `.lrc` sidecar, then the file's tags (a millisecond `SYLT` frame first, read with `lofty`), then a `.txt` sidecar, then, only when `[lyrics].online` / `PHAROS_LYRICS_ONLINE` is on, LRCLIB behind the `OnlineLyrics` trait (answers remembered per item, errors not). The LRC parser handles repeated stamps, `[offset:]`, word stamps and two- or three-digit fractions. `Library.lyrics` (schema v8) and the Jellyfin route both call it; Jellyfin gets `Start: null` on plain lines and `Metadata.IsSynced`. The UI's `LyricsPanel` (`native/lyrics.rs`) shows them over the visualiser on the now-playing screen, between the top strip and the bottom bar: synced lines light up as they are sung, stay centred unless the listener scrolled in the last 4 s, and seek when clicked; a Lyrics chip in the top strip hides the panel and is remembered on the device. Signal: `pharos_lyrics_lookup_total{outcome}` (`sidecar_lrc`, `embedded_synced`, `embedded_text`, `sidecar_text`, `online`, `none`), `pharos_lyrics_online_error_total{provider}`, debug log `lyrics lookup` with `outcome`/`synced`/`lines`/`elapsed_ms`; client log `pharos: lyrics lines= synced=`.|V300
B430|2026-11-04|THE WATCH PAGE'S CONTROLS TOOK TOO LONG TO HIDE, AND COULD STAY UP FOR THE WHOLE FILM. Alison: "the UI when watching things takes too long to disappear". The B292 chrome faded one to two 1.5 s ticks (up to 3 s) after the last pointer move, but only while nothing in it matched `:focus-visible` or `select:focus`. A picked subtitle or audio track left its `<select>` focused, and any key press while a clicked control had focus made that control `:focus-visible`, so after either the title, Back and control bar stayed over the picture until the viewer clicked the video. Cause: the sheet's `.pharos-player.is-idle:not(:has(:focus-visible, select:focus))` exemption, meant for keyboard users and an open track list, had no end. Fix: the chrome fades after four still 500 ms ticks (2 to 2.5 s, `IDLE_TICKS`) whatever has focus, and the player's `:focus-visible` ring hides with it; `TrackPicker` moves focus to the player after a pick (`platform::focus_by_id`) so the shortcuts work again. Every key press still wakes the chrome, so a keyboard user sees it while acting. A track list left open longer than that has the chrome fade under it; the list itself stays. Signal: a refused focus logs the console warning `pharos: focus pharos-player refused`. Guard: V370.
B426|2026-11-04|IT_RPC `library::native_browses_are_counted_by_level_and_kind` FAILED IN EVERY FULL RUN OF THE BINARY BUT PASSED ALONE: its captured log had no DEBUG `native browse` line. tracing-core 0.1.36 caches a callsite's interest when the callsite is first hit. While a single dispatcher is registered (`has_just_one`), it works that interest out from the hitting thread's default dispatcher, not from the registered list. The test's capture subscriber was often the only one registered, so a test on another thread hit the browse `debug!` first with no subscriber, the callsite was cached as `never`, and the capturing test's own events were dropped.|Test-only. `telemetry::subscriber` (the capture helper every it_rpc log test uses) registers a second, empty `NoSubscriber` dispatcher once per process, so tracing always asks every registered dispatcher and a capturing test's subscriber always counts. Signal: the test itself. Guard: it_rpc run in full, which failed 3 of 3 times before the fix and passed with it; `--test-threads=1` passed both before and after, which confirmed a cross-thread race and not a test-order dependency.
B427|2026-11-04|A FLAKY CACHE TEST TURNED UNRELATED PRS RED. `hls_cache::tests::a_retried_encode_keeps_the_tier_a_client_promoted_it_to` failed on both nextest tries in #638's CI with "precondition: the completeness check must have forced a retry". The test ordered its steps with wall-clock sleeps raced against a fake worker's: the joiner arrived after 100 ms hoping the first attempt (held 250 ms) was still running, and the scheduler was read at 420 ms hoping the retry had been submitted and was still inside its 600 ms. On a loaded runner the short attempt, the progress sidecar write and the driver's re-submission took longer than the 170 ms margin, so the read came before the retry. `a_promoted_retry_does_not_move_the_drivers_playhead` shares the worker and the same race. Fix: the fake worker (`retries_the_nth_encode`) reports each attempt as it starts and holds it on an `EncodeGate` until the test lets it finish; the tests wait for the attempt they need, wait for the joiner's promotion to show in the scheduler snapshot, then release, so no step depends on how fast the runner is. Passed 100 of 100 runs pinned to one CPU. Signal: none; test-only. Guard: V366.
B428|2026-11-04|A FLAKY CAST TEST TURNED UNRELATED PRS RED. `it_cast::cast_mock_tv::drift_beyond_the_threshold_is_corrected_and_the_tv_converges` failed in #644's CI with "timed out after 10 s waiting for: the TV in sync to begin". The test's model of the group position (`GroupClock`) anchored each Play, Pause or Seek at the moment the test drained it from bob's channel, not at the instant the group scheduled it, so it lagged the group by however late the read came: 1.65 s on an idle machine after the test's 1.5 s sleep, against a 1.8 s tolerance. A runner a few hundred ms slower pushed the gap past the tolerance, and since the lag never shrinks, the poll could only time out. Fix: `GroupClock` anchors each command at the group's own clock, `epoch_unix_ms + at_server_ms` (the instant the Jellyfin client also schedules against), and `casting()` reads the seeded queue's Play straight away. Reproduced by lengthening that sleep to 4 s: the old model timed out with the same message, the new one passes; the sibling test `pause_seek_and_unpause_by_other_occupants_reach_the_tv_and_it_tracks_the_group` failed the same way with a late read and now passes. Signal: none; test-only. Guard: V366.
B341|2026-11-04|NO IMDB SCORE AND NO ROTTEN TOMATOES SCORE FOR TV SHOWS. B340 put the stored critic score on films, but nothing stored an IMDb score (`CommunityRating` is mixed-source; no NFO on the live library names `<rating name="imdb">`), and a show's `tvshow.nfo` `<criticrating>` never reached `SeriesMetadata`, so shows got no chip at all. Fix: an OMDb (omdbapi.com) adapter behind the `RatingsSource` port (`ratings.rs`, `omdb.rs`) and a background sweep that looks up films and shows, by IMDb id when the item has a `tt` id, else by title, type and first year. Scores go in dedicated columns (`imdb_rating`, `rt_tomatometer`, `ratings_fetched_at`, migration 0063) on `media_items` and `series_metadata`, written only through `RatingsStore::set_ratings`, so a rescan or re-enrichment keeps them. A miss stores empty scores and is not retried until the refresh TTL (30 days); a spent quota or bad key ("Request limit reached!", "Invalid API key!") stops the day and records nothing; any other failure skips the title until the next pass. The sweep spends at most `[omdb].daily_limit` lookups a UTC day (default 900; the free key allows 1000), shows first, never-fetched first. Episodes are not looked up: their scores are show-level and there are too many. The sweep runs only when `PHAROS_OMDB_API_KEY` is set (the chart reads it from `omdb.apiKey.secretName`); without it the server logs `B341 ratings sweep disabled`. An OMDb Tomatometer outranks an NFO `<criticrating>`; a show's scores reach the native summary through the series DTO's `CriticRating` and the non-Jellyfin key `PharosImdbRating`. Signal: the `ratings sweep: nothing due` / `stopped for today` log lines and `omdb: lookup failed` warnings. Guard: V281.
B405|2026-11-04|SHIFT+RIGHT CLICK ON A PLAYER OPENED FIREFOX'S MENU INSTEAD OF SKIPPING. Alison: "the shift + right click opens the browser right click menu rather than going back a track". B296 made Shift+right click the next episode or track, skipping on the release and suppressing the menu in `contextmenu`. Firefox keeps Shift+right click for its own menu: it sends the page no `contextmenu` event at all (checked in Playwright Chromium and Firefox: Chromium logs `mousedown, contextmenu, mouseup`, Firefox `mousedown, mouseup` with Shift and `mousedown, contextmenu, mouseup` without), and on Linux it opens the menu on the press, so the menu took the release and the skip only happened once a menu was already open. No page can stop that menu. Fix: Shift+middle click is now the next item too, a button no browser keeps for itself (its press is default-prevented, so no autoscroll or paste), and both Shift+middle and Shift+right skip on the press, not the release, so Firefox's menu still opens there but the skip happens. Skipping on the press swaps the player before Chromium sends `contextmenu`, so the player's own handler is gone by then; a skipping Shift+right press arms a window capture listener (`swallow_next_context_menu` in `native/web.rs`) that prevents the next `contextmenu` and is disarmed by the next press, so in Firefox the next plain right click keeps its menu. Shift+click stays the previous item and Shift+P / Shift+N are unchanged. Signal: none server-side; the input handling is client-only, as for B296. Guard: V345.
B318|2026-11-03|THE NATIVE UI HAD NO WAY TO JUMP BETWEEN LIBRARIES OR BACK UP FROM A SHOW, SEASON OR EPISODE, AND THE SERVER COULD NOT SAY WHICH LIBRARY A SHOW OR FILM WAS IN. A synthesised series' DTO left `ParentId` unset (`series_dto` in `api/jellyfin/items.rs`), a film's native `Library.detail` sent no parent, and `library_parent_id` took the first media root holding the path, so under a mixed root that also held a TV library an episode's chain could end at the mixed library.|V258. `series_dto` takes the library of the show's episodes as `ParentId`; native detail fills a stored item's missing parent with its library; `library_parent_id` picks the innermost typed library (longest root), then the innermost scan root. The UI shell gains a pill per library (`LibraryNav`, ordered like the home rails) and a breadcrumb trail (`Crumbs`) walked up through `Item.parent` by `native/trail.rs`, one `detail` per step, at most five steps. The client gains `PharosApi::views`. Signal: none server-side; the parent ids are in the replies. Guard: it_rpc `library::detail_names_each_parent_up_to_the_innermost_library`, pharos-server `series_dto_names_its_library_as_parent`, pharos-ui `trail::tests`, `library_buttons_follow_the_home_order_and_mark_the_one_shown`, `crumbs_link_every_step_but_the_page_shown`.
B429|2026-11-04|A QUEUED ONE-EPISODE REFRESH GREW INTO A FORCED RE-PROBE OF THE WHOLE LIBRARY. Two item refreshes sent seconds apart on the live server (Person of Interest, then Vampire Diaries, 2026-11-04T07:13:05Z): the second found the first's one-folder scan running and was coalesced (B100), and the coalesced re-run in `spawn_scan_tracked` replaced the requested roots with every media root and kept the OR'd `force`. So the Vampire Diaries episode waited behind a forced re-probe of the entire catalog, which logged slow COMMITs while it ran. Cause: `ScanTasks` kept only a `pending` flag and `pending_force`, not which roots were asked for. Fix: `start_or_mark_pending` takes the request's roots and queues each with its own OR'd `force`; `finish_or_rerun` hands back those `(root, force)` pairs and the re-run builds a scanner per root, so a forced folder refresh re-probes that folder only and an incremental library scan queued beside it stays incremental. Signal: the coalesce log `scan: already running — coalesced into one pending re-run (B100)` now carries `roots` and `force`, the re-run log `scan: running one coalesced re-run over the queued roots (B100)` carries `roots` and `forced`, and `scan: root scanned` carries `force`. Guard: V367.
B435|2026-11-04|THE MUSIC PLAYER HAD NO VISIBLE PLAY QUEUE, AND A SONG THAT ENDED PLAYED NOTHING AFTER IT, cause: the native UI had no queue at all; "next" was only the neighbouring track on the album, reachable with Shift+N or Shift+right click, and the `Ended` event only sent the stop report.|V375. Fix: `native/queue.rs` holds one app-wide `Queue` (`QueueCtx` from the native app root, so it lives across pages and songs). Songs leave the queue from the front: starting the queue's first song takes that entry out, and starting any other song replaces the album part of the queue with the songs after it on its album, keeping what the listener added. A song that ends plays what follows (`Queue::advance`): the queue's first, or, before the queue has heard of the song, its album's next; the same song queued twice replays in place. A SyncPlay group keeps deciding what plays, and the queue does not pick songs there. The now-playing screen gets a Queue chip beside Lyrics that opens a frosted drawer (closed by default and remembered per device as `pharos.listen.queue`, so the visualiser keeps the screen): pick a song to skip to it, drag a row or use its arrows to move it, remove, clear, and shuffle (which keeps the order from before and restores it when turned off). Entries carry a `Source` (Added, Album, Mix) so a generated mix can be added or dropped on its own. Signal: client logs `pharos: queue started item= album_rest= upcoming= shuffled=`, `pharos: queue advance reason=ended from=queue|album|none next=`, `pharos: queue action=play|remove|move|shuffle_on|shuffle_off|clear upcoming=`, `pharos: queue action=play refused reason=group`, `pharos: queue replay item=`, `pharos: queue panel shown=`, `pharos: queue drag to=`.
B436|2026-11-04|SONGS AND ALBUMS COULD NOT BE QUEUED, cause: song rows and album cards only linked to the song or album.|V376. Fix: `QueueButtons` (`native/browse.rs`) adds "Play next" (front of the queue, in album order for a whole album) and "Add to queue" (the very end) to each song row on an album page, to the album page itself (labelled), and over each album card in a library grid (icons, shown on hover or focus, always on touch). An album card fetches the album's songs first; a complete album page queues the songs it already has. Buttons sit beside the row's link, not inside it. A queued song's row says "In queue". Signal: client log `pharos: queue add place=next|end target=song|album_card|album_page count=`, and on failure `pharos: queue add failed place= target= album= error=`.
B437|2026-11-04|THE LYRICS TOGGLE COULD NOT BE FOUND, cause: the Lyrics chip was drawn only for a song with lyrics, and with online lyrics off on the server most songs have none, so the listener never saw the control.|V377. Fix: the Lyrics chip is always drawn, in one group with the Queue chip; with no lyrics it is disabled and titled "No lyrics for this song".
B440|2026-11-04|THE MUSIC PLAYER COULD NOT KEEP PLAYING ON ITS OWN. There was no way to say "keep playing things like this" or "70s rock I haven't heard in a while": a queue ended when its album did, and the native API had no call to choose songs by rule. Fix: spec 024 dynamic mixes.
