A pure engine (`services::mix::select`) picks songs for a list of rules (genre, artist, album, years, favourite, never played, play count, not played for / played within / added within N days, length, like the seed) at four strengths (require, prefer, avoid, exclude) with an optional share on prefer rules, a seed (song or follow-the-last-song), and artist/album spacing. `Library.mix(spec, recent, count)` (schema v11) runs it over the songs the user may hear and answers each pick with the rules it matched. `pharos-client` carries the types, presets and the `mix.saved` `SavedMixes` pref codec. Signal: `pharos_mix_total{outcome}`, `pharos_mix_relaxed_total{step}`, `pharos_mix_seconds`, INFO `mix: batch picked` / WARN `mix: no batch`.|V380, V381
B450|2026-11-04|THE VOLUME WENT BACK TO FULL ON EVERY NEW TRACK. Alison, listening on Firefox for macOS: "what seemed like a page refresh that kept happening ... kept resetting the volume set on the playback to full volume after I had turned it down". Each track (and each renegotiated stream) remounts `WatchScreen` and its `Player` (`remount_on` keyed by item and session), so the listen screen, its visualiser and its `<audio>` are built afresh; that is the "refresh". A new media element starts at volume 1, and the volume lived only on the old one. Fix: `Control::Volume` stores the volume and mute in `localStorage` under `pharos.volume` (`volume_setting`, e.g. `0.300` or `0.300 muted`), and each `Player` applies the stored value in an effect that runs before its stream attaches, so autoplay never starts loud. Video shares the setting. Signal: console info `pharos: volume restored volume=<v> muted=<bool>` on each mount with a stored value. Guard: V390.
B451|2026-11-04|THE VISUALISER LOGGED THREE WEBGL WARNINGS ON FIREFOX AND GAVE UP ON A LOST CONTEXT FOR GOOD. Alison saw, on Firefox for macOS: "WebGL context was lost", "WEBGL_debug_renderer_info is deprecated in Firefox and will be removed. Please use RENDERER" and "drawArraysInstanced: Drawing without vertex attrib 0 array enabled forces the browser to do expensive emulation work". Causes: `glow::Context::from_webgl2_context` requests every extension it knows, the deprecated one included; the fullscreen triangle came from `gl_VertexID` with no attribute array enabled, which desktop OpenGL emulates per draw (Firefox runs `drawArrays` as `drawArraysInstanced`); the lost line was the old surface releasing its context on the B450 remount, and a real loss was never `preventDefault`ed, so the browser could not restore it and the screen stayed on its CSS look. Fix (ADR-0049): the surface calls `WebGl2RenderingContext` directly with no `glow`; the vertex shader reads `a_corner` from a static buffer enabled at location 0; a lost context is `preventDefault`ed, frames are skipped, and on `webglcontextrestored` the program, buffer and texture are rebuilt, falling back with `context_lost` only after 5 s. The release-on-drop line stays and is expected. Signal: console `pharos: webgl context lost program=<p>, waiting for restore` and `pharos: webgl context restored program=<p> waited_ms=<n>`. Guard: V391.
B421|2026-11-04|A USER COULD NOT CHANGE THEIR OWN PASSWORD IN THE NATIVE UI. Alison: "We also need to expose password changing". Only the Jellyfin `POST /Users/{id}/Password` route could change a password, and the native web UI had no page for it. Fix: schema v10 appends `Session.changePassword @10 (current, new)`, both `$secret`. `services::auth::change_own_password` refuses an empty new password before the throttle (V211; an empty current one is refused as a wrong one), checks the current password through `login_throttle.verify` (V174), stores the new hash with `set_password`, and revokes every other session token the user holds with cause `password_changed` (B235), keeping the caller's. The UI's account menu gains Account (`/ui/settings/account`, `AccountView` in `native/account_settings.rs`): current password, new password and its confirmation, checked before sending. Signal: `password changed` (info, with `revoked`) or `password change refused` (warn, with the cause) in the server log; the console warning `account: change password failed` in the browser. Guard: V361.
B422|2026-11-04|A USER COULD NOT SET AN AVATAR, AND JELLYFIN CLIENTS FOUND NO USER IMAGE. Alison: "maybe per user avatar settings so people can set themselves an avatar". Pharos served none of Jellyfin's user image routes (`/UserImage`, `/Users/{id}/Images/Primary`), so Jellyfin clients drew every user as initials and could not upload a picture, and the native UI had nowhere to set one. Fix: `crate::avatar` takes an upload only if its bytes sniff as PNG, JPEG, WebP or GIF (the declared type is ignored), decodes it with the `image` crate under limits of 8192 px a side and 256 MiB (at most two decodes at once, on blocking threads), applies the EXIF orientation, crops it to a centred square of at most 512 px and re-encodes it as JPEG (PNG when it has alpha), so no byte of the upload but its pixels is kept. The result is stored as the user's `avatar` pref, which is not on the native `Prefs` allowlist. Jellyfin routes: `POST`/`DELETE` by the user or an admin (body base64 as Jellyfin reads it, raw accepted), `GET`/`HEAD` anonymous by user id as for item images (V225), `nosniff`, immutable caching for the current tag, and `UserDto.PrimaryImageTag`. Native: schema v11 appends `Me.avatarTag @3`, `Session.setAvatar @11` (at most 768 KiB, one WebSocket message) and `removeAvatar @12`. The UI's account button shows the picture, and the Account page has a Picture section; the browser scales a larger picture down before sending it. Signal: `pharos_avatar_uploads_total{outcome}`, `avatar set` / `avatar refused` / `avatar removed` in the server log. Guard: V362.
B441|2026-11-04|THE PLAYER HAD NO WAY TO START OR FOLLOW A MIX. With `Library.mix` on the server (B440) the native UI still had nothing to start one from and nothing to keep the queue full. Fix: `native/mixes.rs`. A Mix button in the queue panel opens a picker (More like this song, the presets Drift, Favourites, Rediscover, Deep cuts, New arrivals, the user's saved mixes, New mix…); song rows get a "Start a mix like" button that plays at once. The app root holds the mix that is on (`MixCtx`) and `use_top_up` asks for 10 more songs whenever fewer than 5 are queued (and at once for a mix that plays now, however much is queued), appending them as `Source::Mix` after anything the listener added and remembering why each was picked, which its queue row shows. Starting or stopping a mix takes out only mix (and album) entries; while a mix is on a song that starts adds no album remainder. An answer for a mix that was since switched is dropped by generation. A failed ask pauses the mix with Retry; a mix that ran out says so and stops asking. The editor builds rules (strength, test, value, share), the seed and spacing, and saves to the `mix.saved` pref. Signal: console `pharos: mix start`, `pharos: mix top-up mix= asked= got= exhausted= relaxed= eligible=`, `pharos: mix top-up failed`, `pharos: mix top-up dropped reason=stale`, `pharos: mix stop`, `pharos: mix saved`/`not saved`.|V382
B460|2026-11-04|LEAVING THE PLAYER STOPPED THE MUSIC. The native UI mounted its player inside the watch route (`Screen { route }` rendered `WatchScreen`, which owned the engine, the report clock and the `<audio>`/`<video>`), so any navigation unmounted it: `use_drop` detached the stream and reported Stopped. A listener could queue songs (B435) but not browse for them while one played, because opening an album ended the song. Fix: spec 020, ADR-0052. One player for the session on a stage beside the routed screen (`StageView`, `native/stage.rs`); the watch route sets the stage's item and full view and draws nothing; any other route shrinks it to a floating window (draggable by its title strip, arrow keys move it 16 px, spot kept in `sessionStorage`) or a mini bar along the bottom, chosen with buttons on each; the watch page and the now-playing screen gain a Shrink button, Back shrinks rather than stops, Close stops. A watch navigation while the player is compact plays the item in place, so the page stays. Signal: a play RPC made while compact carries `ui_action=stage_floating|stage_mini` in the server's call logs; the client logs `pharos: stage view=<full|floating|mini|closed> item=<id> reason=<nav|play|button|close>` and `pharos: stage spot x= y=`.|V400, V401, V402, V403.
B461|2026-11-04|CLEAR EMPTIED THE PLAY QUEUE AT ONCE. The queue drawer's Clear (B435) applied `QueueAction::Clear` on the click, so one stray click lost a queue built up over a session, with no undo. Fix: spec 020 US3. Clear opens an in-place confirm in the drawer (`role=alertdialog`, "Clear the queue? This removes N songs.") with Cancel focused; Cancel or Escape keeps the queue, "Clear queue" empties it; an empty queue offers no Clear. Signal: the client logs `pharos: queue action=clear upcoming=0` on a confirmed clear and `pharos: queue clear cancelled upcoming=<n>` on a cancel. The browser case had skipped on every run because the seeded song has no album artist tag; it now uses the track artist.|V404.
B445|2026-11-04|A SONG WHOSE AUDIO TIMESTAMPS START PAST ZERO WAS STORED AS ANALYSED WITH NOTHING IN IT. Found by CodeRabbit on #695. The tempo/energy worker op compared decoded PTS and the seek target against the window as if the stream started at 0; in a container whose audio stream starts later (a transport-stream rip at ~101 s), every frame sat past the window end, the window decoded empty, and the sweep stored "found nothing" (V385), so the song never got a tempo or energy. Cause: `libav/audio_features.rs` ignored `AVStream.start_time`. Fix: PTS is measured from the stream origin and the seek target is offset by it (`AV_NOPTS_VALUE` reads as 0). Signal: `pharos_audio_analysis_total{outcome="nothing"}` on songs with a duration. Guard: V387, pharos-transcode `a_stream_with_a_late_origin_is_windowed_from_its_own_start`.
B446|2026-11-04|THE TEMPO/ENERGY WINDOW SEEK WAS REFUSED FOR EVERY SONG, SO EVERY SONG WAS ANALYSED FROM ITS FIRST 30 S. Found when the B445 test, made to tell a seek from a fallback, failed on batch D. `libav/audio_features.rs` sought with `ictx.seek(ts, ..ts)`; ffmpeg-the-third turns an exclusive end into `ts - 1`, below the target, and `avformat_seek_file` returns EPERM for any `max_ts < ts`. The fallback (read from the top) hid it, and the old test fixture was uniform. Fix: `..=ts`, and the refused seek is logged at debug (target `libav`). `ANALYSIS_VERSION` 2 re-reads songs stored from the wrong window. `libav/fingerprint.rs` had the same `..ts` call (its pts gate kept it correct, but every credits window decoded from the top); it now seeks with `..=ts` too. Guard: V387, pharos-transcode `a_stream_with_a_late_origin_is_windowed_from_its_own_start`.
B452|2026-11-04|A DROPPED CONNECTION REBUILT THE PLAYER MID-SONG. Alison: the apparent page refresh that reset the volume happened "mid track", not between tracks. The native socket drops on every server rollout (several on 2026-11-04: Loki shows `native api connection opened connection=1` at 07:52, 09:52, 11:44 and 11:53) and on some peer closes after ~4.5 to 5 min, and the UI reconnects. `connect` sets a new `NativeApi` in `ctx.api`, and `WatchScreen`'s `playing` resource read that signal, so every reconnect negotiated a new play session; `remount_on(session)` then mounted a new `Player`, with a new `<audio>` at full volume (B450), a new visualiser (whose old context release logged "WebGL context was lost", B451) and a restart from the last reported position. Fix: `playing` renegotiates only when the API appears or goes (`use_memo` on `is_some`), so the stream and its media element carry on; a separate effect renegotiates on each later API and swaps only the `PlaybackGrant` (which lives on the socket) into the `Playing`'s `RefCell`, so progress reports and the stop keep working. Each grant records the API generation it was negotiated on, so a reconnect during the first negotiation is renewed once it lands, an older renewal never overwrites a newer one, and a failed renewal is retried after 1, 2 and 4 s. A first negotiation that failed, or is still waiting, on a socket that has since been replaced starts again once on each newer connection, as any reconnect used to do. Known limit: a reconnect that cannot reach the server at all goes to the Cannot connect page, which drops the screens and the player as before. The stream URL's `tag` is authorized by the device token, not the socket (B75, B256), so the old stream stays valid. Signal: console `pharos: playback grant renewed after reconnect method=<m> attempt=<n>`, `pharos: playback grant renewal retry attempt=<n>: <e>`, or `pharos: playback grant renewal failed, reports stop attempts=<n>: <e>`. Guard: V392.
B453|2026-11-04|THE VOLUME STILL RESET WHERE LOCALSTORAGE COULD NOT BE WRITTEN. B450 kept the volume only in `localStorage`, so in a private window or past the quota each new player started at full volume again (raised by CodeRabbit on #695). Fix: `remember_volume` also keeps the last volume and mute in a page-load `thread_local`, and `restore_volume` takes that first and the stored value only on a fresh page. Signal: `pharos: volume restored ... from=page|storage`, and `pharos: volume storage write failed error=<e>`. Guard: V393.
B466|2026-11-04|THE NOW-PLAYING SCREEN FLASHED BETWEEN SONGS. Alison saw a flash on every track change. The stage (ADR-0052) keeps the player across route changes but still mounted a fresh `WatchScreen` per item (`remount_on(id)`), so when a song ended or the listener skipped, the old player unmounted at once and the stage drew `Loading` until the next song's detail and play RPCs returned, then a new `<audio>`, audio tap and visualiser canvas. Fix (ADR-0053): a queued song hands the stage over (`Stage::hand_off`); its player stays mounted, renamed `pharos-video-out` and no longer reporting, while the next one negotiates out of sight, and goes once the next is ready. The same handover carries the opt-in crossfade. Signal: `pharos: crossfade start|in|out ...` and `pharos: crossfade set seconds=<n>`. Guard: V408, V409.
B361|2026-11-04|LYRICS FETCHED ONLINE WERE FORGOTTEN ON EVERY RESTART. Alison: "if the lyrics are missing we can fetch and cache them". B360's LRCLIB lookup remembered each answer only in a process-wide memo (4096 entries, emptied when full), so every pod restart or rolling deploy asked LRCLIB again for each song played, and a miss stood until the next restart however long that was. Fix: `services::lyrics::cache::DiskCache` keeps each online answer as `<item id>.json` under `[lyrics].cache_dir`, else a `lyrics` sibling of `transcode_cache_dir` / `image_cache_dir` (the subtitle and book cache rule), written through a temp file and renamed. Found words stand until the song's provider, title, artist, album or length changes; a miss is asked again after 7 days (`MISS_TTL`), in memory as on disk. A provider error is still never cached. Signal: info log `lyrics: online lookup on` with `cache_dir`; debug `lyrics: online answer from the disk cache`; warn `lyrics: cache write failed` / `cache entry unreadable`. Guard: V301.
B465|2026-11-04|THE WEB UI LOGGED "playback stop failed: rpc: Disconnected: Peer disconnected" AND A DROPPED PLAYBACK NEVER ENDED. Alison saw it in Firefox's console after a reload. A native playback's stop is a call on its `PlaybackGrant`, a capability of the connection that negotiated it. A reload closes that connection while the page-hide stop is still in flight, and a reconnect (since #710 the screen stays up and a new playback is negotiated) remounts the player, whose drop stops the old grant on the connection that is already gone. Either way the stop never reached the server, and the server did nothing when the grant was released: the playback's transcode session and its now-playing entry stayed until their own timeouts, and the position after the last report was not saved. Fix: `Grant` in `rpc/playback.rs` records the last reported position and whether `stop` ran; released without a stop, its `Drop` runs `play_state::stop` at that position (with no report, it ends the stream and the session but writes no position). Skipped while the server drains, as the next process takes the clients. The UI logs a stop that fails with `Disconnected` at info (`pharos: playback stop skipped reason=disconnected`); any other stop failure still warns. Signal: server `playback: grant released without a stop, playback ended` (info) or `..., ending it failed` (warn). Guard: V407.
B464|2026-11-04|THE MUSIC PLAYER HAD NO PREVIOUS OR NEXT TRACK BUTTONS. Alison's screenshot of the now-playing bar showed only back 10 s, play/pause, forward 10 s and volume; the only way to change track was Shift+P / Shift+N or a Shift+click, and those knew only the album's neighbours, not the queue or a mix.|V406. Fix: the now-playing transport gets "Previous track" and "Next track" either side of the seek buttons, in the same `pharos-listen-skip` style. Next plays what Next would play when the song ends (the queue's first, else the album's next) and is disabled when nothing follows. Previous (`Control::Previous`, handled in `native/app.rs`) starts the song again once it is more than `RESTART_AFTER_MS` (3 s) in, or when nothing came before; otherwise it steps back through what played (`Queue::back`), falling back to the album's previous track, and in a SyncPlay group to the album's previous track only. `Queue::back` keeps its own walk-back list beside `recent`, so a step back takes songs off it without changing `recent`: the song stepped back to starts without reseeding the album part, and the playing song returns to the front of the queue with its source, so Next comes back to it. The video player's previous and next episode buttons are unchanged. Signal: client log `pharos: previous at_ms= to=` (`to=restart` when it started the song again).
B467|2026-11-04|THE UI BUNDLE STOPPED BUILDING AFTER THE BOKO 0.6 BUMP. Renovate #662 moved the lock to wasm-bindgen 0.2.130 (boko 0.6 needs js-sys 0.3.107), but `flake.nix` still built wasm-bindgen-cli 0.2.128, and `dx build` refuses a CLI that does not match the crate exactly, so `pharos-ui-bundle` failed on main and every PR ("dioxus UI" red). The lock cannot go back: boko pins the newer js-sys. Fix: `wasmBindgenCli` is now 0.2.130 with its source and cargo hashes. Guarded by V410.
B468|2026-11-04|AUDIO RAN AHEAD OF THE PICTURE AFTER A DAMAGED STRETCH OF THE SOURCE. Alison, Buffy S02E11 in the /ui/ player on Firefox: black from about 9:06, picture back at 9:19, then the sound about 300 ms ahead of the picture. A second pass over the same spot stayed in sync. The mkv carries exactly 8 MiB of zeroed bytes at offset 607340093 (`0x00 at pos 607345402 invalid as first byte of an EBML number`), so there are no audio packets from 549.66 s to 556.45 s. The continuous Opus encode kept the packet timestamps across the hole, and the mp4 muxer wrote it as one sample stretched over the whole gap (a92 trun: first sample 326400 ticks, 6.8 s). On the first pass the video segment over the hole was a fresh 7.6 s encode (two short attempts, then the B228 filler), so playback stalled and then resumed inside that stretched sample. A Firefox 159 + hls.js replay with a beep every second, served with the same 7.6 s delay, froze at 552.97 s with 53 s buffered and played the post-hole beep 0.45 s off the beat. Served without the delay, as on the cached second pass, it stayed within 25 ms.|B468/V411. Both continuous audio encodes (Opus rendition and muxed AAC) run `aresample=async=1`, which fills the hole with silence, so every fragment decodes to its full window. The same replay then resumed after the stall and stayed within 40 ms. No `HLS_GEN_VERSION` bump: only damaged sources are affected, a bump would cold-start every cached segment, and a re-acquired file changes its source generation and is re-encoded anyway. A damaged title already cached keeps its old audio until then.
B470|2026-11-04|A WATCH-TOGETHER GROUP KEPT FREEZING AND THE MEMBERS DRIFTED APART. Alison and Jana, both on the new UI, made 35 s of progress in 8 minutes: Jana's browser reported buffering 27 times, every report froze the group (V19), and three freezes only ended at the 30 s anti-wedge timeout. The session issues its own seeks (a `Play`/`Pause` landing more than 150 ms off, a hard drift correction past 1.5 s, and the catch-up when a player arrives); each makes the `<video>` fire `waiting`, and `on_media` sent every `waiting` to the server as buffering, so the group's own corrections froze everyone, which drifted the slow member further, which caused the next seek. A player still loading after a drift seek was also sought again every second, restarting the load. Fix: `GroupSession` records when it last sought (`self_seek_ms`); a `waiting` within `SELF_SEEK_GRACE_MS` (5 s) of that is held, not sent. `canplay`/`playing` drop the hold; a hold that outlives the grace is sent as buffering once from `tick`, so a real stall still makes the group wait. While a hold is in force the drift check leaves the player alone. Signal: server `pharos_syncplay_buffering_freeze_total{outcome}` (the `anti_wedge` and `recovered` rates per session should fall) and `syncplay: member buffering — freezing group (V19)` per member in Loki. Guard: V413.
B471|2026-11-04|A WATCH-TOGETHER MEMBER WHOSE PAGE RELOADED STAYED IN THE GROUP FOR 164 S. Alison's pharos-ui tab reloaded during a session with Jana; its native API connection closed cleanly ("Away") at 18:53:13, yet the group kept her seat as connected until the silence TTL pruned it at 18:55:52, so Jana's group waited on and reported a member who was gone. The membership's socket-loss path ran only from `Membership`'s `Drop`, and a closed connection does not reliably drop the capabilities exported on it. Fix: `ConnectionCtx` carries close hooks that `socket.rs` runs once after the close; a native join registers one that runs the same loss as the drop (`lose_seat`): `MemberSocketLost` at once, the seat kept for `socket_loss_grace` so the same device rejoining takes it over, then `RemoveMember`. The generation fence keeps a drop and a close from removing twice. The reloaded page not rejoining by itself is not fixed here. Signal: `pharos_syncplay_member_pruned_total{cause="connection_closed"}` (the TTL prune is now `cause="silence_ttl"`) and Loki `syncplay: native member removed after its connection stayed gone`. Guard: V414.
B469|2026-11-04|A HOLE LATE IN A WINDOW BLANKED THE GOOD PICTURE BEFORE IT. Alison: Buffy S02E11 went black at about 9:06 and came back at 9:19. The source loses its video from about 9:10; segment 91 (546.5 s to 552.6 s) encoded its 85 frames up to the hole on every retry, came up short, and B228 replaced the whole window with filler, so 3.5 s of picture the source still had went black. Fix: `segment_bytes` keeps the last short fMP4 attempt beside the tmp file (`partial_segment_path`), encodes the filler from where the kept frames stop decoding (their first `tfdt` plus every sample duration, not the window start plus the frame count, which overlaps the two when the source's frame grid is offset from the window) to the end of the window, and appends the filler's `moof`/`mdat` fragments to the kept segment (`splice_partial_and_filler`); mpegts and muxed-audio renditions keep the whole-window filler. The fMP4 box helpers moved from the server into `pharos_cache::fmp4` for this. Guarded by `hls_cache::tests::a_window_cut_short_by_a_hole_keeps_its_frames_before_the_hole` and the real-ffmpeg `filler_after_hole.rs` (V412).
B425|2026-11-04|A GROUP PARTICIPANT'S USER COULD BE READ FROM ANOTHER PARTICIPANT. `GroupSnapshot.participants` and `participant_ids` are the sorted roster, but `member_users` was built from the member map's `values()`, so index `i` of one was not index `i` of the other, and it differed run to run. Nothing indexed them together until `Membership.seen` (spec 014 US6) needed each participant's own watched state, where it would have shown one member's history under another's name. Fix: `participant_users`, built from the same roster; `member_users` stays for the V201 checks that need only the set. Signal: none; caught while writing the it_rpc `group::a_member_sees_what_a_peer_has_played_of_an_episode` test. Guard: V365.
B472|2026-11-04|A HOLE IN THE SOURCE AUDIO SHORTER THAN 100 MS WAS CLOSED UP, NOT FILLED. Raised by CodeRabbit on #726 (B468). `aresample=async=1` pads only past its default 100 ms `min_hard_comp`, so a shorter hole was closed up and the audio after it played early by the hole's length: a beep 1 s after a 50 ms hole landed 60 ms early. Fix: `FILL_AUDIO_HOLES` sets `min_hard_comp=0.02`, one Opus frame, for the Opus rendition and the continuous AAC encode. Guarded by `audio_rendition_timeline::a_hole_shorter_than_100ms_is_filled_with_silence` (V415).
B473|2026-11-04|A SEEK SESSION THAT STARTED INSIDE A HOLE DROPPED THE HOLE'S TAIL. Raised by CodeRabbit on #726 (B468). The Opus seek session's `aresample` started at the first packet after the hole, so its audio played early by the rest of the hole: seeking to 12 s in a hole running to 12.5 s put a beep at 13 s 0.5 s early. Fix: seek sessions anchor `aresample` with `first_pts=0`, the seek point after `-ss`; the from-0 session keeps its `adelay` pad and does not anchor, or a late-starting track would be padded twice. Guarded by `audio_rendition_timeline::a_seek_session_starting_inside_a_hole_fills_its_start_with_silence` (V416).
B474|2026-11-04|A SEGMENT PURGE LEFT THE ITEM'S CACHED AUDIO IN SERVICE. Raised by CodeRabbit on #726: audio encoded before the B468 hole fill keeps its cache identity, so a title whose source has an audio hole kept serving the stretched audio after the fix deployed. Alison chose a targeted purge over a global cache bump. `DELETE /Admin/Cache/Segments` quarantined the item's video segments and bumped its epoch but never touched `_audiohls` or `_contaudio`. Fix: the purge also calls `HlsSegmentCache::drop_item_audio`, which removes every audio session of the item (all tracks, bitrates and editions; an audio session spans the whole title, so the range does not apply), and reports them as `AudioDropped`; the epoch bump moves the audio URLs. Guarded by `hls_cache::tests::dropping_an_items_audio_takes_only_that_items_sessions` and `jellyfin_admin::an_admin_can_purge_a_time_range_of_an_items_segments` (V417).
B475|2026-11-04|A VIEWER WHO STARTED A FILM AND THEN JOINED A WATCH-TOGETHER GROUP WAS NOT DRIVEN BY THE GROUP. In Alison and Jana's retest (19:56–20:01), Jana's group session stayed alive (clock reports, pings) but her video made no segment requests after the join, sent no `ready`, and ignored three seeks, each of whose gates waited out the 5 s anti-wedge. The stage keeps the watch screen mounted across routes (ADR-0052), and the screen read `together.current()` once at mount: with no session yet it got `None`, so the session the join created later never received the video or the watched item, the screen never forwarded media events to it, and the viewer's own seek and play/pause ran locally. Fix: a `Seat` in `group_session.rs` holds the stage video apart from any session; a session made later takes it (and the watched item) and catches up, a video mounted later attaches to the live session, and a drop detaches only the video it attached. The screen reads the session each time it uses it. Signal: client log `pharos: group player attached reason=stage_mounted|session_created`, `detached reason=video_gone`, `detach skipped reason=superseded`. Guard: V418.
B476|2026-11-04|A WATCH-TOGETHER VIEWER ON A SLOW CONNECTION KEPT RUNNING DRY AT FULL QUALITY. In Alison and Jana's retest, Jana's segments arrived at about 1.2–3 Mbit/s while the film's remux ran at 1–4.7 Mbit/s, so her player stalled every few seconds and froze the group (V19). pharos-ui always negotiated with `Capabilities.max_bitrate = 0` (no limit) and offered no quality choice, and the server's `remote_default_bitrate_bps` only lowers a transcode's encoder ceiling, so a remux reached her at source bitrate. The server already turns a source above the client's max bitrate into a transcode capped at it (`direct_play_block=bitrate`). Fix: a per-browser "Maximum quality" setting on the Appearance screen (Auto, 8, 4, 2, 1 Mbps; stored as `pharos.player.max_bitrate`), sent as `max_bitrate` on every negotiation and renewal. Signal: client log `pharos: playback max_bitrate=<bps|auto>` per negotiation; server `pharos_playback_decision_total{direct_play_block="bitrate"}`. Guard: V419.
B477|2026-11-04|THE MAXIMUM-QUALITY CHOICE ALSO CAPPED SONGS. Raised by CodeRabbit on #731 (B476). pharos-ui sent the viewer's video limit as `max_bitrate` for every negotiation, and the negotiator checks `max_streaming_bitrate` for audio sources too, so a song above a 1 Mbps choice was transcoded instead of played direct, though the setting says it limits video. The Auto help text also promised the video "as stored", which a browser that cannot play the source does not get. Fix: `negotiation_capabilities(kind)` sends the choice for video and 0 for audio, on the first negotiation and on renewal, and logs `max_bitrate=… chosen=… kind=…`; Auto now reads "sets no bitrate limit". Guarded by `the_maximum_quality_choice_limits_video_only` (V420).
B362|2026-11-04|THE LYRICS CHIP LOOKED BROKEN ON A SONG WITH NO LYRICS. Alison: "The lyrics button still doesn't seem to work on the track I am on (Moby - Run On)", and the same on "Honey". Both files carry an empty ID3 `USLT` frame (6 bytes: UTF-8, no language, no text), have no sidecar, and online lookup is off on the deployment, so the server rightly answers no lyrics. B437 then draws the chip greyed out with "No lyrics for this song" only in its `title`, which a touch screen never shows, so the chip read "Lyrics" and did nothing. Fix: with no lyrics the chip's own text is "No lyrics"; while the lookup is still running it reads "Lyrics", disabled, titled "Looking for lyrics" (`ListenView.lyrics_loading`, from the player's lyrics resource). Signal: client log `pharos: lyrics lines=0`. Guard: V302.
B478|2026-11-04|THE MUSIC PLAYER'S VOLUME SLIDER FLASHED TO FULL ON EVERY TRACK CHANGE. Reported by Alison. Each track mounts a new player, whose controls state began at `MediaState::default()` (volume 1.0), so the slider drew 100% until the restored volume's `volumechange` redrew it. The audio was not affected: the saved volume is set on the new element before its stream attaches (V390). Fix: the player reads the viewer's last volume once at mount (`last_volume`, page load first, then storage) and starts its controls state there with `MediaState::at_volume`, and the same reading is what `restore_volume` applies. Guarded by `a_new_player_starts_at_the_saved_volume` and the Playwright "the volume carries to the next player" case, which samples the slider every frame across the remount (V422).
B479|2026-11-04|A FAILED LYRICS LOOKUP SAID THE SONG HAD NO LYRICS. Raised by CodeRabbit on #738 (B362). The player's lyrics lookup turned an error into the same `None` as a song without lyrics, so once B362 put the words on the chip, a lookup that failed (a dropped socket, a store error) told the listener "No lyrics" for a song that has them. Fix: the lookup keeps the failure apart (`Err`), and the chip reads "Lyrics unavailable" with the tooltip "The lyrics could not be loaded"; "No lyrics" stays for a finished lookup that found none, and a pending one reads "Looking for lyrics" on the chip. The failure still logs `pharos: lyrics unavailable: <error>`. Guarded by `the_lyrics_chip_says_why_it_does_nothing` (V423).
B363|2026-11-04|ONLINE LYRICS WERE NEVER LOOKED UP ON THE DEPLOYMENT. Alison, after LRCLIB was turned on: "The lyrics button just stays dimmed" on Moby "Run On" and "Honey", which LRCLIB has for the exact title, artist, album and length pharos sends. `Song::of` took the title from `item.probe.title`, but the store does not keep that field: the scanner folds the tag into `media_items.title` and reads the probe back with `title: None` (both backends). Every stored song therefore failed the "has a title and artist" check, and the lookup was skipped at debug level with `lyrics: online skipped, no title and artist tags`. No disk-cache file was ever written. The unit tests built items with `probe.title` set, so they passed. Fix: the title falls back to `item.title`. Signal: the lyrics cache directory gains `<id>.json` files; debug `lyrics lookup` with `outcome=online`. Guard: V303.
B480|2026-11-04|THE VISUALISER STILL MOVED WITH THE VOLUME SLIDER, AND THE SCOPE WENT WILD WHEN LOUD. Alison: "They also still seem to react to the volume level, the scope goes crazy if on high volume". Chromium and Firefox both scale the sound a `MediaElementAudioSourceNode` hands the analyser by the element's volume (measured: a 0.5 sine read 0.353 RMS at volume 1, 0.088 at 0.25, 0.0176 at 0.05, 0 muted, in both). The earlier fix (3557b56b) only followed the music's recent peak, which climbs back over about four seconds, so each slider move dimmed or flared the picture for seconds before it settled. Fix: `AudioTap::volume` reports the element's volume (0 when muted) and `Analysis::read` divides each frame by it before any other step (bins lose `20·log10(v)` dB, samples are divided by `v`), so the slider changes nothing from the very next frame; under 0.001 nothing is undone. The peak follower stays, for quietly mastered tracks. Guard: V424.
B481|2026-11-04|THE SCOPE WENT WILD ON A LOUD SONG. Raised with B480. Three causes in the scope's own maths, none of them the volume: the trace was scaled by `0.5 + u_level` on top of a waveform already normalised to 0.9, so a loud song drew about 0.57 of the half-height and ran off the screen; the waveform row picked one sample in eight from the 2048-sample frame, which aliased the treble into spikes; and each frame started wherever the analyser's buffer did, so even a steady tone jumped sideways every frame. Fix: the tap hands over the whole frame and each texel averages four samples (the first ~21 ms, to ~6 kHz); the row starts at the first rising zero crossing that leaves a full row; the scope shader draws the wave at a fixed 0.36 of the height. Guard: V425.
B482|2026-11-04|THE VISUALISER PICKER WAS AN OFF-CENTRE ROW OF BUTTONS. Alison: "I dislike that they are just a row of buttons and that they are offset from the centre atm". The music screen's top strip was a `space-between` flex row of Back, Shrink, the style chips and the panel toggles, so the chips sat wherever the space left over by their neighbours put them, and each new style would have widened the row (on a phone it already scrolled sideways). Fix: the strip is a three-column grid with equal outer columns, so the middle column is the screen's centre; the chips are replaced by one `StylePicker` button there showing the current style's drawing and name, which opens a grid of every style, each with a small drawing, over a scrim. Clicks, keys and the wheel inside it stop at the picker (the screen turns them into transport and volume controls); Escape or a click outside closes it and returns focus to the button; choosing keeps it open, so styles can be compared. Ships with four new styles (Orbit, Horizon, Kaleidoscope, Starfield). Guard: V426.
B483|2026-11-04|LYRICS COULD NOT BE SUNG ALONG TO. Alison: "I want the lyrics to be almost karaoke style if we want". The lyrics panel lit the current line and kept it centred, but gave no sense of where in the line the song was. Fix: an optional karaoke mode. A Karaoke chip beside Lyrics, shown while synced lyrics are on screen, turns it on and off, and the choice is kept on the device (`pharos.listen.karaoke`, off to start). In karaoke mode the sung line is drawn large and filled in from left to right in the accent colour: a text-clipped gradient at `--pharos-wipe` percent, which is a registered CSS property so the fill glides between the player's quarter-second position reports. LRC times lines, not words, so the fill runs evenly from a line's start to the next line's (5 s for the last line); enhanced-LRC word stamps are still dropped by the server. Other lines dim to 40%. Under reduced motion the fill steps instead of gliding. On a phone the panel now starts below the wrapped chip row. Signal: client log `pharos: karaoke on=`. Guard: V427.
B490|2026-11-04|SWITCHING BETWEEN TV SHOWS AND MOVIES FROM THE TOP BAR COULD LEAVE THE OTHER LIBRARY'S GRID ON SCREEN. Reported by Alison. A library route renders `GridView` → `LibraryGrid`; going from one library to another re-rendered the same `LibraryGrid` with new props, but its `use_resource` closure had captured the first library's listing and first page, and props are not reactive, so the heading changed while the grid stayed on the old library.|V435. `GridView` mounts `LibraryGrid` under `remount_on` keyed by the library id, as the non-library grid already did, so a new library starts a new grid (and its own filter bar). Signal: none; client-side render state. Guard: pharos-ui `a_switch_to_another_library_shows_that_library`.
B484|2026-11-04|THE NEW UI COULD NOT APPROVE A QUICK CONNECT CODE. Alison: "We need a UI within the new UI to enter quick connect codes to allow devices to connect". Only the Jellyfin `POST /QuickConnect/Authorize` route could approve a code, so signing a TV in meant opening jellyfin-web; the legacy `/ui/manage` tab calls that REST route with a Jellyfin token and sits behind the admin-only Manage entry. Fix: schema v14 appends `Session.quickConnectAuthorize @13 (code)`, which sends the same `QcMsg::Authorize` as the REST route, so both share the per-user wrong-code throttle (`MAX_AUTHORIZE_FAILURES`). A blank code is `invalid` (field `code`), an unknown or expired one `notFound`, the throttle `limit` with `quick_connect_retry_after_secs`. The account menu gains "Approve a device" (`/ui/settings/approve-device`, `ApproveDeviceView` in `native/approve_device.rs`) for every signed-in user, which takes the 6-digit code with spaces allowed. Signal: the `quick_connect_authorize` method outcome series and RPC span; a refusal also logs the console warning `approve_device: quick connect authorize failed`. Guard: V428.
B541|2026-11-04|A NATIVE RPC WEBSOCKET CLOSED BEFORE QUICKCONNECT FINISHED. An unauthenticated connection closes after AUTH_DEADLINE (30 s, `socket::run`), but a QuickConnect code lives PENDING_TTL (300 s), so a TV app polling every few seconds was cut off and reconnecting per poll hit the unauthenticated IP cap (HTTP 429). Fix: a quickConnectPoll answering notPermitted (pending) re-arms the auth deadline on unauthenticated connections to now + auth deadline. An extension that would keep the connection unauthenticated past [server.native_api].max_unauthenticated_secs (default 300 s, one code's lifetime) is refused, so re-minting codes cannot hold a socket open forever. Configurable for tests via [server.native_api].auth_deadline_secs (NonZeroU32, default 30) and max_unauthenticated_secs. Signal: pharos_rpc_auth_deadline_extended_total{reason="quick_connect_pending"}, pharos_rpc_auth_deadline_extension_refused_total{reason="max_unauthenticated"}, and the debug logs "native api auth deadline extended" / "native api auth deadline extension refused" with connection id. Guard: V436.
B495|2026-11-04|KARAOKE FILL DID NOT MATCH THE SINGING. Alison: "The Karaoke mode is good but the timing isn't right on the highlight of the words". The fill ran evenly from a line's start to the next line's, so a line followed by an instrumental pause crawled across its words, short and long words took the same time per letter, and the fill eased over 300 ms towards each quarter-second position report, trailing the song. Fix: `line_progress` gives each word time in proportion to its syllables (vowel groups less a silent final e; a syllable a character for words with no Latin letters) and takes a line to be sung in at most `SYLLABLE_MS` (700 ms) a syllable, so a pause after it leaves it full; the fill aims `REPORT_MS` (250 ms) ahead and eases over 250 ms. Still an estimate: word times from enhanced LRC and forced alignment follow under B496-B499. Guard: V427.
B400|2026-11-04|THE NATIVE UI NEVER SAID WHEN A FILM OR EPISODE WOULD FINISH. Alison: "Another thing that would be nice to have is what time you will have watched the episode by similar to what Jellyfin shows. This should take into account the ending credits / outro". A title page showed the runtime and the player showed position and length, so the viewer had to add the remaining time to the clock themselves, and nothing allowed for the credits most people skip. Fix: `left_to_watch` (`native/playback.rs`) measures from a position to where the credits start (`credits_start`: the latest `Outro` segment the server detected in the second half, as the "Up next" card uses) while they are still ahead, else to the end. A film's or episode's title page adds "Ends at 10:42 PM" to its facts, from where Play starts (the resume position of an unfinished item); the player shows it beside the position and updates as it plays. With credits ahead the text reads "Ends at 10:40 PM (before credits)". The time is the browser's locale format (`Intl.DateTimeFormat`, the short time style: "10:42 PM", "22:42"). Both redraw it every 30 s, so a paused player's and an open title page's estimate keeps up with the clock. The player hides it on screens narrower than 40rem, where its control bar has no room. Signal: none server-side; client-only. Guard: V340.
B500|2026-11-05|A PART-WATCHED FILM OR EPISODE COULD NOT BE PLAYED FROM THE START, AND NOTHING COULD BE MARKED WATCHED OR UNWATCHED IN THE NATIVE UI. Reported by Alison, twice: the first fix (#657, recorded there as B380/V320) never merged and conflicted with the stage player, so the title page still offered only Resume. Going back to the beginning meant seeking there by hand. The native UI had no watched control, and the native `UserData.setPlayed` refused every synthesised id, so a season or a show could not be marked even by a client that tried; their native detail carried no watched state either.|V445. Play from start beside Resume sets a one-shot `StartOver` for the title and opens its watch route, and `WatchScreen` starts at zero when it is armed, the same way the music Previous start-over does (B464/V406). Mark watched / unwatched on the title page, a tick on each episode row, and season and show marks on the show page, which reload after a mark. `setPlayed` on a series or season id marks every episode in it the user may see, through the cascade the Jellyfin routes already used (moved into `services::user_data::update_folder`, which takes the per-item write lock), and a series or season detail says watched once every visible episode is. Signal: the watch page logs `pharos: playback from_start item={id} reason=start_over`; a failed mark is the console warning `pharos: marking {id} played={played} failed`. Guard: pharos-ui `a_title_offers_play_or_resume_play_from_start_and_a_watched_mark`, it_rpc `a_season_or_series_marks_every_episode_in_it` and `set_played_marks_a_title_and_a_season`, and the Playwright case `an episode, its season and its show mark watched, and play from start` (red with the start-over disarmed: the video began at 2.5 s).
B550|2026-11-05|SEEKING NEAR THE END OF SOME DRAGON BALL EPISODES HUNG PLAYBACK, AND EVERY OTHER TITLE WITH IT. Alison: "I have had a few complete hangs when trying to seek towards the end of Dragon Ball episodes". S01E05 and S01E12 carry a second audio track that runs about 108 s past the picture (S01E05: video ends at 1243.9 s, audio track 2 at 1350.06 s, container 1350.1 s), and the segment grid follows the container duration, so the last ~18 windows hold no video. On the hybrid VAAPI path ffmpeg opens `h264_vaapi` on its first frame; with none it opens it at EOF without a frames context, logs "A hardware frames reference is required" and "Could not open encoder before EOF", and exits 234. `classify_failure` read that as `DeviceBusy`, so the scheduler cooled Vaapi:1, and under the shared-init pin every rendition banded to it, other titles included, failed with "rendition device Vaapi:1 unavailable; refusing to mix encoders" until the window expired. The same stderr also comes from real device faults (a profile the card lacks, a hwupload that cannot configure), so stderr alone cannot decide. Fix: when a hardware run fails with an encoder that never opened, the worker asks ffprobe whether the source has a video packet in the window (`exec::window_has_video`); none makes the failure `WorkerError::NoFrames` (label `no_frames`, not transient: no cooldown, no spill), and the segment cache sends that window straight to the B228 filler, final window included. Signal: `pharos_transcode_device_cooldown_total` stops rising on these seeks; `pharos_segment_produced_total{outcome="filler"}` counts the tail windows. Guard: V460.
B551|2026-11-05|SEEKING OR STALLING IN THE WATCH PLAYER SHOWED NO LOADING INDICATOR. Alison: "When seeking and waiting on the server there is no indication at the moment that the server / client are actually doing anything." While the `<video>` waited for HLS segments after a seek or a mid-play stall, `WatchView` drew nothing over the picture, and the auto-hiding chrome faded after 2 s. Fix: `MediaState` gains `loading: bool`, computed by `playback::is_loading(seeking, ready_state, paused, ended)` (`seeking || (ready_state < 3 && !ended && (!paused || seeking))`) and cleared when `failed` is set. `<video>` wires `onseeking`, `onloadeddata` and `oncanplaythrough` to `MediaEvent::Changed` alongside the existing `Waiting`, `CanPlay`, `Playing` and `Seeked` redraws so group buffering semantics stay unchanged. `WatchView` renders a centred `.pharos-watch-loading` status overlay (`pointer-events: none`, outside the auto-hiding chrome) with a ring spinner that fades in after 300 ms and a "Waiting for the server…" caption after 8 s. Signal: DOM `.pharos-watch-loading[data-loading="true"]`. Guard: V461; pharos-ui `loading_shows_while_seeking_or_playing_without_future_data`, `the_loading_overlay_shows_outside_the_chrome_only_while_loading`.
B552|2026-11-05|THE LAST SEGMENT OF A TITLE COULD BE CACHED EMPTY AND FAIL FOREVER WITH "fmp4: no moof box". Found while testing B550 on the seeded fixture: `fixture-1.webm`'s grid ends in a 0.01 s window (segment 20, from 120.0 s) that holds no frame. The encode exits 0 and its progress report says `frame=0` but `out_time_us=N/A`; `read_progress` read the N/A as "no report", so the zero-frame check never ran, the ftyp/moov-only output passed the 64-byte floor, was cached, and every request returned 500 "fmp4 seg 20: fmp4: no moof box". Fix: a missing output time reads as 0 s, so the window goes to the B228 filler. Signal: `pharos_segment_produced_total{outcome="filler"}` instead of 500s with "no moof box". Guard: V460 (a window with no source video frames is served as a filler), pharos-cache `an_encode_that_wrote_no_frame_reports_zero_frames`.
B542|2026-11-05|NATIVE CLIENTS HAD TO PUT THE FULL DEVICE TOKEN IN MEDIA URLS. The Android TV app's WebView has no cookie (ADR-0034's `pharos_media` cookie needs a same-site cookie jar), so the spike appended `?api_key=<device token>` to image and stream URLs. That token is Full scope: in a URL it reaches history, proxy and CDN logs, and anything holding it can call the whole API. Fix: schema v15 adds `Session.mediaToken @14`, which issues a `TokenScope::Media` token for the device `pharos-native:media`, valid `[server.native_api].media_token_ttl_secs` (NonZeroU32, default 21600). `MEDIA_ALLOWLIST` lets it call only GET/HEAD on `/items/{id}/images/`, `/videos/{id}/` and `/audio/{id}/`; every credential check that honours a token goes through `scope_permits`, and `user_for_token` refuses it, so it cannot resume a native session. Signal: `pharos_media_token_issued_total{outcome=ok|store_unavailable|rejected}`, the info log "native media token issued" (user, token id prefix, never the secret) and `pharos_token_scope_refused_total{scope="media"}`. Guard: V437.
B543|2026-11-05|A MEDIA TOKEN OUTLIVED THE SESSION THAT ISSUED IT. `Session.mediaToken` stored the Media token as an unrelated `auth_tokens` row (device `pharos-native:media`), so signing out, revoking the device (`revoke_tokens_by_device`), revoking by id, or a password change that revoked the other sessions left it working until its TTL, up to 24 h; on a public server a leaked stream URL survived the user ending the session. Fix: migration 0065 adds `auth_tokens.parent_hash REFERENCES auth_tokens(token_hash) ON DELETE CASCADE`; `Session.mediaToken` issues through `issue_child_with_ttl` with the session token as parent, so every path that deletes the parent row, including paths added later, deletes the child in the same statement. User delete already cascaded and a disabled user is refused by `live_user`; both are now tested. Signal: `pharos_media_token_revoked_total{cause}` (the `RevokeCause` label; counted from `TokenStore::count_live_children` just before the revoke, informational only, the cascade does not depend on it) and the info log "auth: media tokens revoked with their parent" (user, cause, children). Revokes that bypass `services::auth` (the Jellyfin session delete, cast hydrate) cascade but are not counted. Guard: V438.
B370|2026-11-04|EPISODE CARDS SHOWED THE SHOW'S NAME TWICE, NOT THE EPISODE'S TITLE. On the home page a Star Trek: The Next Generation episode read "Star Trek: The Next Generation", then the series line, then "S3 · E7". Three causes. The NFO provider folds `tvshow.nfo` under an episode's own NFO, so an episode without one took the show's `<title>` (and its plot, tagline and premiere) as its own. The filename provider ran the film cleaner over an episode's whole stem, which leads with the show and ends in Sonarr's `WEBRip-1080p` (not a tag it knew), so 1,908 live episodes kept their whole file name as their title. And the online pass never touches a title, so the episode name TVDB returns was dropped. Fix: a `tvshow.nfo` no longer fills an episode's title, plot, tagline or premiere; for an episode the filename provider takes the title from the words after its `S03E07` token, less a multi-episode tail, an absolute number and the release tail (`episode_title` in `metadata/filename.rs`), and gives none for a bare scene name; `apply_enrichment` replaces an episode title that is still its file stem or its show's name with the record's episode name. The UI reads such a stand-in as "Episode 7" on a tile and in a show's episode list (`display_name` in `native/views.rs`), so the show's name is not shown twice while old rows wait for a re-probe or their next online pass (30 days). Not fixed: the stored TNG season 1 titles are one episode out against their files (an episode NFO ordering question). Signal: none; the title is in the stored row. Guard: V310.
B390|2026-11-04|AN EPISODE ENDING IN THE NATIVE PLAYER WENT NOWHERE. Alison: "It should have a coming up next and a count down to skip to the next episode like Jellyfin. It should maybe also start showing that suggestion during the detected outro". The watch page had previous and next episode buttons (across a season's edge), but at the end of an episode the video just stopped on its last frame, and an episode left during its credits stayed half-watched in Continue watching. Fix: an "Up next" card (`UpNext` in `native/watch_view.rs`, countdown in `native/playback.rs`) shows the next episode's still, name and numbers over the credits, from the start of a detected outro segment in the episode's second half, else 20 s before the end, and counts down 15 s (never past the end) by the video's position, so pausing pauses it. When it runs out, on Play now (or the next-episode button while the card shows), or when the video ends, the player stops the playback at its full length, which marks the episode played through the server's played threshold, and plays the next episode in place (`Player`'s `on_advance`). Cancel hides the card and lets the episode end. Films and music do not move on. Signal: the console info line `pharos: up next: playing <id> (<reason>)` for each move on (countdown, ended, play now, next button), the console warning `pharos: marking watched failed` when the finishing stop fails, `pharos: up next unavailable` when the next episode's details do not load (the card then says "Next episode"). Guard: V330.
B330|2026-11-03|SEARCH WAS HIDDEN BEHIND A "FIND" LINK, AND THERE WAS NO QUICK WAY TO GO ANYWHERE. The native UI could search every library (`Library.search`, the `/find` screen), but the top bar offered only a plain "Find" link to a separate page with an unstyled field, so the user reported search as missing, and asked for a Slack-style Ctrl+K / Cmd+K switcher in place of the find page. Fix: the shell bar holds a search box on every signed-in screen (`SearchBox` in `native/views.rs`, `TopSearch` in `native/app.rs`). Typing lists the first six matches under it with a thumbnail, the name and what it is (Film, Episode with its series and numbers, Song …); the arrow keys walk them, Enter opens the one picked or, with none picked, the palette with every match; Escape closes the list (a second Escape clears the box). Ctrl+K, or Cmd+K, anywhere opens the palette (`PaletteView`, `PaletteScreen`, rules in `native/palette.rs`): before anything is typed it lists Home, each library, Appearance and Manage, and up to five things to resume from the home reply; typed, the places whose name holds the term and then the search matches, grouped under headings; arrows, Enter and Escape as in the bar, and the same keys toggle it shut. The bar and the palette share one search (`Search` context: text, generation, hits). The find screen is gone: `/find` now opens the palette over home, so old links still land. Signal: `ui_action=search` and `ui_route=palette, ui_action=home` on `CallContext`; a failed home load in the palette warns `palette: home failed`. Guard: pharos-ui `the_shell_bar_holds_the_search_box`, `the_search_list_shows_the_first_matches_and_a_way_to_the_rest`, `the_search_list_stays_shut_until_there_is_something_to_show`, `the_palette_heads_each_group_and_marks_the_active_row`, palette.rs `a_term_keeps_the_places_it_names_then_the_matches`, `ctrl_or_cmd_with_k_toggles`, find.rs `arrows_walk_the_suggestions_and_stop_at_the_ends`; Playwright "search: the bar lists matches as you type …" and "palette: Ctrl+K goes to a page by name, and an old /find link opens it". Not fixed: a hard load of any deep `/ui/...` path serves the app with HTTP status 404; browsers render it, so it is not this bug.
B273|2026-11-02|ACCOUNTS WITH AN EMPTY PASSWORD COULD BE CREATED AND SIGNED INTO, cause: POST /Users/New without a Password and POST /Users/{id}/Password with ResetPassword=true both stored the hash of an empty string, and BuiltinAuth::authenticate verified the empty password against it with no check that a password was non-empty; the CLI create-user and reset-password took an empty value the same way|V211. authenticate refuses an empty password before the user lookup and before argon2, counted in pharos_login_empty_password_total and logged as a warning; /Users/New answers 400 "a password is required" when the password is missing or empty; a reset to an empty password answers 400 and the stored hash is unchanged; an empty NewPw answers 400; the CLI refuses an empty password before opening the database. Tests: auth::tests empty_password_* (the sign-in test fails with the guard disabled), it_auth jellyfin_admin create_user_without_or_with_empty_password_is_400, it_auth password_change_audit reset_password_true_is_400_and_leaves_the_hash_unchanged and empty_new_password_is_400_and_leaves_the_hash_unchanged, main require_password_tests
B555|2026-11-05|OPENING ONE TITLE PAGE FROM ANOTHER KEPT THE FIRST TITLE ON SCREEN. Cause: `TitleScreen` loaded its detail in a `use_resource` that captured the `id` prop by value. Dioxus keeps the screen mounted when the route moves from `/ui/title/A` to `/ui/title/B`, so the URL changed and the page went on showing A, with A's Play link. The top-bar search (#646) made this the usual path. Found when the E2E "a Play while the player floats" opened a film from the song's title page, clicked Play, and replayed the floating song (stage log `item=<song> reason=play`). Fix: the load is `use_reactive!(\|(id,)\| …)`, as `BrowseScreen` already does (V465). Test: the Playwright helper `openFirstHit` waits until the page shows the hit's name after it opens the hit. The floating-play case goes from one title page straight to another.
B556|2026-11-05|WATCHING TOGETHER COULD NOT RESUME A PART-WATCHED TITLE. The title page's *Watch together* always created the group at 0 ms (`session.create(id, 0)`), so a pair half-way through an episode had to start it over or seek by hand once in the group; the new UI had no way to start a group where the viewer left off. Fix: a part-watched film or episode offers *Resume together*, which creates the group at the viewer's saved position (`start_ms`), beside *Watch together from start*, which creates it at 0; an unwatched or watched title keeps the single *Watch together* at 0. Signal: the console line `pharos: group create item=<id> from_ms=<ms>` names the chosen start. Guard: V466.
B559|2026-11-05|OPENING "F9" SHOWED "rpc: Enum value or union discriminant Enum value or union discriminant 2 was not present in the schema. was not present in schema" INSTEAD OF THE TITLE PAGE. Cause: `Library.detail` sent `SubtitleDelivery.image` (2, new in schemaVersion 16) to every client. A native UI tab loaded before the schema-16 deploy reconnected to the new server on its own and could not read the value, and `read_tracks` failed the whole detail with `?`. The live server counted a schema-15 and a schema-7 client after the deploy (`pharos_rpc_client_schema_total`). capnp's own text for the error names the value twice. Fix: `detail` sends `image` only when `CallContext.schemaVersion` is 16 or later, else `burn` as before (V469); the client reads an unknown delivery as `burn` and drops a segment of an unknown kind; `ClientError` names an unknown value once and says to reload; and the native UI asks `serverInfo` on each connect and shows a Reload notice when the server's schema is newer than its own. Test: `native_detail_offers_image_subtitles_to_draw_when_the_server_extracts_them` checks schema 16, 15 and 0; client unit tests cover the reads, the message and `is_outdated_by`.
B557|2026-11-05|SHOWS WERE MATCHED TO FAN WORKS, PARODIES AND THE WRONG ENTRY OF A FRANCHISE. Alison: "The Dragon Ball series is showing the episode images from another Dragon Ball series"; the title page also showed the poster and overview of "Dragon Ball R&R", a fan-made audio drama. Cause: TVDB's `/search` `name` is a show's original-language title (`ドラゴンボール`), and `parse_series_search` read only that, so the real anime scored near zero and an English-titled fan work won on a prefix match with no year: Dragon Ball → R&R (0.827), Dragon Ball Z → Dragon Ball Z Abridged, Attack on Titan, Sailor Moon, Sword Art Online, One-Punch Man and One Piece likewise; most other anime fell through to TMDB (the B125 fallback). Fix: a `SearchCandidate` carries `aliases` (TVDB's `translations.eng` and `aliases`) and `match_best` scores each by its best name; among candidates over the floor an exact name outranks a prefix match, an exact title or translation outranks an exact alias (the 2023 live-action ONE PIECE lists "One Piece" as an alias), and inexact matches within 0.03 go to the earliest-premiered, so "Code Geass" is the 2006 series rather than a 2024 sequel. `TV_MATCH_VERSION` (1) is recorded in server config; a differing or absent value re-admits every searched show and episode on the next backfill pass. Swept against TVDB's live answers for all 200 searched shows: 29 move to the right show, none to a wrong one. Signal: the series rows matched by search with no year at a confidence in [0.76, 0.85) (`SELECT series_name, match_external_id, match_confidence FROM series_metadata WHERE match_source = 'search' AND match_confidence >= 0.76 AND match_confidence < 0.85`), 11 before the fix; and the log line "show matcher version changed". Guard: V467; pharos-core `match_best_scores_a_candidate_by_its_best_alias`, `match_best_ranks_an_exact_name_over_a_prefix_match_with_a_better_year`, `match_best_prefers_an_exact_title_over_an_exact_alias`, `match_best_a_franchise_name_goes_to_the_original`, pharos-server `tvdb_series_search_matches_an_anime_by_its_english_translation`, `a_new_show_matcher_re_admits_searched_shows_and_episodes_once`.
B558|2026-11-05|EPISODES TOOK ANOTHER SHOW'S TITLES, PLOTS, IDS AND STILLS FROM A MISIDENTIFYING SCRAPER'S NFOS, AND TVDB TEXT SHOWED HTML REFERENCES. Jellyfin had identified the library's `TV/Dragon Ball` folder as Super Dragon Ball Heroes and wrote that show's `tvshow.nfo` (`<title>Super Dragon Ball Heroes</title>`, tvdb 347734), an NFO per episode (`<showtitle>Super Dragon Ball Heroes</showtitle>`) and each `<episode>-thumb.jpg`. NFOs are authoritative, so "S01E01 - Secret of the Dragon Balls" was titled "Goku vs. Goku! A Super Battle Begins on Prison Planet!" with that episode's still, and its ids sent the online pass to the same show. `Forever (US)` carries another show's `tvshow.nfo` too ("Us Forever"). Separately, TVDB's JSON leaves HTML references in text, so the overview read "Dragon Ball R&amp;R". Fix: an episode's `tvshow.nfo` (title or original title) and its own NFO (`<showtitle>`, else the show NFO's verdict) must resemble the series name at a title similarity of 0.8 or more; one that does not is skipped, with an info log naming the NFO, and the sidecar detector skips the images the same scrape saved: an episode's `<basename>-*` art when its NFO is rejected, the show folder's art when `tvshow.nfo` is. Artwork rows already stored are upserted per role, so a re-scan does not remove them. Swept against all 154 show NFOs in the deployed library it rejects only those two. The TVDB series and episode parsers decode character references once. Rows already scanned keep the old NFO data until they are re-read. Signal: Loki `{namespace="pharos"} |= "names another show"`. Guard: V468; pharos-scanner `nfos_naming_another_show_are_ignored_with_their_art`, `nfos_naming_the_show_are_kept_with_their_art`, `the_show_name_check_passes_the_librarys_legitimate_variants`; pharos-server `decode_entities_decodes_tvdb_references_once`, `tvdb_series_detail_overview_has_no_html_references`.
B562|2026-11-05|THE NEXT AND PREVIOUS TRACK KEYS DID NOT SKIP. Alison: "the next and previous keyboard shortcuts don't seem to apply to the player, meaning I cannot skip to the next or previous tracks". Two causes. Shift+P / Shift+N were `onkeydown` handlers on the player's own surface (`.pharos-listen`, the watch player div), so they fired only while that element had focus: after a click on a queue row, the shell, or with the player floating or in the mini bar, the keys reached nothing. And the page registered no media session actions, so the keyboard's media keys and the system's media controls (MPRIS on Linux, which Firefox routes to `navigator.mediaSession`) had no `nexttrack` / `previoustrack` to call. Fix: `platform::on_track_skip` (`native/web.rs`), installed once at the app root, listens for Shift+P / Shift+N on the window (not in a text field, no Ctrl/Alt/Meta, no repeat) and sets the media session's `previoustrack` / `nexttrack` handlers; it calls the one handler in `TrackSkipCtx`, which the `Player` in `Slot::Current` sets on each render under its own owner number and clears on unmount only while it still owns it. A song's previous is `Control::Previous` (B464), an episode's the one before, next is what the Next button plays; `offer_track_skips` sets a handler to null when there is nowhere to go. The players' own key handlers no longer take Shift+P / Shift+N, so a press cannot skip twice. web-sys keeps `MediaSession` behind `web_sys_unstable_apis`, so it is reached through `js_sys::Reflect`. Signal: console info `pharos: track skip dir=<previous|next> source=<keyboard|media_session> item=<id> to=<id|song_previous|none>`, and `... to=none reason=no_player` with no player on show. Guard: V472.
B496|2026-11-04|ENHANCED-LRC WORD TIMES WERE THROWN AWAY. Follows B495: the karaoke fill estimated where each word fell even when the lyrics file timed every word, because the LRC parser dropped `<mm:ss.xx>` word stamps. Fix: the parser keeps each stamp as a char offset into the line's cleaned text and a time, shifted by `[offset:]` like line stamps; a line repeated under several line stamps keeps none (word times are absolute) and out-of-order stamps are dropped. The disk cache keeps them (old entries read as none), the native schema carries them as `LyricLine.words` (v17), and the client reads them. In karaoke mode a stamped line fills straight from stamp to stamp, and on by syllables after the last stamp if words follow untimed. Most LRCLIB lyrics time lines only, so this helps stamped sidecar and embedded files today and is the shape forced alignment will fill (B497). Guard: V440.
B545|2026-11-05|THE SUBTITLE SIZE SETTING LEFT FIREFOX SUBTITLES TINY, AND CUES SAT ON THE BOTTOM EDGE. V455 scaled `video::cue` by a percentage (75 to 150%). Chromium resolves that against its own video-sized default, but Firefox resolves it against the ~16px inherited font, so on Firefox every size was 12 to 24px and Extra large still read as very small; the rounded backdrop and the `translateY` lift used `::-webkit-media-text-track-*` pseudo-elements Firefox does not have, so Firefox cues kept square bars on the bottom edge. Fix: the size is `scale x max(14px, min(2.8cqw, 5vh))` against `.pharos-player` as an inline-size container (Firefox, Chromium and WebKit all resolve it; the mini player drops the 14px floor), the steps widen to 0.8/1/1.3/1.65, Firefox gets a translucent `::cue` background, and the native player's `show_text_track` and the non-native player's `lift_cues_as_shown` lift cues with the standard `VTTCue.line` instead of the WebKit-only transform: `lifted_line` gives `-(n + 1)` for a cue of `n` rendered lines, counting a line that wraps at the player's width and cue size as the lines it wraps to (`cue_chars_per_line`). Guarded by V455.
B561|2026-11-05|THE "UP NEXT" CARD SHOWED ONLY SECONDS BEFORE THE END, SO VIEWERS SAT THROUGH THE CREDITS. Alison: "The up next card needs to appear earlier in the episode, it doesn't appear until right before the end of the episode which can mean it forces the watcher to watch the credits". The card (B390) shows where the server detected the credits, but an episode with no detected outro fell back to 20 s before the end, and the chapter classifier missed the usual anime and rip titles ("ED", "ED 2", "Ending", "Rolling credits"). Fix: `classify_chapter_title` (`services/segments.rs`) reads any title containing "credits" (after the intro check, so "Opening Credits" stays an intro), except one naming a scene around the credits ("Post-Credits Scene", "Postcredits", "After the Credits"; only letters and digits compared, so any space, hyphen or dash between the words counts the same), which is no segment at all whatever else it holds, and a title of "ED" or "Ending" with at most a number or "theme"/"song" after it ("[ED 2]", "Ending Theme") as an Outro, and "OP" likewise as an Intro, so "The Ending", "Ed meets the king" and "Operation" stay ordinary chapters. Without detected credits, `up_next_at` (`native/playback.rs`) shows the card 1/20 of the runtime before the end (66 s of a 22-minute episode), between 20 s and 90 s but never in the first half. A card shown from that guess counts down to the end instead of 15 s, so a scene the guess covers is never cut short; Play now and Cancel work as before. "Ends at" still allows only for detected credits. Signal: the console info line `pharos: up next: playing <id> (<reason>) ... from=Some(UpNextFrom { at_ms, credits })` says whether the card came from detected credits or the guess. Guard: V471, V330.
B560|2026-11-05|A LIBRARY FORGOT ITS FILTERS AND SCROLL POSITION WHEN THE VIEWER CAME BACK FROM A TITLE. Cause: `LibraryGrid` held its sort, direction, genre and watched choice in a signal that started at the default on every mount, and nothing recorded where the grid was scrolled; going from a library to a series or film and back (Back, a breadcrumb, a library button) mounted a fresh grid, so the viewer was put at the top of the unfiltered listing. Reported by Alison while browsing TV shows deep into a filtered list. Fix: the choice is written to the URL query (`?sort=&dir=&genre=&watched=`, the default left bare) with `history.replaceState` and kept per library in `sessionStorage`; a library opens on its URL's choice, else the tab's last one for it. The grid records its first visible tile and that tile's offset once scrolling settles, per library and choice, and on coming back pages through to that tile and puts it where it was. A new choice starts at the top. A "Reset filters" button shows whenever the choice is not the default. Test: `browse::tests::a_library_choice_round_trips_through_its_query`, `a_library_query_reads_what_it_can_and_ignores_the_rest`, and the Playwright case `browse keeps a library's filters and place across a title`. Guard: V470.
B563|2026-11-05|A PAUSED SONG TOOK A NOTICEABLE MOMENT TO SOUND AGAIN AFTER PLAY. Alison, in Firefox: "if I pause the music and then hit play there is a bit of a lag in resuming the music". The server is not in the path: a song is served whole (or in 8 MiB windows) over `/audio/{id}/universal`, a resume makes no new negotiation, transcode or segment request, and the play/pause reports it sends answer in 1–8 ms after the `playing` event. Measured first, then one suspected cause addressed: the cause is not yet confirmed, and the deployed signal decides it. `native/resume.rs` (`ResumeProbe`) times each resume from the play control to the element's `playing` event, then to the first visualiser analyser window whose peak (divided by the volume) passes -60 dBFS, timing out after 5 s; `platform::resume_context` records the element's readyState, the buffer ahead, the engine and the tap's `AudioContext` state and latencies, and the visualiser tap registers its nodes so the probe can read them. Signal: console info `pharos: resume click_to_playing_ms=<ms|timeout> playing_to_audible_ms=<ms|untapped|muted|paused|superseded|timeout> ready_state=<0-4> buffered_ahead_ms=<ms> context_state=<running|suspended|interrupted|closed|untapped> base_latency_ms=<ms|none> output_latency_ms=<ms|none> context_resumed=<true|false> engine=<direct|hlsjs|native_hls>` and the same fields as a `playbackResume` client event. A play (`platform::toggle_play`, `platform::play`) on a tapped element whose `AudioContext` is `suspended` (or `interrupted`, WebKit's state while a call or another app holds the audio session) resumes the context first, as a routed element plays into a suspended context in silence; signal: console info `pharos: resume context was not running state=<suspended|interrupted>`, then, when the `resume()` promise settles, console info `pharos: resume context resumed state=<state>` or warn `pharos: resume context resume() rejected: <error> state=<state>` (`context_resumed` in the report only says a wake was asked for). Guard: V473.
B564|2026-11-05|SHOW DESCRIPTIONS CAME IN THE LANGUAGE THE SHOW WAS MADE IN. Alison: "it gathered a description for dragonball in Japanese, most users speak english." Cause: TVDB's base series and episode records are written in the show's original language, and `tvdb.rs` read `data.overview` from them without naming a language, so anime got Japanese text and French shows French text. Fix: series are read from `/series/{id}/extended?meta=translations` and episodes from `/episodes/{id}/translations/{lang}` (only for languages the episode lists), English goes into the default columns when TVDB has it, and every language fetched is a row in `metadata_translations` (migration 0066) with the base text flagged `is_original` and a missing language as an empty row. A backfill pass, on by default (`[metadata] fetch_translations`), fetches English plus every language a user picked, 500 per run, and replaces a stored default description still equal to the base text. Each user picks their description language (`DescriptionLanguagePreference`, native `Prefs` key `description.language`); `services::metadata_text` resolves it for both APIs: their language, else the default column (English when TVDB has it, otherwise the original), with no further English or original lookup; "original" reads the row flagged `is_original`, else the default column (ADR-0062). Signal: `sum by (language, outcome) (increase(pharos_metadata_translation_total{provider="tvdb"}[1d]))`, Loki `{namespace="pharos"} |= "tvdb text falls back to the default text"` and `|= "replaced a stored provider base-record description with the English text"`. Guard: V474.
B565|2026-11-05|THE WEB UI'S BROWSER TAB READ "pharosdioxus \| ⛺", AND THE ANDROID TV APP'S LAUNCHER LABEL READ "PharosTv". Cause: dx writes `[web.app] title` into index.html's `{app_title}` placeholder and, with no placeholder there, inserts it before `</title>`; Dioxus.toml set no title, so dx's default "dioxus \| ⛺" was appended to index.html's "pharos". The TV manifest's label was `@string/app_name`, which dx fills with the bin name in Pascal case. No page set its own title either, so every tab read the same. Reported by Alison. Fix: index.html's title is `{app_title}` and Dioxus.toml sets `title = "Pharos"`; the TV manifest labels the app "Pharos" directly; `Root` sets the tab title from the route and the name of the item the page loaded ("Alien · Pharos", "Pilot · Breaking Bad · Pharos", "Appearance · Pharos"). Test: `route::tests::the_tab_names_the_page_then_the_app`, `views::tests::an_episode_page_is_named_with_its_show`, and the Playwright case `the tab names the page, then Pharos`. Guard: V475.
B568|2026-11-05|A MEMBER'S BUFFERING END COULD REACH THE GROUP BEFORE ITS START, FREEZING PLAYBACK. Live at 19:23:41 UTC: a member's `buffering(true)` (waiting) then `buffering(false)` (canplay) reached the group actor as BufferingEnd then BufferingStart, so the server kept the member marked buffering for good and the group froze repeatedly until the 30 s anti-wedge or a manual unpause. Cause: the UI's group session spawned each group command (buffering, ready, seek, pause, unpause) as its own RPC, so two sent back to back ran concurrently; on the server each goes through `route_command` (`group_ops.rs`), which awaits `resolve_with_retry` and `check` before `h.tx.send`, so a slow first call let the second overtake it. Fix: `Session::command` (`native/group_session.rs`) queues each command and one task sends them one at a time, starting a command only once the previous RPC has answered (success or error), or after `COMMAND_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s) without an answer, which warns `pharos: group command timed out kind=<kind> after_ms=10000`; the queue survives a re-join. Test: `group_commands_reach_the_server_in_the_order_they_were_sent` (a `FakeGroups` that holds each `buffering` until released, the second released first). Signal: client info `pharos: group command sent kind=<buffering(true|false)|ready(playing=..)|seek(ms)|pause|unpause> queued=<n>` (n counts the command in flight too) in send order, and the server's `syncplay: command dispatched` lines in Loki `{namespace="pharos"}` arriving in that same order. Guard: V481.
B566|2026-11-05|OPENING A SHOW, SEASON, ALBUM OR ARTIST WAITED ON FOUR FULL-TABLE SCANS, AND A POSTER TAG COULD RE-READ THE WHOLE LIBRARY. Alison asked why library items load slowly. Live traces of `rpc.library.query` showed two costs. `resolve_parent_filter` turned a synth series, season, artist or album id back into its key by running `SELECT DISTINCT` over `media_items` for series keys, season keys, artist names and album names, then hashing every row, on every request; those queries carried no span, so a season page showed ~450 ms of unexplained idle between `get` and `query`. And `resolve_synth_image_item` read the whole library with an uncached `stores.list()` (~250 ms, 175 ms of it CPU decoding ~14k rows) for each synth id missing from its memo, up to five times in one 1.5 s listing. Fix: the distinct-key queries are traced; `synth_parent_filter` answers from a memo of every synth parent id built in one pass (`SynthParents`), rebuilding when the library generation has moved, or for an id it does not know when the memo is over 60 s old, with one caller building while hits keep answering, so a known id costs no scan and a genre or person id at most one a minute; the synth poster warm reads `list_items_cached`, whose key now includes the artwork epoch so a cover the backfill records still changes the representative (B155); an id the cached list does not name is checked once against the store before its negative is kept. Signal: `pharos_synth_parent_lookup_total{outcome=hit|miss|rebuilt}`, the `distinct_*` and `build_synth_parents` spans, and fewer `list` spans under `rpc.library.query`. Guard: V477.
B567|2026-11-05|`?FORMAT=AVIF` AND `?FORMAT=WEBP` SILENTLY SERVED JPEG IN PRODUCTION, AND WOULD HAVE LOST QUALITY ELSEWHERE. Found measuring library load time for Alison. The production `ffmpeg-lib` backend's `transcode_image` returns NotImplemented, so the route fell back to the JPEG under the AVIF URL; the spawn backend re-encoded the already-lossy q:v 3 JPEG, which in a 30-image SSIMULACRA2 benchmark never reached the JPEG's own quality at any WebP setting. An inline encode would also have made a cold tile wait 0.1-1 s. Fix: `ImageCache::encoded_artwork` encodes the original sidecar or extracted frame with ffmpeg (libaom `tune=iq`, 4:4:4, crf 22: 0.73x the JPEG's bytes for posters at equal or better quality) in a one-slot background queue at bucketed widths, and the route serves the sized JPEG marked `no-cache` until the encode lands (ADR-0063, V478).|
B569|2026-11-05|THE SEEK BAR'S CHAPTER TICKS WERE ALL BUT INVISIBLE, SO THE PLAYER SEEMED TO HAVE NONE. Alison, comparing it with Jellyfin's player: chapter markers were missing from the seek bar. Cause: the ticks were 2px black lines on a 4px bar that is itself a dark wash over a dark gradient, so they only showed against the played fill, and the flat bar gave no sense of where the play head sat. Fix: light ticks with a dark rim that stand 2px proud of the bar and dim once passed (`data-passed`), a grooved track with a raised, lit fill, and a knob at the play head shown on hover or while paused. Motion only answers the pointer (the bar swells, the knob grows in, a sheen crosses the fill), never loops while the chrome is hidden, and stops under reduced motion. Video player only; the music screen's seek is its own. Test: `watch_view::tests::the_seek_bar_marks_passed_chapters_and_the_play_head`, `theme::css::tests::the_seek_bar_holds_still_for_reduced_motion_and_while_idle`. Guard: V482.
B570|2026-11-05|EPISODES OF SHOWS TVDB HAS RENUMBERED CARRIED THE NEXT EPISODE'S TITLE, PLOT AND STILL. "Star Trek - The Next Generation - S01E03 - The Naked Now" showed as "Code of Honor".
