TVDB now lists the two-part pilot "Encounter at Farpoint" as one episode, so every later season-1 episode sits one number below the file's, and both the Jellyfin scrape that wrote the library's NFOs and pharos's own TVDB pass looked the file's `S01E03` up by number and got Code of Honor: NFO `<title>`, `<plot>` and the `<stem>-thumb.jpg` still, then TVDB's overview and still. Swept against the live library, 296 NFO-identified episodes have a stored title unlike their filename's; among them the same one-to-three-number shift moves The A-Team (35), Star Trek Voyager (20), The Six Million Dollar Man (19), The Next Generation (13), Quantum Leap, Roseanne, PLUR1BUS, Buffy and Sliders, while Sailor Moon, One Piece and Frieren (dub vs translated names of the same episode) do not move. Fix: the online pass passes the episode's own name, from its filename (B370's `episode_title`), to the TVDB fetch; `pick_episode` keeps the numbered record when its name agrees, and otherwise takes the one record within 3 numbers in the same season whose name reads at least 0.9 alike with the same part numbers, a stand-in name ("Episode 6", a release tag) or a tie leaving the number in charge. A record placed that way replaces the item's title, plot and premiere date and its scraped Thumb, since its NFO described the episode next to it; the show-folder Primary stays. The translation pass places the episode the same way. TVDB only: TMDB, the fallback when TVDB is not configured, still places an episode by number. Rows already stored change at their next refresh (TTL) or a targeted reset. A re-probe re-reads the NFO until the next refresh. Signal: Loki `{namespace="pharos"} |= "re-identified by its name"`. Guard: V483; pharos-server `an_episode_takes_the_record_its_name_matches`, `an_episode_keeps_its_numbered_record_without_a_clear_named_one`, `an_episode_past_the_end_of_the_list_is_found_by_its_name`, `a_reidentified_episode_replaces_its_scraped_text_and_still`, `an_episode_on_its_own_number_keeps_its_local_text_and_still`.
B571|2026-11-05|EPISODE AND SHOW BACKDROPS POINTED AT JELLYFIN'S PATHS, SO NONE OF THEM LOADED. 10,497 episode Backdrop rows on the deployed library held paths like `/media/TV/<show>/fanart.jpg`, and 77 held Jellyfin's internal `/config/data/metadata/...` cache. Cause: the library's NFOs were written by Jellyfin, which records art paths as its own container sees them, and `push_artwork` stored any non-URL value verbatim as a local file. Pharos mounts the library at `/var/lib/pharos/media`, so those paths name nothing, and because the NFO ref outranks the sidecar scan the real `fanart.jpg` beside `tvshow.nfo` never won the role. Fix: `locate_local_art` joins a relative art path without `..` to the NFO's folder, replaces an absolute path that does not exist with the file of the same name beside the NFO, and drops a path that still names no file. Rows already stored keep their dead path until the file is probed again or the rows are reset. Test: `metadata::nfo::tests::a_jellyfin_art_path_is_found_beside_its_nfo`, `a_relative_art_path_is_read_from_its_nfos_folder`, `an_art_path_naming_no_file_here_is_dropped`, `an_art_url_is_kept_as_written`, `an_art_path_climbing_out_of_its_nfos_folder_is_dropped`, `two_art_paths_found_as_one_file_are_kept_once`, `a_dead_nfo_art_path_gives_way_to_the_show_folders_image`. Guard: V484.
B580|2026-11-05|A SONG'S OR ALBUM'S BREADCRUMB TRAIL STOPPED AT THE ALBUM, SO IT NEVER LED BACK TO THE MUSIC LIBRARY. Cause: the native UI builds the trail by walking each item's parent through `Library.detail`, and the synthesised album and artist DTOs (`synth_album_or_artist`) set no `ParentId`. A track named its album and the walk ended there, while a film or episode reached its library (B318). Fix: an album names its album artist as its parent (or its library when it has no artist tag), and an artist names the library holding most of its visible tracks (lowest id on a tie, so the answer does not depend on track order), matching the music browse (library → artists → albums → tracks). Test: `it_rpc::library::a_tracks_parents_climb_through_album_and_artist_to_its_library`. Guard: V258.
B581|2026-11-05|RULES AFTER THE COMPACT PLAYER'S NARROW-WINDOW BLOCK APPLIED ONLY IN A WINDOW UNDER 40REM WIDE (the admin Duplicates table, and anything appended since). The `@media (max-width: 40rem)` block added in 16cad500 was never closed, so the browser read the rest of the sheet as inside it.|Close the block. Guard: `theme::css::tests::the_sheet_closes_every_block`.
B582|2026-11-05|STARTING A FILM OR EPISODE DID NOT GO FULLSCREEN. Reported by Alison: a play from a title page or an episode row opened the watch page inside the browser window, so every play needed the player's own Fullscreen button too. Browsers grant the Fullscreen API only inside a user gesture, and the player mounts after the click, once the route has changed, so it cannot ask for itself.|The play click itself asks: `Link { plays }` on the title page Play/Resume and the episode row Play, and the Play from start button, call `stage::fullscreen_for_play`, which puts the whole page in fullscreen (`platform::enter_page_fullscreen`) when `plays_fullscreen` holds: a film or an episode, not playing into the floating window or mini bar, and no video in picture-in-picture. Songs never go fullscreen. The page, not the player, is fullscreen, so the next episode's new player keeps it; the player leaving the full view (Back, Shrink, Close) leaves it (`exit_page_fullscreen`, which leaves the player's own F-key fullscreen alone). Guard: V498.
B583|2026-11-05|THE FLOATING PLAYER COULD NOT BE RESIZED. Reported by Alison: the floating window was fixed at `min(20rem, 60vw)` wide, too small to watch from and with no way to change it.|A corner handle (`StageGrip`, stage.rs) on the top corner away from the edge the window sits nearest (`Grip::for_spot`; the bottom strip carries Close, so never a bottom corner). Dragging it, or the arrow keys on it, sets the width through `resized`, which keeps the opposite bottom corner put, ties the height to the 16:9 picture, and keeps the window between 240 px (`MIN_WIDTH_PX`) and the viewport. The width is an inline `--pharos-stage-user-w` the CSS caps at 100vw, kept for the tab in `pharos.stage.width`. A song's window is the strip alone and has no handle. Guard: V499.
B578|2026-11-05|EVERY LIBRARY TRANSITION BUT THE RAIN ANIMATED THE FIRST ROWS, THEN THE REST OF THE SCREEN APPEARED AT ONCE. Alison: "the transition works great for a couple rows then the rest of the ones on the screen happen suddenly". Cause: browse.rs `arrival` capped each tile's place in the wave at 24 (`ARRIVAL_WAVE`), so on a desktop grid of eight or nine columns the tiles from the third row on all shared one delay and landed together; the rain was spared because it staggers by its own scattered place. Fix: no cap, and web.rs `stage_arrival` places each arriving tile by its row plus column on screen (`--fx-wave`, from `browse::arrival_cells`), so the wave runs corner to corner over however many rows are in view and ends about a second after it starts. Test: `browse::tests::the_tiles_from_the_first_in_view_arrive_and_each_has_its_place`, `browse::tests::arriving_tiles_know_their_row_and_column_on_screen`. Guard: V476.
B579|2026-11-05|A LIBRARY OPENED BACK WHERE THE VIEWER LEFT IT PLAYED NO TRANSITION, SO A CHANGED SETTING SEEMED NOT TO TAKE. Alison was "fairly certain they don't always actually switch when you change in settings". The setting itself applied every time (checked in Firefox: `data-library-transition` and each tile's `animation-name` followed every change). Cause: only the grid's first 60 tiles carried `pharos-arrive`, but a library reopened by Back, a breadcrumb or its library button scrolls to where the viewer was (B560), so the tiles in view were often past the 60th and none animated, or sat between the 24th and 60th and landed together. Fix: `PosterList` takes `arrive_from`, the tile `PagedGrid` restores to, and the 60 tiles from there arrive, with the 30 before it, since a place near a library's end cannot scroll to the top of the screen; once the grid has scrolled back, `stage_arrival` counts rows from the first in view, so the wave starts on screen. Test: `browse::tests::the_tiles_from_the_first_in_view_arrive_and_each_has_its_place`, `browse::tests::arriving_tiles_know_their_row_and_column_on_screen`. Guard: V476.
B585|2026-11-05|A WATCH-TOGETHER GROUP SAT PAUSED WHILE ONE MEMBER PLAYED ON. Retest of B568 (Alison on Zen, Jana on Chrome, ~21:50Z, Jana's diagnostics on). At 21:51:55.79 Jana's client sent `buffering(true)` at 26 761 ms; the server froze the group (V19, the freeze `Pause` goes to everyone but her) and held it 27 843 ms until Jana pressed pause (`buffering freeze lifted outcome=superseded still_buffering=1`). She was not starved: segment 5 had been served 4 s earlier, segments 6-10 then came in pairs every ~12 s (real-time consumption) at 4-8 ms each, and her `groupDrift` stayed at -132..-136 ms the whole time against her own unfrozen clock, so her video kept playing. The same shape froze her solo group at 21:50:11 (3.3 s in, 12 s buffered) until the 30 s anti-wedge. CAUSE: 0d7f4624 (2026-11-04) mapped the video's `stalled` event to `MediaEvent::Waiting`. Chrome fires `stalled` on an MSE element when nothing has been appended for 3 s, which hls.js does routinely once its buffer target is met, while playback continues; both reports land ~3.5 s after her last segment append. No `canplay`/`playing` follows a `stalled` that never dropped `readyState`, so `buffering(false)` never went. Firefox (Alison) does not fire it, which is why only the Chrome member ever stalled.|`onstalled` no longer reaches the player's media events; only `waiting` reports running out of data (V501). Backstop for any recovery the video does not announce: once the playhead has moved `PLAYING_AGAIN_MS` (500) past where buffering was reported, with the video not paused, the member reports buffering over and logs `pharos: group buffering ended reason=playhead_moved` (V502). Guards: `buffering_ends_once_the_playhead_moves_on_its_own`, `a_paused_video_does_not_end_buffering_by_time` (verified red with the backstop disarmed). Query for a recurrence: `sum by (outcome) (pharos_syncplay_buffering_freeze_total)` — `superseded` and `anti_wedge` freezes with a member whose `groupDrift` stays flat.
B586|2026-11-05|A GROUP MEMBER BEHIND ON A SLOW SEGMENT WAS HARD-SEEKED FURTHER BEHIND. Same session as B585: Jana's segment 3 took 2 046 ms to transcode, she reported buffering at 21:51:46, was hard-seeked at 21:51:47 (drift -2 378 ms) onto segments not yet fetched (segment 5: 1 908 ms), and again at 21:51:52 (-3 200 ms). `check_drift` held off only for a wait held inside our own seek's grace (`held_waiting`), not for one already reported as buffering.|`check_drift` also holds off while `buffering_sent` is set; the member loads where it is and the group's freeze and resume bring it back in line (V503). Guard: `a_buffering_member_is_not_drift_seeked` (verified red with the check disarmed).
B584|2026-11-05|NFO-IDENTIFIED EPISODES NEVER GOT THEIR TVDB RECORD, SO B570'S RENUMBERING FIX NEVER REACHED THEM. After B570 deployed, resetting 123 renumbered episodes (TNG, The A-Team, Voyager and six more) brought back the same wrong titles, and the backfill logged "detail fetch returned nothing — id resolved but no record" for 91 of them. Cause: an episode NFO written by Jellyfin or Kodi holds the episode's own TVDB id (`<tvdbid>46511</tvdbid>` for TNG S01E03, show 71470), which the NFO provider stores as the item's tvdb id, and the backfill passed it to `get_episode` as a SERIES id, asking for `/series/46511/episodes/default`. TVDB had no such series, so the row was stamped and kept its NFO text; B570's tests passed because the fake enricher accepts any id. An episode id that is also a real series id could instead return another show's episode at the same numbers. Fix: `episode_series` asks `/episodes/{id}` first, and when that names an episode in the file's season within three numbers of the file's (the most B570 moves one) it reads the episode's `seriesId` and fetches that series' list, where B570 places the record by name. A 404, a record with no series or numbers, or an episode further off keeps the id as a series id (an episode with no NFO inherits the show's id from `tvshow.nfo`) and is not asked about again; a failed lookup fails the record so it is retried. An id that is both a series id and another show's episode at nearby numbers would still be misread; that coincidence is accepted. The stored provider id stays the episode's. Test: `tvdb::tests::an_episode_id_is_read_through_to_its_series`, `an_id_is_a_series_id_unless_its_episode_is_at_the_files_numbers`. Guard: V504.
B587|2026-11-05|EPISODES NAMED IN KODI'S `1x16` FORM OUTSIDE A SEASON FOLDER WERE STORED AS MOVIES. Six Star Trek: The Next Generation files at the show folder's top level (`Star Trek The Next Generation - 1x16 - Too Short a Season.avi`, and `1x01x02` for the two-part pilot) were kind `movie`, so they were missing from the show's season 1 and showed up among the films. Five of them are the library's only copy of their episode. Cause: `is_episode_path` promotes a file to an episode only on an `S01E02` token or a season folder, while `parse_series_info` already reads the numbers from `1x16`, so the form was understood but never reached it. Fix: `has_nxnn_token` recognises a standalone token of one or two season digits, `x`, then two or three episode digits (followed by a separator or another `x`, and by ` - ` for a two-digit season), which keeps a frame size such as `1920x1080`, a title's `2x4` and a film's `10x10` out. Files already stored keep kind `movie` until they are probed again. Test: `fs::tests::an_nxnn_token_names_an_episode`. Guard: V505.

B590|2026-11-05|THE MUSIC QUEUE LOST ITS MIX BUTTON WHILE THE TAB WAS IN A GROUP ON A FILM. Alison started Watch together on The Accountant from the player (V479) at 23:13, a group of one, then played songs; the Up next panel showed only Shuffle and Clear. Every gate on the player (`queued`, autoplay, the episode's up next, play/pause/seek routing) asked only whether a membership was active, so the song was treated as the group's: Mix hid as it does in a group (spec 024), and her pause and seek on the song were sent to the film's group, whose Play and Pause in turn moved the song. Fix: the session records the item the group plays (`GroupState::item`, from `GroupUpdate::Item`); the group drives the stage's player, its controls and its track picks only while the stage shows that item (`Session::drives`, `GroupView::drives`). A member on another item stays in the group, with its member strip and Leave, and plays that item on their own. Tests: `a_group_does_not_drive_a_player_showing_another_item`, `a_group_that_has_not_named_its_item_drives_the_player`, e2e `a song opened while in a group on a film plays on its own, with Mix`. Guard: V508.
B572|2026-11-05|THE NATIVE PLAYER DREW ASS TRACKS WITHOUT THE FILE'S OWN FONTS, AND A SIDECAR .ASS NEVER PLAYED STYLED. Spec 026 slice 0. JASSUB was given only its default face, so a style naming a font the MKV ships fell back to it; the native track list called every sidecar `webvtt`, so `styled_subtitle_url` never took the `.ass`/`.ssa` ones to `stream.ass`. Fix: `Library.detail` lists the file's font attachments (`fonts`, schemaVersion 19), `GET /videos/{id}/subtitles/{index}/fonts/{n}` serves each cut to the glyphs the track draws (skera; `subsetter` strips the cmap table libass needs), the UI hands them to JASSUB, and a sidecar script is listed as `ass`/`ssa` in the native detail only (the Jellyfin wire keeps `webvtt` behind `Stream.vtt`). Guard: V488, V489.
B588|2026-11-05|RENUMBERED EPISODES KEPT THEIR NFO'S WRONG TITLES WHEN TVDB AGREED WITH THE FILENAMES. After B584 let NFO-identified episodes reach TVDB, the reset of 123 renumbered episodes fixed 37 of 38 The A-Team episodes and none of TNG, Voyager, The Six Million Dollar Man, Roseanne, Quantum Leap, Pluribus, Buffy or Sliders. For those shows TVDB's default order matches Sonarr's filenames (S01E03 is "The Naked Now" in both), and only Jellyfin's NFOs were written in a shifted order ("Code of Honor"). B570 adopts the record's text only when the record moves off the file's number, and here it does not, so local data won and the NFO's title, plot and still stayed. Fix: the TVDB record now carries the names of its season neighbours within B570's shift (`neighbour_titles`), and `nfo_names_another` also adopts the record when its title matches the filename's episode name (`title_similarity` ≥ 0.9) while the stored title matches a neighbour's instead, logged with the file name, stored title and record title. A stored title that is the same episode's name in another language matches no neighbour and stays. Test: `metadata_backfill::tests::a_record_named_as_the_file_replaces_an_nfo_naming_another`, `an_nfo_title_in_another_language_stays`, `an_episode_on_its_own_number_keeps_its_local_text_and_still`, `tvdb::tests::an_episode_record_names_its_neighbours`. Guard: V506.
B589|2026-11-05|THE MUSIC LIBRARY TOOK 2-3 S TO LOAD AFTER EVERY DEPLOY WHILE TV AND MOVIES TOOK ~100 MS. The artist listing files a track under its album artist, but the synth poster map keyed artists only by track artist, so the five album-artist-only names on the live server ("Various Artists", "Unknown" and three more) never resolved from it, and each miss read the whole 15.5k-row `media_items` table uncached to confirm its negative: five ~400 ms scans per artist page whenever the memo was cold (a restart or an artwork change). Fix: `build_synth_image_map` also keys each track under `filed_artist`, the listing's own rule; an uncached confirm scan runs at most once per library generation and artwork epoch; a library change drops the memo's negatives. Tests: `images::tests::an_album_artist_with_no_track_of_its_own_resolves_from_the_map`, `images::tests::unknown_synth_ids_confirm_against_the_store_once_per_generation`. Guard: V507.
B496|2026-11-04|ENHANCED-LRC WORD TIMES WERE THROWN AWAY. Follows B495: the karaoke fill estimated where each word fell even when the lyrics file timed every word, because the LRC parser dropped `<mm:ss.xx>` word stamps. Fix: the parser keeps each stamp as a char offset into the line's cleaned text and a time, shifted by `[offset:]` like line stamps; a line repeated under several line stamps keeps none (word times are absolute) and out-of-order stamps are dropped. The disk cache keeps them (old entries read as none), the native schema carries them as `LyricLine.words` (v15), and the client reads them. In karaoke mode a stamped line fills straight from stamp to stamp, and on by syllables after the last stamp if words follow untimed. Most LRCLIB lyrics time lines only, so this helps stamped sidecar and embedded files today and is the shape forced alignment will fill (B497). Guard: V440.
B497|2026-11-05|NO WORD TIMES FOR MOST LYRICS. Follows B496: LRCLIB and most files time lines only, so the karaoke fill stays an estimate. Fix: `pharos-aligner` (ADR-0066), a separate Python service on nixpkgs torch/torchaudio answering `POST /align` with word stamps: each line's window (its LRC span plus 250 ms) is decoded with ffmpeg from the shared media mount and force-aligned with torchaudio's MMS_FA wav2vec2 model, one request at a time. Words fold to the model's a-z alphabet; words with no Latin letters are not stamped. Packaged as flake outputs `aligner` and `alignerOci`, with the 1.2 GB weights fetched at build time. CI does not publish it. Checked by hand on Moby "Run On": 12 lines aligned in 25 s on CPU. Guard: V441.
B498|2026-11-05|THE SERVER COULD NOT ASK FOR WORD TIMES. Fix: `Library.lyrics` takes `wantWords` and `Lyrics` carries `wordsPending` (schema v20). With `[lyrics].aligner_url` set (`PHAROS_LYRICS_ALIGNER_URL`), a call that wants words for synced lyrics without them queues one job on a bounded, de-duplicated queue drained by a single task, which sends the song's path under `[lyrics].aligner_media_root` (default `/var/lib/pharos/media`) and its lines to the aligner. Answers are kept in `{id}.words.json` beside the lyrics cache, keyed on a SHA-256 of path, file size, mtime and lines. Word stamps from the source win. Off unless configured. Guard: V441.
B499|2026-11-05|KARAOKE NEVER PICKED UP ALIGNED WORD TIMES. Fix: with karaoke on when a song starts, the lyrics call wants words. While the answer says `wordsPending`, the screen asks again every 20 s, up to 15 times, and swaps in the aligned lines, kept with their song. Turning karaoke on mid-song asks on the song's next play. Signal: client log `pharos: lyrics word times arrived`. Guard: V441.
B591|2026-11-05|THE NOW-PLAYING SCREEN NAMED A SONG BUT NOT WHO SANG IT OR WHAT IT WAS FROM. Asked for by Alison: the line under the title showed only the year and genres, so a song from a shuffled queue or a mix could not be told apart from another of the same name. The native `ItemDetail` carried no artist or album, so the screen had nothing to show.|`ItemDetail` carries the song's track `artists` and `album` (additive, schemaVersion 21; an older server sends neither and the line leaves them out), and `ListenView`'s line reads "Artist · Album · Track 3 · 1998 · Genre", leaving out each part it lacks. The track number is the summary's `indexNumber`. The line now stays on one line with an ellipsis, as the title does, and its full text is its tooltip. Test: `listen::tests::the_meta_line_names_the_artist_album_and_track_first`, `it_rpc::detail_subtitle_scripts::a_songs_detail_names_its_artists_and_album`. Guard: V509.
B602|2026-11-06|SPEC 028 SLICE 2: THE SERVER HAD NO PER-LINE AUDIO SIGNAL TO DIRECT SUBTITLE EFFECTS FROM. The libav worker offered a whole-file RMS waveform and one 30 s tempo/energy window, nothing per subtitle line and no scene cuts. Fix: `TinyOp::SubtitleSignals` reads the file once: it decodes the subtitle-timed audio stream (front-centre channel when the layout has one, else a downmix) at 11 025 Hz for per-line BS.1770 K-weighted loudness, peak, YIN pitch and the share of energy above 3.4 kHz, and reads video packet key flags without decoding for `cuts_from_keyframes` (keyframes off the median GOP cadence). Negative start pts are dropped, gaps filled with capped silence, read errors counted and bounded. Tests: `subtitle_signals::tests::*`, `it_libav::libav_subtitle_signals::{front_centre_loudness_pitch_and_cuts, the_pool_carries_the_op_through_a_worker}`. Guard: V534.
B600|2026-11-06|WHETHER A STYLED SUBTITLE TRACK WENT OUT STYLED OR AS PLAIN TEXT COULD NOT BE SEEN. Before B572 every sidecar `.ass` went to the native player as WebVTT and drew as plain text, and nothing on the server said so: the `stream.ass` and `stream.vtt` handlers logged no outcome, and the player's fall back from JASSUB to plain text (`not ready after N ms`, a worker error) was only a browser console warning. Fix: both handlers count `pharos_subtitle_delivery_total{format,source,codec_class,outcome}` on success and failure alike, with one `subtitle delivery` log line carrying the codec, index, outcome, error and reason; `format="vtt",codec_class="styled"` is an ASS/SSA track going out as plain text: a client without an ASS renderer, or the native player's hidden `<track>` fallback, which it fetches beside `stream.ass`, so the ratio of `ass` to `vtt` for styled tracks is the signal rather than either count alone. The player logs `pharos: subtitle renderer=jassub mode=styled|plain reason=…` and sends it as a `rendererMode` client event (opted-in users). Tests: `it_media_ffmpeg::subtitle_delivery::subtitle_deliveries_are_counted_by_form_and_class`, `subtitles::tests::delivery_labels_are_distinct`, `playback::tests::a_styled_subtitle_fallback_is_logged_and_sent_with_its_reason`. Guard: V531.
B598|2026-11-06|THE TV LIBRARY PAGE TOOK ~400 MS TO LIST AND ~350 MS TO INDEX, AND OPENING A SHOW ~330 MS, WHILE MOVIES LISTED IN ~30 MS. The series grid folds every episode in the library into series tiles, and its listing and its letter index each read every episode with all ~80 columns, decoding each one's subtitle, audio, chapter and attachment JSON and long text that the fold never reads (~270 ms of server CPU per call on the live library). Resolving the library as a parent also read all its item ids just to see it was non-empty, twice per listing (~50 ms each). A synthesised series or season detail found its show by scanning the whole cached item list, which a 60 s age limit left cold on most visits (~260 ms). Fix: `MediaStore::query_shallow` selects those stream and text columns as NULL and the series fold reads through it; `LibraryStore::library_has_items` is an `EXISTS` probe; series and season detail resolve their id through the synth parent memo and read only that show's rows. Tests: `backend_conformance` (`query_shallow` rows equal `query` rows less the omitted fields; `library_has_items`), `it_items::jellyfin_items` series and season counts. Guard: V519.
B598|2026-11-06|THE TV LIBRARY PAGE TOOK ~400 MS TO LIST AND ~350 MS TO INDEX, AND OPENING A SHOW OR A BOX SET ~330 MS, WHILE MOVIES LISTED IN ~30 MS. The series grid folds every episode in the library into series tiles, and its listing and its letter index each read every episode with all ~80 columns, decoding each one's subtitle, audio, chapter and attachment JSON and long text that the fold never reads (~270 ms of server CPU per call on the live library). Resolving the library as a parent also read all its item ids just to see it was non-empty, twice per listing (~50 ms each). Every non-numeric detail id (a show, a season, a box set and more) was first tried as a series or season by scanning the whole cached item list, which a 60 s age limit left cold on most visits (~260 ms). Fix: `MediaStore::query_shallow` selects those stream and text columns as NULL and the series fold reads through it; `LibraryStore::library_has_items` is an `EXISTS` probe; series and season detail resolve their id through the synth parent memo and read only that show's rows. Tests: `backend_conformance` (`query_shallow` rows equal `query` rows less the omitted fields; `library_has_items`), `it_items::jellyfin_items` series counts, `it_rpc::library::browsing_a_library_a_series_and_a_season_answers_detail_then_listing`. Guard: V519.
B599|2026-11-06|A TV SHOW OPENED FROM A SCROLLED LIBRARY GRID OPENED PART WAY DOWN ITS EPISODE LIST, NOT AT ITS NAME AND OVERVIEW. Navigating in place never resets the window scroll, and only the library grid sets it for itself (V470); the show page kept whatever offset the grid it replaced had, clamped to its own height. Fix: `ShowView` scrolls to the top whenever the series it shows changes, on opening included; a season tab keeps the series and so keeps the scroll. B597|2026-11-06|THE CTRL+K SEARCH LISTED EPISODES AND SONGS BUT NEVER A SHOW, ARTIST OR ALBUM, AND "S01E02" MATCHED NOTHING. Native `Library.search` answered only stored rows ranked by full-text score, so "dragon ball" listed Dragon Ball GT episodes (some wrongly titled with the show's name) instead of the show, "moby" one song rather than the artist, "signify" tracks but not the album, and "dragon ball s01", "s1", "s01e02" or "s01e2" found nothing. Arrow keys could also walk the pick below the palette's visible rows. Fix: `services::search` lists, for a name of three or more characters, the shows it names (whole name, start, later word, anywhere; shorter first), then the artists, an exactly named artist's own albums, and the albums it names, before the stored rows; a trailing `s01`/`s1`/`s01e02`/`s01e2`/`s1 e2` swaps each show for that season or episode. The palette scrolls the least it takes to show the picked row, and a season hit names its show. Tests: `services::search::tests::*`, `it_rpc::library::native_search_names_a_show_then_its_season_or_episode`, `native_search_names_an_artist_with_albums_and_an_album_before_its_tracks`, the dioxus e2e `palette: a show, its season and its episode by name, and the pick stays in sight`. Guard: V518.
B601|2026-11-06|SPEC 028 SLICE 1: THERE WAS NO WAY TO TURN AN EFFECT PLAN INTO TIER-BOUNDED ASS. Spec 028 directs subtitle effects per line, but nothing compiled a plan and the source subtitles into a script a device could draw within its budget. Fix: the `pharos-styling` crate holds the plan types, a reader for SRT, WebVTT and ASS sources (bad cues skipped and counted, source signs kept with their own style lines), a budget checker per device tier, the pharos theme, the compiler, the ten effects (Impact, Tremor, Hush, Inner voice, Echo, Wire, Memory, Caption, Aside, Burst) with the karaoke fills, and a cross-line limiter that drops a line to the next cheaper tier's form when animated events on screen or event starts per 10 s would break a budget. Source signs pass through unchanged and are exempt from the checker; a source that itself starts over 60 events in 10 s breaches only the rate rule, as the compiler never drops lines. Tests: `compile::tests::same_inputs_same_bytes`, `compile::tests::every_effect_keeps_every_budget`, `compile::tests::typeset_events_pass_through_with_their_own_style_line`, `compile::tests::a_source_denser_than_six_starts_a_second_breaches_only_the_rate_rule`, `budget::tests::passthrough_events_are_exempt_from_every_rule`, `it_styling::compile_props::{compiled_output_parses_and_keeps_budgets, subtle_caps_intensity_at_one_and_off_is_plain}`. Guard: V532, V533.
B605|2026-11-06|SPEC 028 SLICE 5: THE UI HAD NO WAY TO CHOOSE SUBTITLE EFFECTS AND NO RULE FOR WHICH STYLED-TRACK TIER A DEVICE STARTS ON. Spec 028 (US2, FR-006, FR-018): the styled track is fetched at `?tier=low|tv|desktop`, and the viewer must be able to calm it. Added `EffectsLevel` (Off, Subtle, Full; absent or unknown reads as Subtle) kept per device under `pharos.subtitles.effects`, with an Effects select on Settings → Appearance, stored like the subtitle size (session choice outranks storage; a failed write says the choice applies until reload). Added the pure `Tier`, `DeviceClass`, `device_class` and `starting_tier`: reduced motion gives Low, Tv and Phone give Tv, Desktop gives Desktop, capped at the tier the device last dropped to; `current_starting_tier` reads the user agent, `(pointer: coarse)`, `(max-width: 600px)` and `prefers-reduced-motion` and logs `pharos: subtitle tier=… device=… reduced_motion=… remembered=…`. The player does not fetch the styled track yet (T028-19). Tests: `effects_level_round_trips_and_falls_back_to_subtle`, `effects_level_presses_only_the_stored_choice`, `effects_level_defaults_to_subtle`, `a_chosen_effects_level_outlives_a_storage_that_keeps_nothing`, `tier_labels_are_the_servers_tier_query_values`, `device_class_follows_the_user_agent_pointer_and_shell`, `starting_tier_follows_device_motion_and_the_remembered_drop`, `the_starting_tier_log_line_names_every_input`. B592|2026-11-06|A FILM OR SHOW FOUND FROM THE SEARCH BOX COULD NOT BE PLAYED WITHOUT THE MOUSE. Opening a title page left the focus in the search box (or on nothing), so Enter did nothing and Tab walked the whole top bar first; a show page had no Play at all, only per-episode buttons, and nothing moved between seasons or episodes from the keyboard. Fix: spec 029 keyboard modes. A title page focuses its Play or Resume when it opens (without scrolling) and a show page gains a Play or Resume for the next episode of the season shown, focused the same way; arrows and h/j/k/l move between controls by position, `[`/`]` step the season tabs, `p`/`r`/`t`/`T`/`w` press play, play from start, watch together (from start) and mark watched, and the bindings come from one table with a which-key panel (`g`, `?`). Tests: compat-playwright `keyboard.spec.ts`, `keymap::tests`. Guard: V510.
B615|2026-11-06|A LIBRARY TILE LANDING UNDER A STILL POINTER JUMPED LEFT AND UP AS ITS TRANSITION ENDED. Alison: "the Fade animation on tv shows seem to weirdly shift left and up suddenly towards the end", in Matrix rain and Warp too. Cause: the poster's hover lift (`.pharos-poster:hover .pharos-poster-art`: up, scaled 1.05 from the centre) applies to whichever tile ends up under the pointer, and a grid that remounts, as when the viewer comes back to a library from a show, arrives under a pointer that has not moved. The rain and the warp bring the tile in from elsewhere, so `:hover` turns true only as it lands and the lift starts right at the end; under the fade it lifts while it rises. A local seeded run logged the first tile at rest go from x 24, y 298, 157 px wide to x 20, y 284, 165 px wide once the transition ended, with no pointer move. Fix: web.rs `stage_arrival` sets `data-lift-held` on each grid whose tiles it places and drops it on the next `pointermove`. The sheet then keeps the lift and its glow off a hovered (not focused) poster in a held grid. The same run with the fix kept the tile at rest at x 24, y 298 until the pointer moved, then lifted it with its usual transition. Tests: `theme::css::tests::an_arrived_grid_holds_the_hover_lift_but_not_focus`. Guard: V550.
B611|2026-11-06|AVIF POSTERS WERE BIGGER THAN THE JPEG THEY REPLACED. Found on the live server: at fillWidth=480, 9 of 12 movie posters came back larger as AVIF than as JPEG (47.8 kB against 36.7 kB). ADR-0063 tuned crf 22 against an ffmpeg CLI `-q:v 3` JPEG, but production's libav MJPEG encoder sets `global_quality` without the QSCALE flag, so it is rate-controlled and much lower quality (SSIMULACRA2 about 67 against 79). Fix: crf 31, re-tuned against an emulation of the production JPEG (0.74-0.9x its bytes, 2.5-8 points better), `ENCODE_VERSION` 2, and the route sends the JPEG whenever an encoded copy is bigger (ADR-0068, V546).|
B612|2026-11-06|THE WATCH PAGE'S NEIGHBOUR LOOKUPS STALLED FOR UP TO 600 MS AFTER EVERY LIBRARY CHANGE. Tempo showed `rpc.library.query` for the watch page's neighbours at about 600 ms idle against 5 ms busy. Cause: B566's synth parent memo rebuilt whenever the library generation moved (about every 20 minutes live), and every request for a series, season, artist or album id waited on the build mutex while one request ran four `SELECT DISTINCT` scans one after another (94-511 ms). Fix: an id the memo knows answers from it at once and starts one background rebuild; only an unknown id waits. The four scans run concurrently, and the wait has its own span (V547).
B603|2026-11-06|SPEC 028 SLICE 3: NOTHING CHOSE WHICH SUBTITLE LINES GET AN EFFECT. The compiler (B601) draws whatever plan it is given, but no code turned a track and its audio signals into one. Fix: `pharos_styling::direct` runs the plan.md rules in order, first match wins (Sfx, Narration, Phone, Whisper, Thought, OffScreen, AttackCall, Shout, karaoke Sweep inside an Intro or Outro, else Plain), with a `reason` per line naming the rule and its inputs. Loudness, pitch and high-band medians are taken per Intro, Outro and rest, Shout needs 3 LU more inside an Intro or Outro, and the capitals rule is off on a track that is 80% capitals. A line with no usable audio matches text rules only. A line that spans a scene cut keeps its intent and gets `static_only`, which `compile` draws in its Low form (FR-011). Typeset source lines stay Plain. The `subtitle_styling` background job (spec 028 FR-014) styles anime and animation videos one at a time behind a `bg_io` permit, waits while transcodes are queued, takes the opened item first, and stores the plan against the file signature (version-only rows for undecodable or source-less items). Signal: `pharos_subtitle_styling_total{outcome,stage}`, `pharos_subtitle_effect_total{intent,source}`.
B606|2026-11-06|SPEC 028 SLICE 6: NOTHING WATCHED THE VIDEO'S DROPPED FRAMES, SO A STYLED SUBTITLE TRACK COULD NOT FALL TO A CHEAPER TIER ON A DEVICE THAT COULD NOT KEEP UP. Spec 028 (US2, FR-019, T028-20): added the pure `TierDrop` state machine fed `getVideoPlaybackQuality` counters once a second. Over budget means the dropped-frame delta is more than 5% of the presented-frame delta across a full 10 s sliding window (a partial window never counts; frames dropped with none presented do; samples from a hidden page or a paused or stalled video, and backwards counters, restart the window); it then drops one tier (Desktop to Tv to Low), at most once per 30 s, never below Low, and clears the window. A drop is remembered per device under `pharos.subtitles.tier_drop` as `<tier>@<unix_ms>` (session choice outranks storage; it never rises, expires after 14 days, and unknown reads as none) and caps `current_starting_tier`, logged as `pharos: subtitle tier drop from=… to=… reason=dropped_frames dropped=… presented=… window_ms=…`, and sent as a `rendererMode` client event with `renderer`, `from`, `to`, `reason`, `dropped`, `presented` and `window_ms` (the event's free-form fields carry them, so the wire type is unchanged). `on_quality_sample` is the glue; the player does not sample yet because the styled track is not served (T028-19, T028-21 wiring). Tests: `a_steady_good_stream_never_drops`, `a_sustained_bad_stream_drops_once_per_cooldown`, `the_cooldown_spaces_drops_thirty_seconds_apart`, `low_is_the_floor`, `a_partial_window_does_nothing`, `exactly_five_percent_is_within_budget`, `counters_going_backwards_restart_the_window`, `a_recovered_stream_stops_dropping`, `a_stored_tier_reads_back_and_unknown_is_none`, `the_tier_drop_log_line_names_the_measurement`, `the_tier_drop_report_is_a_renderer_mode_event_with_the_measurement`, `a_dropped_tier_outlives_a_storage_that_keeps_nothing`, `the_starting_tier_is_capped_at_the_dropped_one`, `a_bad_stream_drops_the_tier_once_and_remembers_it`, `samples_while_not_counting_restart_the_window`, `dropped_frames_with_none_presented_are_over_budget`, `a_stored_cap_lasts_fourteen_days_and_a_bare_tier_still_counts`, `a_later_drop_never_raises_the_stored_cap`, `a_hidden_page_never_drops_the_tier`. Guard: V536.
B616|2026-11-06|CTRL+K REOPENED WITH THE LAST SEARCH STILL TYPED. The palette and the top bar share one search, so opening the palette again showed the previous term and its matches instead of a fresh box. Fix (Slack's behaviour): Ctrl+K / Cmd+K and the keyboard layer's palette key clear the search as they open it, and a blank palette lists at most `palette::SEARCH_LIMIT` (5) past searches first, under "Recent searches"; a term is kept (localStorage `pharos.search.recent`, newest first, once in any case) when a match found by it is opened, and picking a past search searches it again without closing. The top bar's Enter still opens the palette on its own term. Tests: `palette::tests::a_blank_term_lists_past_searches_first_and_they_go_nowhere`, `a_search_is_remembered_once_newest_first_and_only_the_last_few`, the dioxus e2e `palette: a show, its season and its episode by name, and the pick stays in sight`. Guard: V551.
B617|2026-11-06|IN THE PLAYER, `g h` SEEKED BACK 10 S INSTEAD OF GOING HOME, AND NO OTHER `g` SEQUENCE WORKED THERE. Alison: "gh also doesn't work that ends up seeking back. gq and the other 'g' shortcuts also don't work". The video player and the music full player read their transport keys in their own `onkeydown`, which Dioxus runs before the page-wide key layer hears the key, so with a `g` begun the player took `h` as Seek back, `l` as Seek forward, and so on, and the layer never saw the second key. Fix: keys.rs publishes `sequence_pending()` while a sequence waits for its next key, and both players leave every key to the layer while it is set (`not_transport`). Test: compat-playwright `g h in the player goes home rather than seeking back (B617)`, in Chromium and Firefox. Guard: V552.
B618|2026-11-06|THE PLAYER'S KEYS (`m`, `f`, SPACE, THE ARROWS, …) DID NOTHING UNTIL THE PLAYER ITSELF HAD THE FOCUS. Alison: "When in the player the M and F mappings don't seem to work". Pressing Play from a title page removes the focused button, so the watch page opens with the focus on the page body; only the player element's own `onkeydown` reads the transport keys, and the key layer passed them on unhandled. A local seeded run in Chromium and Firefox showed `m` leave the video unmuted with the focus on the body, and mute it once the player was focused. Fix: the key layer sends a transport key it hears to the page's player (`#pharos-player` or `.pharos-listen`) when the focus is outside it, focusing the player first; Space on a focused button or link still presses it. Test: compat-playwright `the player's keys work with the focus anywhere on the page (B618)`, in Chromium and Firefox. Guard: V552.
B619|2026-11-06|THE VIDEO PLAYER'S KEY HELP LISTED MUSIC PLAYER KEYS THAT DO NOTHING THERE, AND SQUEEZED ITS SEEK ROW. Alison: "If those keys don't make sense in the context of a full screen player the contextual help is wrong". `F` (open the full player), `M` (mini bar or floating player) and `g q` (now playing) were bound in every mode, so `?` in the video player listed them although the video page has no mini bar or floating window. The ten keys `0` to `9` were listed one by one and pushed "Seek to 0–90 %" into a column one word wide. Fix: those three bindings leave the video player's mode (they stay everywhere else), and the help writes a run of digits as `0–9`. Tests: `keymap::tests::the_video_player_lists_no_music_player_keys`, `keymap::tests::the_seek_to_a_tenth_keys_show_as_one_run`. Guard: V553.
B620|2026-11-06|THE KEY HELP LISTED KEYS THAT DID NOTHING ON THE PAGE IT WAS OPENED ON. Alison: "Verify the contextual help is correct in other contexts too". The help listed every binding of the mode, so a film's page offered the season keys `[` and `]`, a page without a group to watch with offered `t` and `T`, a video library offered the music keys `a`, `A` and `x`, and every page offered `F`, `M` and `g q` with nothing playing. Fix: `keymap::help` takes what the page offers, and the key layer lists a key that presses a control (`data-key-action`), steps tabs (`data-key-tabs`) or focuses an area (`data-key-area`) only when the page shows that control, those tabs or that area. Moving, Go to and General keys are always listed. Tests: `keymap::tests::the_help_lists_only_what_the_page_offers`; compat-playwright `? lists only the keys the page can use`, in Chromium and Firefox. Guard: V554.
B621|2026-11-06|THE PLAYER'S KEYS WENT DEAD AFTER THE VOLUME SLIDER OR A TRACK MENU WAS USED, UNTIL THE VIDEO WAS CLICKED. Alison: "I have had a couple of occasions where buttons like m for mute do nothing. I then click the actual video to give it focus then the keys start working". The volume slider, the music player's seek slider and the subtitle, audio and tracks menus each stopped every key pressed on them from reaching the player, and the focus stays on a slider after it is dragged and on a menu left without a pick. A local seeded run in Chromium and Firefox showed `m` do nothing with the focus on the volume slider. Fix: a slider keeps only the arrows, Home, End, Page Up and Page Down, and a menu those and Enter and Space; every other key goes on to the player. Test: compat-playwright `m still mutes after the volume slider is used (B621)`, in Chromium and Firefox. Guard: V555.
B622|2026-11-06|THE PALETTE'S TEXT HAD TO BE CLEARED WITH CTRL+A BEFORE TYPING. When the palette opened on a term (the top bar's Enter, or any path that kept the last one), the caret sat after it, so typing appended to the old term. Fix: the palette selects its whole field as it opens, so typing replaces whatever it opened on; arrows and Enter still act on its matches. Test: the dioxus e2e `search: the bar lists matches as you type, Enter opens them all, nonsense says no matches`. Guard: V556.
B623|2026-11-06|SHIFT+M DID NOTHING IN THE FULL MUSIC PLAYER. Alison: "When in the full player should the M shortcut work for swapping to the mini player?" Shift+M pressed the size control of the mini bar or floating window, which the full player does not show, so from the full player it did nothing (and, after B620, was left out of its key help). Fix: in the full player and its queue Shift+M presses Shrink, as Escape does there, taking the song to the small player it was last in; on any other page it still switches between the mini bar and the floating window. Tests: `keymap::tests::music_keys_resolve_by_mode`; compat-playwright `an album plays, queues and shrinks from the keyboard`. Guard: V557.
B624|2026-11-06|CTRL+K "GO MOBY" FOUND NOTHING, THOUGH "GO: THE VERY BEST OF MOBY" (ARTIST MOBY) WAS IN THE LIBRARY. Alison: "it should be fuzzy to a degree and take into account multiple things. This should have given me the album Go the best of Moby". The search term was matched as one substring of a single column (title, show, artist or album), and a show, artist or album was named only when its own name held the whole term, so two words from different names, words out of order, or a typo found nothing. Fix: the term is split into words, each of which must be found (any case) in any of the title, show, artist or album, in any order; an album is scored by its own name and its artist's; shows, artists and albums are ranked together, best named first (exact word, then the start of a word, then inside one, then a typo; then the whole phrase in the name, fewer unmatched words, shorter names); and when nothing is found as typed, the search runs again allowing one typo in each word of four letters or more. `pharos_search_total{pass,outcome}` counts which pass answered and whether it found anything. Test: `native_search_names_an_album_by_words_from_its_name_and_artist`, `fuzzy::tests`. Guard: V558.
B625|2026-11-06|KARAOKE LYRICS DID NOT FOLLOW THE SONG WHEN THE FULL PLAYER WAS OPENED FROM THE MINI BAR. Alison: "If I start playing something and am in the mini player and then swap to the full the karaoke lyrics don't start immediately and can fall out of sync." The music player's screen stays mounted behind the mini bar and floating window, lyrics panel included. The panel centres the sung line only when the line changes, and a hidden panel cannot scroll, so on opening the full player it sat at the top with the sung line out of view until the next line began, and stayed off the line on long lines. Fix: the lyrics panel is drawn only while the full player is in sight, so it is drawn fresh as the player opens and centres the line being sung at once. Test: `listen::tests::the_lyrics_panel_is_drawn_only_while_the_screen_is_in_sight`. Guard: V559.
B626|2026-11-07|KARAOKE WORD TIMES SMEARED ACROSS INSTRUMENTAL BREAKS. Alison, testing on Firestarter: "This seems very wrong atm". The aligner matched each line against audio from its own stamp to the next line's, so a line followed by a break got a window that was mostly music, and CTC, with no label for anything but the lyrics, spread the words across all of it: one Firestarter line had a 34 s window and its words landed from 106 s to 123 s. Every song tried had about two such lines. Fix (#904): MMS_FA's star token after each line absorbs the music after it (none before, since a line is sung from its stamp and a leading star let words drift late); a line's window is capped at 0.7 s a word plus 2 s; and an aligner version in the word-time cache key re-aligns songs answered before. On 416 TTS words laid at known times over four library songs, words more than 0.5 s out fell from 31% to 10% (42% to 15% at 100 words a minute). Test: `aligner/tests/test_plan.py::test_window_stops_short_of_a_long_break`. Guard: V560.
B627|2026-11-07|KARAOKE FOLLOWED LYRICS TIMED FOR ANOTHER CUT OF THE SONG. Firestarter on the Ministry of Sound Anthems II compilation is a 2:28 edit, but LRCLIB's entry at that length carries the 4:40 album version's stamps (first line at 0:51, lines to 4:37), so the lit line had nothing to do with what was playing and the aligner forced the words into the wrong audio. Fix (#905): when a synced line starts more than 3 s past the song's probed end, every line's time and word stamps are dropped and the words show as plain lyrics; the check runs on every lookup, so answers already cached are caught. Signal: `pharos_lyrics_unsynced_total{reason="past_song_end",origin}` and info log `lyrics: synced lines run past the song's end, shown unsynced` with `last_line_ms`/`duration_ms`. Test: `services::lyrics::tests::synced_lyrics_past_the_end_of_the_song_lose_their_times`. Guard: V561.
B628|2026-11-07|THE ALIGNER IMAGE WAS NEVER PUBLISHED FROM MAIN. The publish job built `.#alignerOci` with `--no-link`, then ran `nix develop` for the GHCR tag check, which started the build node's auto-GC (B284) and deleted the unrooted stream script; streaming it then failed with "No such file or directory", failing main's publish job twice running after #904 and leaving the cluster on the old aligner. Fix: the script is built with `--out-link /nix/var/nix/gcroots/gha/pharos-aligner-oci`, rooting it in the host's gcroots drop box. Test: none (the publish job runs only on main pushes); signal: the job's `publish: pharos-aligner:<tag> pushed` line.
B629|2026-11-07|THE FFMPEG-SPAWN BUILD DID NOT COMPILE. `state.rs` held an `Option<Arc<IntegrityMemo>>` field and a `with_integrity_memo` setter with no cfg, but `lib.rs` gated `pub mod integrity_memo` behind `ffmpeg-lib`, so `cargo check -p pharos-server --no-default-features --features ffmpeg-spawn` failed with `error[E0433]: cannot find integrity_memo in the crate root`. Nothing in CI builds the spawn feature (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `flake.nix` do not mention it), so it went unnoticed. Fix: `pub mod integrity_memo` is ungated; the module imports only `pharos_transcode::integrity`, which is ungated in pharos-transcode, so no cfg is needed. `integrity_backfill` stays gated (it needs the libav pool). Test: none (no CI job builds the spawn feature); signal: the `cannot find integrity_memo` cargo error line. Guard: none.
B630|2026-11-07|THE SINGLE-FLIGHT TEST FAILED INTERMITTENTLY IN CI. `a_second_requester_awaits_the_result_rather_than_the_lock` placed its second requester with a 100 ms sleep and asserted the run finished within 700 ms of a 400 ms stub encode; on a loaded runner process spawns alone blew that bound (1.10 s measured, encode count still 1), so the failure said nothing about the cache. Reproduced 300/300 failures with the test binary pinned to one CPU beside 8 busy loops (0/300 idle). Fix: the stub encoder blocks on a gate file the test opens once both requesters wait, the first encode start is awaited by state, and the wall-clock assertion is gone (the encode-count assertion carries the property). Test: `hls_cache::tests::a_second_requester_awaits_the_result_rather_than_the_lock` itself, 0/300 failures under the same load. Guard: none new (test-only; no production code was wrong).
B631|2026-11-07|THE CANCELLATION TEST TIMED ITS SETUP WITH SLEEPS. `cancelling_the_first_requester_does_not_abort_the_encode` placed its second requester with a 100 ms sleep and let it attach with a 50 ms sleep, against a 400 ms sleep-based stub. On a starved runner those sleeps could land before the first encode starts or before the second requester attaches, so the encode count would reflect scheduling rather than the cache. Fix: the stub blocks on a gate file that the test opens only after the abort; the first encode start is awaited by state (encode_count == 1), the second requester's attachment by the registry receiver count (== 2), and the abort is confirmed by its JoinError. Test: `hls_cache::tests::cancelling_the_first_requester_does_not_abort_the_encode` itself; not reproduced: 0/200 failures before and after the change with the binary pinned to one CPU beside 8 busy loops. Guard: none new (test-only; no production code was wrong).
B632|2026-11-07|THE SYNCPLAY GROUPJOINED UPDATE SHOWED PLACEHOLDERS. `GroupJoined`'s `GroupInfoDto` hardcoded `GroupName: "SyncPlay"` and `State: "Idle"` in `socket::translate_outbound`, because `ServerMsg::Joined` carried only the group id, leader and roster; a client joining "Movie Night" mid-playback saw an idle group named SyncPlay until the next update. A remote-group handle (non-owner replica) also built its synthetic `Joined` from `""` / `Idle`. Fix: `ServerMsg::Joined` carries `group_name` and `state`, filled from the actor; a remote handle fills them from the owner's persisted snapshot, and with no readable snapshot drops the reply so the owner's `Joined` over delivery is the first the client sees. Test: `group::tests::joined_carries_group_name_and_play_state`, `socket::tests::group_joined_carries_real_name_and_state`, `distributed::tests::a_remote_join_reply_carries_the_snapshot_name_and_state`, `a_remote_join_reply_without_a_snapshot_is_dropped`. Guard: V581.
B633|2026-11-07|JOINING A DEAD GROUPID SPAWNED A PHANTOM GROUP. `/SyncPlay/Join` and the native `/sync/v1/ws` Join called `get_or_create` on any id, so a group that dissolved between `/SyncPlay/List` and Join was recreated empty and the caller landed alone in it instead of getting Jellyfin's `GroupDoesNotExist` toast. Fix: both transports resolve through `sync_recovery::joinable_group`: a live registry group, else a fresh readable persisted snapshot or a lease held by another replica (`held_elsewhere`, since a snapshot's `updated_at` only moves on mutation), else `GroupDoesNotExist` / `unknown group`; the user's access is checked before a cold group is hydrated, and a group the user may not see gets the same answer as an absent one. Test: `it_syncplay::syncplay_join_unknown_group::*`, `item_access::a_native_sync_join_of_a_dead_group_spawns_nothing`. Guard: V582.
B604|2026-11-07|SPEC 028 SLICE 4: THE GENERATED STYLED SUBTITLE TRACK WAS NEVER LISTED OR SERVED. Spec 028 (US1, FR-017, T028-15, T028-16): a stored effect plan had no route to a player. Fix: `SubtitleKind::Styled { tier, level, compiler_version, director_version, plan_stamp }` in `pharos-cache` (disk letter `y`; the name carries `y<tier>.<level>.c<compiler>.d<director>.p<styled_at>`, so a restyle or a version bump never reads an old script; pharos-cache cannot depend on pharos-styling, so the caller passes ordinals and plain numbers). `styled_track::current` returns an item's row only while it has a plan, `director_version` is current, the video file's whole-second mtime and size still equal the row's and, for a sidecar source, the sidecar at that slot still has its signature. `watch::tracks` takes that row and appends "English (Pharos styled)" (`eng`, `ass`, not default) at `sidecar_base + sidecar_count` to the native detail only, with `drawn` the source track's `stream.vtt` and the new `Track.styled` written to capnp `styledUrl`; Jellyfin `MediaStreams` never lists it. A native negotiation naming that index negotiates its source track (text, drawn by the client, no burn) and the grant echoes the pick. `stream.ass` for that index reads `?tier=` (default `tv`) and `?effects=` (default `subtle`), 400 on an unknown value and 404 without a current plan, compiles the plan over the source (an ASS source or any sidecar as the file, an embedded SRT or other text stream from its WebVTT extraction), caches it, and answers `text/x-ssa` with `X-Pharos-Subtitle-Tier`. `stream.vtt`, `stream.js`, `subtitles.srt` and the fonts route answer 404 for the slot by name (burn resolves from the probe's embedded streams, so the slot never reaches it). `DeliverySource` gains `generated`, counted for success and every failure with B600's log fields. Tests: `the_styled_track_is_listed_only_while_a_current_plan_exists`, `the_styled_track_negotiates_as_its_source_track`, `a_tier_and_level_compile_the_plan_into_a_parsing_script`, `the_default_is_tier_tv_and_effects_subtle`, `an_unknown_tier_or_level_is_a_400_and_no_plan_a_404`, `the_same_request_gives_identical_bytes_cached_or_cold`, `an_srt_source_is_styled_too`, `the_plain_routes_refuse_the_styled_index`, `deliveries_are_counted_as_generated`, `styled_names_differ_per_field`. Guard: V532.
B634|2026-11-07|ASS SUBTITLES WERE DRAWN OVER THE CONTROL BAR. JASSUB draws an ASS track (the source script or the spec 028 Pharos styled track) on a canvas it fits to the picture, and nothing moved that canvas when the control bar showed: paused, or awake while playing, a bottom line such as KonoSuba S01E01's 9:18 "Hey, wait!" sat on the seek bar. V455's cue lift covers WebVTT only and the Rust renderer's inset covers its own canvas only. Fix: while the bar shows, the player sets `--pharos-ass-lift` on `.pharos-video-frame` to the overlap of the canvas's laid-out bottom with the bar's laid-out top (`styled_lift`: `player bottom - bar height`, so a bar sliding in is measured where it ends, and a letterboxed picture ending above the bar does not move), and 0 while it is hidden. pharos.css turns it into `transform: translateY(...)` with a 0.2 s transition (none under reduced motion), so the compositor moves the drawn frame and libass draws nothing again. It is recomputed when the bar shows or hides, when the renderer gets ready, and when a ResizeObserver sees the canvas refitted (after JASSUB, at most once a frame), never per pointer move. `will-change: transform` keeps the canvas on its own layer: without it Firefox did not show a frame drawn while paused after a seek. Checked in Chromium and Firefox with muted audio (108 px lift at 1280x720 paused or awake, 0 when idle). Guarded by pharos-ui `the_ass_canvas_clears_the_control_bar_only_while_it_shows` and `the_ass_canvas_moves_by_a_transform_in_the_stylesheet` (V583).
B635|2026-11-07|THE PLAYER FETCHED THE STYLED SUBTITLE SCRIPT TWICE AND ITS FIRST RENDERER NEVER DREW. The styled subtitle effect first ran before the `<video>` had metadata (`videoWidth` 0, `readyState` 0) and built a JASSUB renderer that never drew; about 30 ms after metadata arrived the effect ran again with the same pick, tore that renderer down and built a second, fetching `stream.ass` again. Building only after the first run without the second (a same-URL skip alone) drew nothing. Fix: wait for `HAVE_METADATA` (or the ready budget) before building, building nothing if the pick changed meanwhile (`video_metadata`), and rebuild only for a different script or none (`restart_styled`). Checked with a canvas and fetch probe in Chromium and Firefox: one JASSUB canvas and one `stream.ass` request per pick, and the 9:18 shout drawn paused, playing and at Effects Full. Guarded by pharos-ui `the_ass_renderer_is_rebuilt_only_for_a_new_script`.
B636|2026-11-07|A USER-LESS ACTIVITY ENTRY SERIALIZED `UserId` AS null. `state.rs` record_activity passed `None` through to the DTO, so `/System/ActivityLog/Entries` and the `ActivityLogEntry` socket push carried `"UserId": null`. Jellyfin stores the `Guid` default for such entries and serializes `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` (`ActivityManager.ConvertToOldModel`; the entity's `UserId` is `Guid`). jellyfin-sdk-kotlin declares `ActivityLogEntry.userId` as a required non-null UUID, so a null fails decoding of the whole list. Fix: `activity_user_id` maps `None` to the nil GUID. Test: `state::shutdown_tests::a_user_less_activity_entry_serializes_the_nil_guid_not_null`. Guard: none new (the test pins the value; no invariant covers required-field nullability yet).
B660|2026-11-07|A GROUP AUDIO SWAP LEFT EVERY MEMBER PAUSED WHILE THE GROUP PLAYED. In the pharos-ui watch-together player, a group audio pick that needs a new playback (direct play to an HLS transcode, `verdict=renegotiate`) remounts the `<video>`. The new one is mounted without `autoplay` (the group drives it); the `play()` that `catch_up` sent on attach was cut off ("The play() request was interrupted by a call to pause()") and nothing started it again: the drift check skipped a paused player, and the server still had every member playing, so it sent no command. Found by the together e2e audio case (both members sat paused at the swap position on `multitrack.mkv`). Fix: `Session::check_drift` resumes a driven player it finds paused while the group plays, with nothing loading (not `held_waiting`, `buffering_sent` or `owes_ready`), once the server's last command has settled (`SETTLE_AFTER_COMMAND_MS`): it seeks to the group's position and plays, logging `pharos: group resume reason=paused_while_group_plays position_ms=… expected_ms=…`, at most once per settle window so a refused `play()` is not retried every second. Guarded by V610.
B710|2026-11-07|A TV DREW THE DESKTOP LAYOUT. `device_class` already told a TV user agent (or the Android shell) from a desktop, but only the styled subtitle tier read it: on a TV the header sat at 16-24 px from the screen edge, inside Android TV's 48 x 27 px overscan margin, and the 2 px focus ring was hard to find from a sofa. Fix: `boot_theme` sets `data-device` on `<html>` from `this_device()`; under `[data-device="tv"]` the banner and `.pharos-main` keep to the title-safe area (rails still scroll out through a 48 px gutter) and the focus ring is 3 px with a glow. Found by a D-pad walk at the TV's 960 x 540 viewport (spec 025). Guarded by `tv.spec.ts` "a TV keeps the page inside the title-safe area" (V660).
B711|2026-11-07|THE ARROW KEYS WALKED OUT OF A MODAL DIALOG. `move_focus` scoped a move to the nearest `[data-keys]` area, which for the setup / what's-new dialog is the whole page behind it: ArrowDown from its Next button focused a poster under the backdrop, so a TV remote lost the dialog it had to answer. Fix: a focused `[aria-modal='true']` dialog is the scope, and with the focus on nothing an open modal is searched first. Guarded by `whats-new.spec.ts` "the arrow keys stay inside the dialog" (V661).
B712|2026-11-07|AFTER OPENING A PAGE OR PRESSING BACK, THE FIRST ARROW WENT TO THE HEADER. A route change leaves the focus on `<body>`; `move_focus` then took the first control in view of the whole page, the brand link, and Back from a show lost the tile it was opened from, so a remote walked down from the top every time. Fix: `move_focus` remembers, per page path, the control it last focused (`href`, else `id`); with the focus on nothing it returns there, else to the first control in view inside `main`. Guarded by `tv.spec.ts` "Back returns the remote to the tile it opened" and "the first press after a page opens lands on its content" (V662).
B661|2026-11-07|THE DIRECTOR STYLED WHOLE SCENES OF DUBBED FILMS AND CARTOONS AS PHONE CALLS. Rule 3 also fired with no text cue when a line's high-band share was under 30% of the track median at about median loudness. That fits a phone line, but it fits a dub's mix, a cartoon's voice processing and a dull microphone just as well: live, The Lion King had 59 Phone lines, My Little Pony S08E04 had 60, and none was a call. Fix: Phone fires only on a bracket naming a device (`[phone]`, `(radio)`, `[TV]`, `(intercom)`, `(over radio)`, `(on the phone)`, `(through the intercom)`). The high-band path is gone, and `DIRECTOR_VERSION` is 2 so the background job re-directs every stored plan. Guarded by V614 and pharos-styling `a_dull_voice_without_a_cue_is_not_a_phone` and `phone`.
B662|2026-11-07|PHONE AND RADIO LINES WERE A GREEN MONOSPACED BOX THAT STUTTERED IN. Wire set Courier Prime at 44 in mint green on an opaque box (BorderStyle 3), and on Desktop ran three alpha steps in its first 200 ms. On a real episode that read as a terminal pasted over the picture, and the alpha steps read as flicker rather than a signal coming in. Fix: Wire is the dialogue face and size (Liberation Sans 56) in silver-blue rgb(200,225,255), with a soft dark outline (2.5) and a small shadow (1.5), BorderStyle 1. Desktop fades in (200/160 ms) and settles once from a paler tint over 400 ms; TV keeps its fade; Low is the style alone. `COMPILER_VERSION` is 2 so served tracks recompile. Guarded by pharos-styling `wire_is_dialogue_shaped_in_silver_blue` and `desktop_fades_once_without_a_stutter`.
B663|2026-11-07|A DIRECTOR BUMP HID EVERY STYLED TRACK UNTIL THE JOB RE-STYLED IT. `styled_track::current` refused any row whose `director_version` differed from the build's, so bumping `DIRECTOR_VERSION` (B661) withdrew "English (Pharos styled)" from all ~765 styled items at once. The job re-styles one item at a time with a fresh signal pass, about 40 items an hour live, so most of them would have stayed hidden for most of a day. The pass also took items in library order, so the tracks viewers had lost waited behind the backfill of never-styled items. Fix: a plan from an older director is served until the job replaces it (only a newer director's plan, from a rollback, is refused); the compiled-track cache is keyed on the plan's own director version; and the pass re-styles items that had a styled plan before items that never did. Guarded by V613, it_rpc `styled_track` and `subtitle_styling::tests::a_served_stale_plan_is_redone_before_new_items`.
B730|2026-11-07|THE DATABASE POOL WEDGED FOR GOOD WITH EVERY CONNECTION IDLE. Local seeded sqlite pharos, 2026-11-07T23:40:01Z (and again 23:48:39Z): every acquire timed out after 30 s ("pool timed out while waiting for an open connection") and the probe logged "pool saturated" every 5 s while `pharos_db_pool_idle` read 8 and `pharos_db_pool_in_use` 0; it never recovered. Each time a native connection signed in and closed "Away" 15–19 ms later, mid-boot, with its calls queued on the pool. Cause: capnp-rpc 0.27 keeps an incoming call's future in the connection's answer table, and the future holds the connection state through its `Results`, a cycle that only `ConnectionState::disconnect` breaks. `rpc/socket.rs` drops the served `RpcSystem` from its `select!` as soon as the socket side ends, before the RPC side reads EOF, so disconnect never ran and every call in flight lived on unpolled. A call parked in sqlx's acquire stays in tokio's FIFO semaphore queue; a freed slot is handed to it and never comes back, so eight leaked waiters own all eight slots while the connections sit idle. Fix: `pharos_api::DisconnectOnDrop` wraps the `RpcSystem` that `rpc::serve` runs and polls its `Disconnector` once from `Drop`, which disconnects synchronously and drops the calls (each now counted `cancelled`). Signals: `pharos_rpc_calls_in_flight{interface,method}` stays above 0 after a leak; the pool probe now logs `size`/`idle` and, when it times out with idle connections, an ERROR naming stalled waiters instead of "saturated". Tests: `a_connection_closed_mid_call_gives_the_pool_back` (fails with "size=8 idle=8" without the fix), `dropping_a_guarded_rpc_system_drops_its_calls`, `a_bare_rpc_system_dropped_mid_call_leaks_the_call` (pins the upstream behaviour), `leaked_waiters_starve_the_pool_with_every_connection_idle`. Guarded by V680.
B731|2026-11-07|A DATABASE OUTAGE LOGGED ONLY AS "POOL SATURATED". Prod, 2026-11-07 19:18:46Z to 19:25:51Z: CNPG terminated `pharos-db-1` at 19:18:37Z and its replacement accepted connections at 19:25:43Z. For those seven minutes pharos logged 86 "pool acquire probe timed out; pool saturated" lines and every caller saw "pool timed out while waiting for an open connection". `in_use` read 4–7 because sqlx counts an acquire that is retrying its connect as checked out, and `size` fell from 8 to 4 as the dead connections went. Nothing named the database. Telling the outage apart from load took a separate investigation across Loki, Prometheus and the postgres pod's logs. Cause: sqlx retries a failing connect until the acquire deadline and returns only `PoolTimedOut`, and the probe classified by idle count alone. Fix: when the probe times out with no idle connection but room to open one (`size < max`), it connects once directly (bounded by 2 s) and logs "database unreachable" with the connect error. Every probe timeout is now counted in `pharos_db_pool_probe_timeouts_total{backend,verdict}`, where verdict is `saturated`, `stalled_waiters` or `database_unreachable`. Tests: `a_probe_timeout_is_classified_by_the_pool_at_the_timeout`, `probe_timeout_verdict_labels_are_distinct_and_stable`, and the verdict assertions in `a_saturated_pool_records_the_probe_timeout_instead_of_hanging` and `leaked_waiters_starve_the_pool_with_every_connection_idle`. Pharos recovered within 8 s of the database returning, so no recovery change was needed. Guarded by V681.
B664|2026-11-07|A MEMBER WHO JOINED A PAUSED GROUP SAT AT THEIR OWN RESUME POINT, AWAY FROM EVERYONE ELSE. In the pharos-ui watch-together player, a joiner's video loads at the member's own saved position (start_ms), applied by the engine after the group session's catch-up seek; the ready that followed reported the member there, and with the group paused nothing corrected it (the drift check runs only while playing). Found by exploratory testing: a joiner 30 s and 70 s from a paused group. In Firefox the order differs: the video loaded before the group's item arrived, the item found the item already showing and did nothing, so the member never said ready at all. Fix: `Session::on_media` holds an owed ready once and seeks the player to the group's expected position when it is more than `SEEK_ABOVE_MS` away, sending ready when that seek lands (`pharos: group ready held reason=away`); a group item for the item already showing seeks the player to the item's position (a no-op within `HOLD_BELOW_MS`). Inside the full suite Firefox still lost one race: the engine applied the saved position after the catch-up seek had landed and the ready had gone. The root fix: a player loading the item the group plays starts at the group's position (`Session::start_for`, `pharos: playback group_start`), so there is no late resume seek to race. Guarded by V615.
B665|2026-11-07|RELOADING THE PAGE DROPPED A MEMBER OUT OF THEIR WATCH-TOGETHER GROUP. The server keeps a member whose socket closed in the group for the 20 s socket-loss grace, and the tab's group device id lives in sessionStorage so a reload is the same member (V25), but nothing in the reloaded tab joined again: it played the film alone while the others saw a member who was not there until the grace ran out. Found by exploratory testing. Fix: the tab keeps its group's id in sessionStorage (`pharos.group.id`) while in one, cleared when it leaves or the group ends, and once connected after a load joins it again (`pharos: group rejoin reason=reload`), taking the kept seat back. In Firefox the reloaded player could load while the rejoin was in flight; its `canplay` found no group, the group's item found the player already in place and did not seek, and no ready ever went, so the group waited on that member. The session now remembers whether the player has data, in a group or not, and joining or a no-move item seek says ready from it. Guarded by V616.
